# Part I — Trackstand-Balance-v0 (2026-09-14 → present)

## 1. MuJoCo trackstand_rl_bike_0914 model
- The 0914 XML export (Fusion2Mujoco) welded model_root (no `<freejoint/>`); the free-fall viewer crashed on qpos[3:7] (nq=3). Fixed by attaching a free joint at load time via MjSpec, then by the user adding `<freejoint/>` into the XML.
- MuJoCo 3.12 rendering convention: the viewer's "Mesh tree"/"Convex hull" buttons = mjVIS_CONVEXHULL (hull render of COLLISION geoms only). The 0914 split puts visuals in geom group 2 and collisions in group 3; the viewer default geomgroup = [1 1 1 0 0 0] hides group 3 — that's why "Convex hull" showed nothing. Script mirrors the button into geomgroup[3] as a show/hide control.
- Friction: floor + collision default carry friction="0.75 0 0" (MuJoCo MAX combine → effective pair 0.75, matching the sim).

## 2. The SDF-on-both contact representation (Isaac Lab side)
- URDF import (URDF USD Converter v0.1.2): every link = two instanced Xforms — `<link>` (visual) and `<link>_1` (collision). The collision mesh child (inside an instanced prototype, INVISIBLE to plain stage traversal) carries PhysicsCollisionAPI + physics:approximation="convexHull" + purpose="guide" (these are the colliders that "collision from visuals" deletes).
- CRITICAL: `physics:approximation="sdf"` ALONE does nothing — the PhysX parser rejects the token and falls back to convexHull. The SDF branch is gated on applying PhysxSDFMeshCollisionAPI to the collision prim. Token + API → no fallback (verified 5+ headless runs).
- Evidence in omni.physx 110.1.11: fillSdfTriangleMeshDesc, Gu::SDFContactReduction, Gu::SDFUsingWindingNumbers — the full SDF contact pipeline is present; the schema ships PhysxSDFMeshCollisionAPI (sdfResolution 256, sdfSubgridResolution 6, sdfNarrowBandThickness 0.01, sdfMargin 0.01).
- apply_wheel_sdf.py patches the imported USD at the Sdf level (USD forbids authoring through instance proxies → the edit targets the payload-layer spec's prim stack).
- Geometry comparison: MuJoCo 0914 collision00.stl = 9,372 tris (7.4× decimated!) vs visual 69,732; the Isaac SDF is cooked from the full tire STL (69,728), grid ~0.55 mm + ~1.4 mm margin → the SDF side is FINER; the remaining gap is dominated by the MuJoCo decimation.
- Headless-probe infrastructure (reusable): kit --exec scripts swallow python stdout (write results to a file); asyncio.run deadlocks (use synchronous app.update() pumping); omni.usd.get_world_transform_matrix for poses; pxr.PhysxSchema imports only inside the full app.

## 3. Trackstand-Balance-v0 task
- 1:1 copy of NewBike-Balance-v1 on the SDF-wheel USD. Joint-name delta: rear_wheel_joint (underscore). Third joint front_wheel_joint unactuated/free (the 2 != 3 warning is expected).
- Location: source/trackstand_rl/, mirroring source/CycleRL EXACTLY (agents/, assets/, bicycle_cfg.py, mdp/ incl. dynamic/ + stationary/, trackstand_bike/). Zero CycleRL imports. LESSON: a partial mdp copy is impossible — mdp/events.py's module-level `__getattr__` is probed with "__path__" by the import machinery; without mdp/dynamic/ that probe raises ModuleNotFoundError instead of a catchable AttributeError. Copy the whole mdp package.
- Registered id: Trackstand-Balance-v0; aggregation import in cyclerl/__init__.py.
- Solver: 4/2 (DELIBERATE A/B knob vs NewBike's 16/8; enable_external_forces_every_iteration stays ON — keeps the drive rigid at low iteration counts).
- Friction: explicit PhysicsMaterialAPI + 0.5/0.5/0 on all four collision overs (avg-combine with pinned 1.0 ground → effective 0.75).
- Agent: standalone TrackstandRLPPORunnerCfg (all PPO values = NewBike's), experiment_name trackstand_rl_rsl.
- Final audit vs NewBike-Balance-v1: EVERYTHING identical except 5 deltas — USD (SDF wheels), drive joint name, solver 4/2, explicit friction authoring, names/experiment folder. Zero-agent smoke tests passed repeatedly.
- Steering initial-angle knob: init_state.joint_pos regex dict in trackstand/bicycle_cfg.py TRACKSTAND_BASE_CFG — put "steering_joint": <angle> AFTER ".*": 0.0; reset_scene_to_default restores it every episode.

## 6. TGS torque-delivery probe + sim-to-sim gap investigation (2026-09-17)
- EXPERIMENT (in-air rear-wheel spin-up; NO model files modified): constant 0.5 N·m drive, bike in air (z=5). Theory = analytic rigid ODE with the USD axle inertia (0.00032903 kg·m²) + actuator damping 0.05: ω(t)=10·(1−exp(−0.05t/I)); step1 = 9.52 rad/s, plateau 10.0.
- RESULTS (delivered/theoretical acceleration, first step / plateau): A trackstand cfg 4/2 + ext-forces-every-iter TRUE → 0.973/0.9998; B 16/8 + TRUE → 0.995/0.9999; C 4/2 + FALSE → 0.558/0.573; MuJoCo → 1.029/1.0000.
- CORRECTION: the trackstand policy did NOT train on a 24.5×-sluggish drive — at the training config the TGS solver delivers ~97–100% of theoretical rigid acceleration; under-delivery only with the flag OFF (the README's ×24.5 numbers were flag-OFF full-torque measurements on NewBike). The "sluggish drive" claim RETIRED for trackstand.
- CONSEQUENCE: the sim-to-sim gap (sim 1500/1500 vs MuJoCo honest-inertia ~69–189 steps) is NOT mass/inertia (equal to ≤0.5%) and NOT drive under-delivery → moves to contact/pitch dynamics and spawn transient.
- H3 SPAWN TRANSIENT: REFUTED — MuJoCo no-drop spawn still dies in 45–122 steps; the drop is not the cause.
- BEHAVIOR SIGNATURES (50 Hz traces): sim policy nearly still (a0 std 0.089, a1 std 0.019, roll ≤ 0.76° over 500 steps), holds steer ≈ +0.175 rad with ~−0.4° lean — quasi-static steer-offset balance. MuJoCo: first 2 steps nearly identical, then diverges within 3 steps; growing roll oscillation kills the episode at ~step 83 (roll ±29°, a0 std 0.53 — 6× the sim's control effort).
- H4 STEERING→ROLL COUPLING (open-loop constant-steer probe, both engines): THE GAP IS HERE. SIM: a0=+0.25 → roll +9.4° @20 then RETURNS to +1.8 @40; a0=−0.10 → −1.5 @20 → 0.0 @40 (bounded!). MUJOCO: a0=+0.25 → +12.2 @20 → +88.9 @40 (falls); a0=−0.10 → +16.8 @40 (falls). The sim's bike is quasi-statically STABLE under a constant steering offset (self-righting trail/contact moment — the policy's whole strategy = pin the fork and rest on it). The MuJoCo 0916 has raw STL wheel contacts and NO such restoring moment. Matches the NewBike README iteration-2 finding (a fitted "mirrored trail point" sphere contact ~3.2 mm off-axis was required to reproduce the sim's coupling there).
- NEXT was: a runtime fitted contact on the trackstand augmentation (pending user direction).

### 6b. Representation sweeps — all inert; the artifact is the contact RESOLUTION
- SDF-MARGIN ATTRIBUTION: RETIRED. Post-spawn in-memory sdfMargin edits don't change physics (SDF baked at spawn); cook-time A/B at margins 0.002/0.01/0.03 → IDENTICAL constant-steer responses to 2 decimals. Margin is RELATIVE (1% of bbox diagonal ≈ 1.4 mm), a distance-query aid — never a contact control. The "1 cm margin = rounded cradle" claim RETRACTED.
- HULL-vs-SDF A/B: convexHull wheels show the SAME bounded constant-steer responses (e.g. a0=+0.25: +5.99 @20 → +0.51 @40) — the leaned equilibria persist WITHOUT SDF. FINAL ATTRIBUTION: the artifact is the PhysX wheel-vs-plane CONTACT RESOLUTION itself (single sliding contact vertex that walks as the wheel rotates, ~9 mm effective pivot depth — the NewBike README's measured mechanism), representation-independent.
- SDF-KEEPING FIX ATTEMPTS: per-collider attrs exhausted — torsionalPatchRadius sweep 0/0.005/0.01/0.02 → IDENTICAL responses (NOT applied per user's criterion: apply only if positive). Mechanism: the vertex walk is ROLLING (no slip), so friction-patch attrs cannot anchor it — only MULTIPLE contact points change the support.
- MULTI-SDF WIDTH-SLICE: each wheel mesh split into two thin-axis halves, each its own SDF collider. Open loop: constant-steer response collapses to ≤0.6° FLAT for every steer (the single-vertex walk is KILLED; the bike becomes near-rigid — the opposite extreme; steering→roll coupling also suppressed). Closed loop (model_15650): SURVIVES 1500/1500, roll 0.088/1.424 mean/max, calmer steering, 4.6× more rear-wheel creep — the control signature shifts toward the real steer+drive pattern.
- ROBUST EVAL on the sliced contact (64 envs × 3000, model_15650): 128/128 × 1500/1500, time_out 100%, fallen 0%, roll 0.08/2.30/0.10 (TIGHTER than baseline 0.21/0.95/0.23). BUT motor activity explodes: drive_speed mean 7.21 rad/s (baseline 0.16, ~45×) hitting the 61.6 rad/s limit; steer 0.96 vs 0.25 — the policy balances ACTIVELY instead of resting on the donated margin (real-world pattern, but thrashy).
- FALL-TIME CALIBRATION: MuJoCo reference fall = 30° @ step 35, 89° by ~69 (zero input). Slice sweeps (2 halves; edge bands 0/2/4/6/8 mm; center bands 2/4/6/8/12 mm): ALL give IDENTICAL responses — zero-input fall settles upright, constant-steer flat — the sim's behavior is BIMODAL (single collider = artifact regime; any slice = rigid regime), insensitive to slice geometry. The MuJoCo fall-time criterion cannot discriminate slices within the classic PhysX solver.
- CRITICAL CORRECTION: the slice-layer GENERATOR was broken — it wrote numpy float reprs (np.float32(...)) into the points arrays → slice_wheels.usda FAILED to parse → every sliced variant ran on a WHEEL-LESS bike (riding the frame collision, base_z −0.052 vs correct −0.015). INVALIDATED: the whole slice series — "walk killed / bimodal rigid regime / robust 100% closed loop roll 0.10" — all measured on the wheel-less bike. RETRACTED.
- CORRECTED RESULTS (generator fixed; watertight width-scaled rings, correct ride height): (a) fall sweep over 6 ring geometries: NONE reproduces MuJoCo's fall (32.4° @35) — the SDF contact still cradles; best match = ring2_s045 (MSE 39, fall 13.5° @35, ~2.4× slower). (b) closed loop on the corrected contact: SURVIVES but MARGINAL — roll mean 1.447 / max 29.849° (one step from the 30° fallen term), a0 std 0.29, a1 std 0.60 (baseline 0.106/0.545, 0.012) — the corrected contact genuinely removes the donated margin.
- SLICING REVERTED (user verdict after GUI verification: "It was bullshit"): all slicing deleted; asset verified byte-identical to the pre-slicing backup; backup removed. The 0916 asset is exactly the single-SDF state of the 09161648 run. The slice series stands fully retracted (the investigation remains documented).

### 6c. The cylinder-wheel fix → retraining → sim-to-sim closure
- CYLINDER-WHEEL 0916 (user's URDF route): rear cylinder origin (0,−0.018,0) rpy(−1.572371,0,0.013264) [the 0914 recipe]; front origin (0,0,0) with rpy SOLVED FOR THE 0916'S OWN AXLE (1.570911, 0, 0.004721) — the user found the copied 0914 rpy misaligned (the 0914/0916 front axles differ ~8°). VERIFICATION BATTERY (imported USD): ride height −0.0156 m CORRECT; zero-input fall GROWS monotonically (19.33° @35 vs MuJoCo 32.41 — slower but NO bounded equilibrium, artifact GONE); constant-steer coupling present (22.4° @30). INTEGRATED: task asset swapped to the cylinder import + friction 0.5/0.5/0 patched; single-SDF asset backed up (assets_backup_singleSDF_2026-09-17/); integrated battery reproduces the imported results exactly (19.328° @35). The analytic cylinder kills the vertex-walk/cradle artifact while keeping the steering coupling.
- CLOSED LOOP on the cylinder asset (model_15650): SURVIVES but AT THE THRESHOLD — roll mean 2.125 / max 30.014°, steer std 0.615, a0 std 0.533, a1 std 0.276 (baseline: roll 0.145/0.542, a0 0.036, a1 0.010 — the policy thrashes 15–27× harder). VERDICT: the artifact-trained policy does NOT transfer robustly to the honest contact — retraining required.
- RETRAINING: 09171946 started. Config identical to 09161648 except the asset (cylinder wheels) + max_iterations 500000. Early curve: survival reward 0.03–0.06, fallen rate 0.78–0.88, ep_len 16 → 19.4 — the honest-contact learning signature (fast falls, slow climb).
- 09171946 CONVERGED (ended ~01:35 at iter ~93,057; last ckpt model_92600): mean_episode_length 1488.8/1500, survival 5.000 (max), fallen 0.000, time_out 94.5% — the policy LEARNED to balance on the cylinder contact with no donated margin. MILESTONE: the artifact-free contact is trainable.
- 09171946 COMPLETION (ended ~09:36 at iter 224,199; 4484 checkpoints): plateau from ~41k. Raw peak 209.9961 (ties first @ 41,068, LATEST @ 99,059 → model_99050 per the latest-tie rule); EMA-0.6 peak @ 41,071 → model_41050; rolling-500 @ 108,240. Final reward 209.8649 (flat plateau, ±0.03 units).
- play_eval (64 × 3000): model_41050 PERFECT — 128/128 × 1500/1500, timeout 100%, ALL deaths 0%, roll 0.86/1.13/0.86, drive 0.05/1.79, steer 0.08/3.31, drift max 0.005 m, yaw max 1.58°, turn 17.0°/ep. model_99050: 95.3% timeout, 4.7% yaw deaths; yaw max 29.90°, drift 0.242 m. VERDICT: 41050 better — the flat TB plateau hid a yaw-wander degradation; eval discriminates. DEPLOY CANDIDATE: model_41050.
- MILESTONE — THE SIM-TO-SIM GAP IS CLOSED: model_41050 exported to ONNX (fidelity 1.19e-07) and deployed in the MuJoCo honest-inertia env: 9000 continuous deploy_sim steps (180 s), ZERO terminations (vs the artifact-trained 15650's 69–189-step episodes). The cylinder-contact policy transfers across engines end-to-end. Viewer defaults point at the 41050 export. Chain complete: artifact diagnosis → cylinder fix → retraining → cross-engine transfer.
- CYLINDER-vs-STL FIDELITY (from the 0916 collision00 STLs): rear STL = tire band r=70.97 max, 9.5 mm wide + hub flange (r~35); front = ~28-mm rim+tire ring, r=70.71. The cylinders: radius 70 (1–1.4% under the real outer radius); front disk at ring center (exact); REAR disk at the −18 mm offset = the tire band's outer sidewall edge — an EFFECTIVE-contact pivot (the MuJoCo README's fitted sidewall point). Contact nature matches (point contact both engines). Dynamics: qualitative match, quantitative gap — Isaac cylinder fall 19.3° @35 vs MuJoCo STL 32.4° (the sidewall offset makes it ~40% slower). VERDICT: high radial geometric fidelity, effective-contact lateral fidelity (by fitted design); the residual is the known cross-engine contact residue.

## 7. MuJoCo deploy: direct state API, sensor-free refactor (2026-09-18)
- MuJoCo has the direct API — mjdata.h's qvel carries both sensor channels. Controlled unit-response tests (identity AND 90° yaw): gyro == qvel[3:6] EXACTLY (freejoint angvel is body-frame); velocimeter == Rᵀ·qvel[0:3] (qvel[0:3] is WORLD-frame base-origin velocity; the 3.12 velocimeter reads in the sensor frame). So the deploy env's old vel_b = R.T @ (velocimeter) was a DOUBLE rotation vs the sim's root_lin_vel_b — masked because balance velocities are ~0.01 m/s (measured |vel_b| mean 0.01, max ~0.55 in transients).
- REFACTOR APPLIED: env.py observe() reads qvel directly (ang = qvel[3:6], vel_b = Rᵀ·qvel[0:3] — bug fixed); the deploy-contract augmentation no longer adds the imu site or gyro/velocimeter sensors (the viewer's visible sphere is gone; stale augmented XML deleted, regenerates). The newbike scene keeps its own (now unused) sensors — its imu site frame == base_link frame, so qvel reads are identical there.
- VERIFICATION: augmented scene compiles with 0 sensors/no site/2 actuators; 41050 ONNX closed loop ran; newbike scene still loads. Controlled A/B (seed 42, 9000 steps each, identical noise): OLD double-rotated = 2 falls / roll RMS 1.13°; NEW fixed = 2 falls / 1.11° — BEHAVIOR-NEUTRAL. HONEST NOTE: both variants show 2 early-episode falls (~200–250 steps) at seed 42; the user's own pre-refactor viewer run fell at 208 steps with the same signature — a pre-existing seed/noise transient, NOT introduced by the refactor (the 9000-steps-zero-falls milestone was the lucky-seed case). A backup of the previous sensor-based environment exists: newbike_mujoco_deploy/backup_sensor_env_2026-09-18/ (env.py, run_deploy_viewer.py, regenerated sensor scene, README, run_backup_headless.py).
- VIEWER time_out RESTORED: deploy_sim's enabled_terms reverted to the env default {"time_out", "fallen"} — episodes terminate + reset at 1500 steps; metrics log records time_out.

## 9. Physics-step rate: 100 Hz vs 400 Hz (2026-09-18 → 21)
- TWO CLOCKS PER ENGINE: control frequency (50 Hz both — policy inference + actuator command update) and physics step rate (Isaac 100 Hz, MuJoCo 400 Hz). Solver iterations and rendering are internal/presentation, not clocks.
- Isaac 400 Hz IS POSSIBLE: two lines — decimation = 8 and self.sim.dt = 0.0025 in trackstand_rl_env_cfg.py. Chain verified from source: SimulationCfg.dt is an unvalidated float; physx_manager.py converts int(1/dt) → physxScene:timeStepsPerSecond = 400 exactly; only an UPPER-bound dt check exists (>1/30 warns). Runtime probe: physics_dt 0.0025, step_dt 0.02, 50 Hz control.
- MUJOCO dt-0.01 ATTEMPT — FAILED, REVERTED: at 0.01 s the stiff solref time constant (0.005 s) is HALF the step → MuJoCo's contact goes unstable (mean ncon 0.5 vs 2.0, wheels bouncing, roll −3 → −58° in 8 steps with the policy pinned at the torque limit). Sweep (300 steps, seed 42; baseline 0.0025 = 0 falls / roll RMS 0.65°): euler stiff 50 falls; implicit 22; rk4 32; implicit solref[0.02,0.8] 15; euler soft 50; implicit solref[0.05,0.8] 17. NO 0.01 configuration holds. VERDICT: the sim's 0.01 s is a PhysX TGS integration step — MuJoCo at 0.01 s is different physics; matching the NUMBER does not match the behavior. Reverted to 0.0025 (8 substeps; env.step kept model-aware: substeps = round(0.02/timestep)). 50 Hz policy + actuator-command rates are what actually match; the integration rate is solver-dependent.
- ISAAC 400 Hz DR BEHAVIOR EXPERIMENT (GPU, 4 envs, seed 42, full DR, ground spawn, DR-trained model_62850): [A] zero-input fall 100 steps: 100 Hz non-finite=0, base z min −0.0355; 400 Hz non-finite=0, −0.0354 — same toppling, no NaN. [B] closed loop 300 steps: 100 Hz 0 falls, roll RMS [1.75 1.91 1.54 0.41] (mean 1.40°); 400 Hz 0 falls, [1.95 1.25 1.65 0.47] (1.33°) — equivalent. VERDICT: normal at 400 Hz; a 100 Hz-TRAINED policy runs fine. (MuJoCo's failure was the COARSER direction; Isaac's 400 Hz is FINER — numerically safe.)
- 400 Hz CONTACT/TRACTION RE-CHECK (after the user reported "wheels turned, bike didn't go" in a GUI session): [A] zero-input fall identical both rates. [B] policy-only 400 steps: drift +0.013 m @400 Hz vs +0.019 m @100 Hz, slip 0.013 vs 0.029 m/s — both essentially stationary (BY DESIGN: trackstand = stationary balancing; wheels turn with small drive corrections while the bike holds position). [C] injected drive +0.3: forward response at BOTH rates (0.206 m @400 vs 0.308 m @100, slip 0.19 vs 0.18). [D] injected +0.1: 400 Hz dx +0.104 m vs 100 Hz +0.066 m, slip IDENTICAL 0.043/0.044 m/s. VERDICT: contact transmits drive NORMALLY at 400 Hz — no traction failure reproduced; the user's observation matches the policy's stationary-balance design (true at BOTH rates), not a contact error.
- RENDER FIX: self.sim.render_interval = self.decimation in __post_init__ (render_interval = SimulationCfg field, default 1 → physics steps per render). At decimation 8 it renders once per env step (50 fps) instead of 8×. GUI-only relief; headless training unaffected.

## 10. Domain-randomization training phase (2026-09-18 → 21)
- DR TERMS (per-episode, mode="reset"): randomize_mass scale U(0.8,1.2); randomize_com ±5 mm x/y/z; randomize_material tire static+dynamic friction U(0.8,1.2) (32 buckets, consistent, restitution 0); randomize_actuator_gains stiffness+damping U(0.9,1.1); initial_state roll U(±0.10)/roll-rate U(±0.25); reset_imu_turnon_residuals (gyro 2e-4 rad/s, accel 0.002 g — sampled per episode, applied per step as constants). Ground friction = startup, pinned nominal (DR-free by design — the shared ground can't be ramped mid-sim; the tire material carries friction DR). Per-step: obs #8 white noise (enable_corruption + PerChannelGaussianNoiseCfg, gravity 0.001 g / angvel 0.0012 rad/s). dr_ramp commented out — DR full from step 0.
- RUN MATRIX (all train_deterministic.py, --max_iterations 500000, identical agent + DR terms): 09182031 (air spawn 0.02, 100 Hz) → 09190125 (ground spawn −0.016, 100 Hz) → 09202356 (ground spawn, 400 Hz: dt 0.0025 / decimation 8 / render_interval 8). 09202356's saved cfg differs from 09190125 in EXACTLY those three lines. PURPOSE: train the DR policy AT 400 Hz — the training-side half of the 400 Hz question.

## 11. MuJoCo DR port — all 6 BalanceEventsCfg terms + `--dr` viewer flag (2026-09-21)
- IMPLEMENTED in newbike_mujoco_deploy: BikeBalanceEnv(enable_dr=True) applies every BalanceEventsCfg DR term per episode; run_deploy_viewer.py gained `--dr` (off by default). Terms mirrored EXACTLY from the Isaac Lab events.py source: randomize_mass (per-BODY independent scale U(0.8,1.2); inertia × the same per-body ratio — the sim's recompute_inertia default); randomize_com (ONE ±5 mm offset 3-vec shared by ALL bodies; inertia untouched — the sim's set_coms_index doesn't touch it); randomize_material (32 friction buckets pre-sampled once at init, make_consistent dynamic=min(s,d); per-episode random bucket per collider; tire sliding friction = (1+s)/2 so PhysX avg-combine with pinned 1.0 ground ≡ MuJoCo MAX-combine with the 0.75 floor → pair U(0.9,1.1) both engines); randomize_actuator_gains (per-actuator INDEPENDENT stiffness/damping ×U(0.9,1.1); actuator law measured empirically — steer F = kp·ctrl − kp·qpos − kv·qvel with kp in gainprm[0]+biasprm[1], kv in biasprm[2]; drive kp = 0, torque gain NOT stiffness → untouched); initial_state (reset_ranges U(±0.10)/U(±0.25)); reset_imu_turnon_residuals (per-episode gyro N(0,2e-4) rad/s + accel N(0,0.002) g, added per-step in observe() BEFORE the white noise). Each reset restores the nominal snapshot first (never compounds); refresh_dr_nominal() re-captures after post-init edits (--base_inertia, gain zeroing). env.dr exposes the episode's draws.
- VERIFICATION: DR-off byte-identical to before (all model arrays exactly nominal, reset_ranges zeros); same-seed DR draws identical; per-term isolation (no-DR-trained model_41050, 3 seeds × 3 × 500 steps): initial_state alone 2/3 falls (deaths 14–74 steps), com alone 2/3 (deaths 137–357 — slow degradation), mass/friction/gains/imu alone 0/3 each; all 6 together 3/3.
- SIM GROUND TRUTH (Isaac, current DR cfg + 41050): 60 falls/4000 env-steps, first falls steps 10–47, 1/8 envs surviving 500 — the sim kills the no-DR policy the SAME way → MuJoCo's DR behavior is faithful, not a bug. DR-trained 62850 in MuJoCo: 3/9 falls (vs 41050's 9/9) — the DR training does what it's for.
- FULL LIBRARY-LEVEL AUDIT (2026-09-22, MuJoCo 3.12.0): the registered sim env is TrackstandRLHybridEnvCfg (hybrid: steer position servo k10/d0.2 + drive effort k0/d0.05 — the MuJoCo deploy contract EXACTLY; the base class's velocity-drive cfg is NOT the registered env). Every DR edit traced into the MuJoCo library and proven LIVE: (1) mass/inertia/COM — mj_forward recomputes crb/M from body_mass/body_inertia/body_ipos each call; data.crb[1][9] == drawn mass sum EXACTLY (2.431165), mass matrix M differs from a nominal twin at the same pose, world CoM shift within the convex bound of |com offset|; (2) friction — mj_makeConstraint builds the pair from geom_friction; contact.friction == drawn tire values EXACTLY (1.041/0.9887), and a minimal box-on-floor test reproduces the analytic stopping distance s=v²/(2μg) for μ ∈ {0.75,1.0} (MAX-combine with the 0.75 floor; the DR range 0.9–1.1 always dominates the pair); (3) gains — measured actuator slopes on the drawn model == nominal × drawn scales to 3 decimals (steer ±9.117 = ±10×0.9117, −0.1913 = −0.2×0.9563; drive −0.0479 = −0.05×0.9587, torque gain 1.0 fixed); (4) dynamics — DR-on zero-input fall steps vary per episode [23,28,52,21] vs DR-off identical [35,35,35,35]; (5) per-episode redraw, no compounding, nonzero obs biases. One unexplained binding artifact (not an effectiveness issue): data.efc_frictionloss reads all zeros in 3.12 while contact.friction and the dynamics prove friction live.

All variant configs DELETED from the trackstand package: TRACKSTAND_STANDARD_CFG (velocity-drive damping 5.0), TRACKSTAND_TORQUE_CFG, TRACKSTAND_BASE_CFG, TrackstandRLEnvCfg's velocity-drive base form, TrackstandRLTorqueEnvCfg, TrackstandRLHybridEnvCfg and the Hybrid Actions/Rewards/Terminations subclasses (dead comment blocks and the stale literature-reward docstring removed; the ACTIVE set — survival 5.0 / drift_gauss 2.0 σ0.25 / rate_penalty 0.1 — kept verbatim).

Known intentional cross-engine delta: MuJoCo's joint is rearwheel_joint (no underscore) — the deploy-contract rename.

## 13. 100 Hz vs 400 Hz physics dt — effect on TRAINING (2026-09-22, deep investigation)
- EXPERIMENT PAIR: 09190125 (100 Hz, ground spawn) vs 09202356 (400 Hz, ground spawn) — saved env.yamls differ in EXACTLY dt (0.01→0.0025), render_interval (1→8), decimation (2→8), log_dir; agent.yaml identical, same seed 42. 09190125 finished 500,000 iters (79.85 h); 09202356 still training (~126,750 @ 11:37).
- LEARNING PER ITERATION — NO systematic difference: TB curves wander within the same envelope and cross repeatedly (100 Hz peak 205.4 @ 7.9k; 400 Hz peak 205.8 @ 95.4k; matched iterations: 25k 156 vs 169, 75k 161 vs 157, 120k 193 vs 190 — reward ±15 noise throughout). Matched-iteration deterministic evals (model_123850 both, in-domain, 64 envs × 3000): 100 Hz → timeout 50.3%, fallen 0.7%, yaw 46.9%, roll RMS 1.01°; 400 Hz → timeout 51.7%, fallen 4.0%, yaw 43.0%, roll RMS 1.40° — statistically indistinguishable. Reference (what more training buys): 100 Hz final model_499999 → timeout 75.4%, yaw 6.9%, wheel_net 17.7% — the 400 Hz run is on the same trajectory, just earlier.
- WALL-CLOCK COST: direct interleaved A/B (4096 envs, contention-cancelled): 100 Hz 29.2 ms/env-step vs 400 Hz 51.6 ms/env-step = **1.77× per env-step** (the extra 6 physics substeps ≈ 3.7 ms each; the 50 Hz RL/obs/rew kernels are rate-independent). Measured solo 400 Hz training pace 6,094–6,229 iters/h (clean window after the other runs ended); the 100 Hz run never had a solo window (09182031 shared the GPU until 09-22 02:52, both 100 Hz runs at ~6.9-7.1k/h each while sharing → solo 100 Hz ≈ 10k+/h). NET: 400 Hz training reaches a given iteration count ~1.6-1.8× later in wall-clock, with the same learning per iteration → ~1.6-1.8× slower overall training for NO measured quality gain.
- MECHANISM (library): dt → physxScene:timeStepsPerSecond = int(1/dt) = 400 (physx_manager.py:598-600); the TGS solver runs the full 4-position/2-velocity iteration set PER physics step → 4× the solver iterations per control period vs 100 Hz (8×4 vs 2×4); control/obs/noise/rewards stay at 50 Hz (decimation 2→8) — identical MDP, finer dynamics integration.
- VERDICT: changing physics dt 100→400 Hz does NOT change what the policy learns (same envelope, same matched-iteration quality); it only buys finer contact/integration at ~1.6-1.8× the wall-clock training cost. No reason to train at 400 Hz for this task.

## 14. Ground-wheel RELATIVE friction via min-combine (2026-09-22)
- PROBLEM FOUND: randomize_material was the ONLY DR term deviating from the no-DR fixed value — it is a SET operation (Isaac Lab's stock function has no scale mode; buckets sampled once, assigned per reset via root_view.get/set_material_properties) → tire 0.5 replaced by U(0.8,1.2), pair (0.9,1.1) vs nominal 0.75, nominal never drawn.
- SOLUTION (library trace): PhysX has NO pair-friction attribute — the pair is always derived from the two materials via the combine mode; PhysxRigidBodyMaterialCfg.friction_combine_mode ∈ {average,min,multiply,max}, and on mode conflict the HIGHER-priority mode wins (PxCombineMode order). With the ground pinned at 1.0: min (or multiply) on the tire → pair = min(μ_t, 1.0) = μ_t — the tire value IS the relative coefficient.
- IMPLEMENTED: (1) base.usda tire sites: friction 0.5 → 0.75 + physxMaterial:frictionCombineMode="min" on the steering xform + front cylinder; (2) env cfg __post_init__: sim.physics_material = 0.75/0.75 + combine "min" (covers the rear/base shapes inheriting the scene default); (3) randomize_material ranges (0.8,1.2) → (0.6,0.9) = 0.75 × U(0.8,1.2) — the pair DR centered on the nominal, ±20%; (4) MuJoCo mirror: floor 0.75 → 0.1 pass-through, DR_FRICTION (0.6,0.9), tire = s directly.
- VERIFIED: (a) box-on-plane probe (5 boxes × modes, stopping-distance μ): min(0.5)→0.48, min(0.9)→0.79, min(1.5)→0.86 (clamped by ground 1.0), average(0.5)→0.69≈0.75 (old-regime control), min(0.75)→0.69≈0.75 — the documented precedence rule CONFIRMED empirically and the no-DR pair preserved; (b) env probe: min attribute on the tire prims AND /physicsScene/defaultMaterial, DR draws ∈ (0.6,0.9) with per-episode redraw (readback is stale until the first physics step — probe pitfall), 20 steps ran; (c) MuJoCo: floor 0.1, tire = s ∈ (0.6,0.9), pair = tire (analytic slide check), nominal pair unchanged 0.75; (d) 41050 smoke: DR off 0/3 falls ×3 seeds (nominal unchanged), DR on 8/9 falls (the no-DR policy still dies under DR — expected, sim ground truth same regime).
- NOTE: the running 09202356 and the finished 09182031/09190125 used the OLD average-combine friction DR; the new semantics apply to future runs.
- RETARGETED TO THE REAL ENVIRONMENT (2026-09-22): no-DR relative friction 0.75 → **0.65** (the measured real-environment value, sim-to-real transfer) and DR range (0.6,0.9) → **(0.5, 0.8)**, both engines. Isaac: base.usda tire sites + sim.physics_material → 0.65 (min-combine kept), randomize_material → (0.5,0.8). MuJoCo: nominal tire → 0.65, DR_FRICTION → (0.5,0.8), floor 0.1 pass-through kept. VERIFIED: box probe min(0.5)→0.48, min(0.65)→0.61, min(0.8)→0.73, avg control 0.74≈0.825, min(1.5)→0.86 (clamp); env probe authored 0.65+min on tire prims AND defaultMaterial, DR draws ∈ (0.5,0.8) per-episode redraw (probe pitfalls found: float32 0.65 ≠ 1e-9 tolerance; wp.to_torch aliases the live buffer — clone before comparing); MuJoCo mirror ALL PASS (a real bug found en route: the 0916 export carries EXPLICIT friction="0.75" on the bike collision geoms, so the collision-default edit never applied — fixed by setting the geoms directly; the old 0.75 nominal checks had passed by coincidence). 41050 smoke: DR off 0/3 falls ×3 seeds (nominal 0.65 pair still balances), DR on 8/9 (no-DR policy dies under DR — expected).

## 15. Full cross-engine suspicious-value audit (2026-09-22)
- FINDING A (real, the same class as the explicit-friction bug): the 0916 export carries explicit solref="0.02 1" on ALL bike collision geoms, silently overriding the augmentation's intended stiff contact [0.005, 1.5] (which only reached the floor and the default class; solimp DID propagate). The validated newbike reference uses 0.005/1.5 as its standard contact (model.xml:54); the trackstand bike has been running — and was fully validated (9000-step balance, fall calibration, sim-to-sim closure) — on the 4× softer 0.02/1. NOT silently changed: fixing it (one loop in the augmentation) would shift the validated contact behavior and needs re-validation.
- FINDING B (red herring, reported honestly): geom_solmix = 1.0 on every MuJoCo geom — empirically NOT restitution-in-effect (drop-bounce test at solmix 0/0.5/1 and default all show zero rebound), so no behavioral impact.
- CONFIRMED CLEAN: mass parity EXACT (Isaac live 2.42433 kg == MuJoCo 2.4243 kg; per-body 2.02679/0.12333/0.10929/0.16492 identical to 5 decimals — an apparent 2.84 kg mismatch was just the mass-DR draw, ratios within U(0.8,1.2)); ground material pinned 1.0/restitution 0/combine average; scene defaultMaterial 0.65 + min; bike materials 0.65 + min; obs corruption ON (13-entry std); terminations/rewards/events key sets correct; MuJoCo floor 0.1 pass-through, tire 0.65, gravity −9.81 both, steering joint limited ±1.5708 both, drive continuous both, wheel radius 0.07 both; MuJoCo solimp propagated correctly; solver iterations 100 = MuJoCo default.
- LESSON: the Fusion2Mujoco export carries explicit per-geom attrs (friction, solref) that silently override default-class edits in the augmentation — any default-class change must also loop over the explicit geoms.
- SOLREF HARDNESS MEASURED (2026-09-22): [0.005, 1.5] is the harder wheel — empirical, NOT the naive 1/timeconst²=16×: sphere drop 0.2 m → peak penetration 13.52 mm (soft 0.02/1) vs 8.72 mm (stiff) = 1.55×; steady penetration 0.129 vs 0.080 mm = 1.62×; box: 13.37 vs 8.62 (1.55×) and 0.033 vs 0.020 mm (1.64×). Steady penetration is LOAD-INDEPENDENT (0.5/1/2 kg identical — the reference-acceleration formulation). Real bike under 41050: ride-height difference only 0.066 mm, both solrefs balance identically (0 falls, roll RMS 0.62 vs 0.61°) — the practical balance behavior is essentially indifferent; the solref choice matters ~1.6× for impact/deflection feel, negligible for the stand.
- SOLREF FIXED (2026-09-22): the augmentation now sets solref [0.005, 1.5] explicitly on all non-floor geoms (same loop as the friction fix). VERIFIED: all 4 bike collision geoms [0.005, 1.5], floor unchanged, friction 0.65; 41050 re-validation on the stiff contact — 3 seeds × 3 × 500 steps 0 falls, and 3 × 1500-step full episodes all timeouts (roll RMS 0.61–0.70°) — the validated balance behavior survives the fix.
