## 19. Raspberry Pi trackstand deployment and hardware alignment (2026-09-23 → 25)
- IMPLEMENTED `deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/` as a ROS 2 package for **stationary** balancing with the `09171946` run's `model_41050` ONNX export. Its five nodes handle IMU, CAN motor I/O, RC kickstand input, servo output, and 50 Hz policy inference. The RC steering/throttle sticks do not command the bike; kickstands start down, and a confirmed up request enables balancing only when sensor/motor data are ready. Down disables balancing; faults latch until a down/up cycle.
- OBSERVATIONS/ACTIONS: the policy receives the task's 13 channels from IMU orientation/rates and steering/rear-wheel encoders; longitudinal speed is estimated as `0.07 m × rear-wheel rad/s`, and lateral speed is set to zero because no other sensor is available. A 200 Hz CAN loop applies the simulated steering position PD and rear torque/damping laws. The policy ONNX output was checked against its checkpoint over 128 seeded observations (maximum difference `9.537e-07`).
- HARDWARE CONFIG matched to the active `ros2_ws` files: steering motor ID 2 (host `0x102`, feedback `0x002`), rear motor ID 1 (`0x101`/`0x001`); steering encoder/torque signs `+1/+1`, rear signs `-1/-1`. The CH340 IMU uses `/dev/serial/by-id/usb-1a86_USB_Serial-if00-port0`, `imu_link`, 100 Hz, identity mounting transform, zero gyro bias, and 0.25 s source stale limit. The previous deployment settings came from `new_balance` and were superseded for this bike.
- RC/SERVOS: the receiver is RadioMaster/ELRS CRSF on the CP2102 by-id UART at 420000 baud, with the `ros2_ws` port exclusions, baud candidates, soft CRC, and channel-range detection. Paired kickstands use CH7: high=up, low=down, middle=hold, eight matching frames to debounce. The `ros2_ws` signed servo angles convert to raw STS3250 targets: up `[1648, 2454]`, down `[1972, 2085]` for IDs `[1, 2]`. The obsolete SBUS reader was replaced by a CRSF parser.
- VERIFIED: deployment pure tests `11 passed`; ROS 2 Humble package built with zero stderr. These checks establish code/config consistency, not physical sign or timing correctness. Before an unsupported balance test, verify IMU lean direction, motor directions and torque constants, CH7 states, and both servo poses with the stands down and motor `dry_run: true` (see `deploy/docs/HARDWARE_CHECKLIST.md`).

## 20. Deployment library encapsulation and deep YbIMU audit (2026-09-24 → 25)
- LIBRARY STRUCTURE: deployment now contains the Tsinghua hardware libraries as real Python subpackages instead of flat copies. `trackstand_deploy/YbImuLib/` contains byte-identical `YbImuSerialLib.py`, `YbImuI2cLib.py`, and `__init__.py`; the serial source matches the nominal Tsinghua file at SHA-256 `6c9c3208c8280900abd657889c3050d986dd1789c0cd543ebb402225a3f8dd96`. `trackstand_deploy/servo_control/sms_sts.py` is likewise the exact Tsinghua servo protocol implementation. Deployment-specific behavior stays in adapters (`imu_serial.py`, ROS publishers/drivers), `setup.py` uses `find_packages()`, and `smbus2` is declared. The obsolete flat `vendor_ybimu.py` and `sms_sts.py` duplicates were removed. Pure deployment tests: `11 passed`.
- FRESHNESS FIX: the IMU adapter records a monotonic timestamp after a checksummed frame parses, and `imu_publisher` publishes at most once for each advancing timestamp. A completely stalled USB stream or dead RX thread therefore no longer republishes one cached quaternion/gyro forever with fresh ROS stamps; `/trackstand/imu` stops and the 50 Hz policy's 0.06 s IMU timeout fails closed on the next tick (about 60–80 ms after the last callback). Quaternion order is correct (`vendor [w,x,y,z]` → internal/ROS `[x,y,z,w]`), raw gyro scaling is correctly rad/s, configured bias is converted deg/s→rad/s once, zero/non-finite quaternions are rejected, and the `R_BS` mount/bias transform matches the earlier Tsinghua calculation.
- CRITICAL LIMIT OF THE FIX: freshness is packet-global, not sample-specific. The wire protocol reports raw accel/gyro/mag (`0x04`), quaternion (`0x16`), Euler (`0x26`), barometer, version, and status in separate frames. The adapter advances the same timestamp after ANY parsed/unknown checksummed function. The publisher then reads quaternion and gyro from separate mutable caches. Thus new quaternion+old gyro, new gyro+old quaternion, or even an unrelated fresh frame with both policy fields stale can pass as a fresh IMU observation. The receive thread also writes components sequentially and the getters have no lock, so a timer callback can observe a torn quaternion or a cache mixture spanning packet cycles. The audit document's prior claim "new hardware sample" is therefore true only at the packet level, not for a coherent policy IMU sample.
- FUSION/MODEL GAP REMAINS: deployment sets report rate but never calls `set_algo_type(6|9)`, so it inherits unverified persistent firmware state. The proprietary onboard AHRS remains opaque; its filter latency, convergence, acceleration-induced tilt error, possible nine-axis magnetic interference, and quaternion discontinuities are neither measured nor reproduced in training. The exact `model_41050` saved env models only small independent white noise (`0.001` gravity, `0.0012 rad/s` gyro), not serial/AHRS latency or split-frame skew. ROS stamps are publication time rather than acquisition/parse time.
- ROBUSTNESS GAPS: the vendor parser checks the checksum but enforces only `length <= 40`, not a minimum or exact length per function; a checksummed short known frame can raise in `struct.unpack` and kill the unguarded daemon RX thread. Parsing uses native-endian `h/f` despite a little-endian protocol. USB/serial exceptions have no reconnect, restart, or explicit diagnostic; fail-closed behavior is safe but recovery requires restarting the node. There are no quaternion jump/norm-plausibility, gyro saturation/range, quaternion-vs-gyro, or quaternion-vs-acceleration checks. Corrected bike-frame data is published with `frame_id=imu_link`, which is harmless to the current policy (it ignores frame_id) but misleading to other ROS consumers.
- CALIBRATION IS A HARDWARE GATE: deployment and the active `ros2_ws` use identity mounting and zero gyro bias, while older Tsinghua balance configs record `[180,180,0]` and `[0.0113,0,0] dps`. Disk evidence proves conflicting historical configurations, not which one matches the present physical mounting. No ride/calibration logs were found, so actual bias, packet skew, AHRS latency/noise, fusion mode, and dynamic tilt error remain unquantified. Complete the sign/bias/latency checks on the restrained bicycle before unsupported balance.
- TEST GAP / NEXT FIX: existing tests cover only identity mount correction; none exercises parser lengths/checksums, unit endpoints, per-type freshness, selective raw/quaternion freezing, thread races, serial failure/reconnect, non-identity mounting, saturation, or quaternion jumps. The next implementation should keep locked per-type counters/timestamps and an immutable snapshot, publish only after BOTH raw gyro and quaternion advance within a bounded skew, select the firmware fusion mode explicitly, validate exact payload lengths, and add diagnostics/reconnect plus focused parser/freshness tests.
- OTHER ENCAPSULATION AUDIT: the remaining strong library candidate is Tsinghua's `GIM_Motor_Driver/motor_driver_python` package. Do not switch motor I/O to it mechanically: its receive path matches only CAN arbitration ID, while deployment also matches the echoed command byte and drains stale responses—necessary with two interleaved motors. If vendored, preserve `motor_io.py` as the ROS/safety adapter and retain command-aware response matching, watchdogs, signs, gearing, torque-constant validation, and limits. SBUS/CRSF parsing is internal modularization rather than another reusable Tsinghua library; NumPy, ONNX Runtime, python-can, pyserial, smbus2, and ROS remain external dependencies.

---

## 21. Isaac Lab domain randomization made opt-in (2026-09-27)

- DEFAULT CHANGE: `Trackstand-Balance-v0` now starts with `domain_randomization=False`: nominal authored mass/COM/friction/motor gains, upright initial roll, zero initial roll rate, and no per-episode IMU residual biases. The shared ground remains fixed at friction 1.0. Observation white noise is STILL ON (gravity sigma 0.001 in normalized-g units; angular velocity sigma 0.0012 rad/s); no-DR does not mean noise-free observations.
- OPT-IN: `--dr` restores the previous full-strength reset events, independently sampled per environment/episode, without a ramp: body mass/inertia scales U(0.8,1.2), COM offsets +/-0.005 m, per-shape friction assignments from 32 pre-sampled U(0.5,0.8) material buckets, actuator stiffness/damping scales U(0.9,1.1), initial roll U(+/-0.10 rad), roll rate U(+/-0.25 rad/s), and constant episode IMU residuals (gyro sigma 0.0002 rad/s; gravity-channel sigma 0.002). These settings remain fixed within an episode; white noise is resampled per observation.
- CLI/IMPLEMENTATION: removed the old `--no_dr` arguments and ramp-pinning logic (previously ineffective for this task because it had no active `dr_ramp`). RSL-RL `train.py`, `train_deterministic.py`, `play.py`, and `play_eval.py` share the opt-in flag; four SKRL train/play/evaluation counterparts were updated too, but their pre-existing legacy CycleRL runtime dependencies were not ported or exercised. `TrackstandRLEnvCfg.set_domain_randomization()` selects events before environment construction; `scripts/domain_randomization.py` applies/logs the switch. The effective boolean is included when the environment configuration is serialized. RSL-RL play's registration import now uses the current Trackstand package.
- UNCHANGED: 400 Hz physics, 50 Hz policy control, 13 observations, action scales/motor limits, rewards, all six termination conditions, and observation white-noise magnitudes. No hardware deployment configuration, existing training logs, or original checkpoints were changed.
- VERIFIED: all 10 checks in `isaaclab_train/tests/test_domain_randomization.py` passed in the installed Isaac Lab Python, covering opt-in/default behavior, obsolete-flag rejection, constructor opt-in, reversible/idempotent pre-construction switching, independent configuration instances, serialization, and preservation of unrelated settings. Command from `isaaclab_train/`: `/home/lmbike/IsaacLab/_isaac_sim/python.sh -B -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_domain_randomization.py -v`. Python syntax checks and `git diff --check` passed; Ruff was unavailable.
- LIVE SMOKE: the real `play_eval.py` loop ran in both modes using a temporary copy of `09221945/model_91850.pt`, 16 environments x 96 steps, seed 42, CUDA 0, headless. Reset assertions confirmed unchanged authored mass/COM/material/gains and no IMU bias buffers without `--dr`; with `--dr`, mass/COM/material/gains, initial poses, and IMU biases resampled, with different mass draws across environments. Effective configurations matched saved 09221945 settings apart from the intended DR switch and evaluation/path metadata. These are short smoke checks, NOT performance benchmarks. Audit/log artifacts: `/tmp/trackstand_dr_optin.Pc693b/` (temporary).
- REPRODUCTION: omit `--dr` for nominal training/evaluation; append it to reproduce earlier full-DR evaluations. Example from `isaaclab_train/`: `python scripts/rsl_rl/play_eval.py --task Trackstand-Balance-v0 --checkpoint /path/to/model.pt --num_envs 64 --num_steps 3000 --seed 42 --dr`. Usage is documented in `isaaclab_train/README.md`; historical full-DR results must not be compared to the new default without accounting for the regime change.

## 22. Earlier user-run ledger and first bike trial (from `isaaclab_train/logs/rsl_rl/cfg_record.txt`, lines 398–421)

- Historical training snapshots, **not substitute evaluations**: 09182031 used full DR, 100 Hz physics, and a 2 cm air spawn; the manual record reports mean episode length 721.1 at `model_492400`. 09190125 changed to a ground spawn at 100 Hz; its manual result was left as `xxxx` at `model_7850`, so no numerical result is asserted here. 09202356 used full DR, ground spawn and 400 Hz physics; it was aborted over the subsequently investigated friction inconsistency. The source's 2026-09-21 note says sim-to-sim transfer and DR/noise parity still needed verification at that time; later sections above document subsequent work.
- 09221945 was started after the ground–wheel friction retargeting to nominal 0.65 and DR 0.5–0.8, at 400 Hz with a ground spawn. The manual record first describes the real-world friction target approximately as 0.6–0.8; the actual adopted training configuration was nominal 0.65 with DR 0.5–0.8, as detailed above. Its **interim training** metric is mean episode length 1369 at `model_91850`; this is not a completed-run performance claim. Its historical 2026-09-22 note also proposed following up on IMU concerns and tracing the roughly 6% failed MuJoCo environments.
- The 2026-09-26 manual checklist for the first hardware trial was: verify IMU quaternion/rate and bias; motor B0 torque constant plus A2/A3 position/speed units; two distinct servo up/down targets; RC switch publication; end-to-end RC-to-servo response; then the final control flow. The note said the IMU and motor checks were done at that point, while servo/RC/control-flow checks remained. On 2026-09-27, the user noted motor command/feedback sign differences and called for close attention to them. These are dated progress notes, not current verification of every hardware behavior.
- **First on-bike deployment report, 2026-09-28:** after the six checklist points were reported checked, deployment with the no-DR 09171946 `model_41050` failed: the motors trembled vigorously and the bike fell within 2–3 seconds. The manual record proposes trying DR-trained 09221945 `model_91850` next and recording policy outputs plus motor position, velocity, and torque in a rosbag for comparison with Isaac/MuJoCo torque traces. Two **unverified hypotheses**, not diagnoses, were recorded: (1) `model_41050` was trained only at ground–wheel relative friction 0.75 and may lack real-world adaptability; (2) the deployment low-level control rate might have been too high for the motor to track steering targets, possibly with latency. The source explicitly expressed uncertainty between 100 and 200 Hz; the deployment configuration documented above specifies 200 Hz, but target tracking has not been established by this note.

## 23. Real-bike Stage A/B/C rosbag diagnosis (recorded 2026-10-01; analyzed 2026-10-02)

- SCOPE / ARTIFACTS: analyzed the three diagnostic recordings under `rosbags/`: A `rosbag2_2026_10_01-16_11_49` (10011611, 126.68 s, 150,000 decoded messages), B `rosbag2_2026_10_01-17_18_11` (10011718, 436.35 s, 549,816 messages), C `rosbag2_2026_10_01-17_32_00` (10011732, 122.01 s, 143,283 messages). Full methods, source evidence, reproduction commands, and five plots: [diagnostic report](deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02/README.md); exact statistics/events and bag/model SHA-256 hashes: [summary.json](deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02/summary.json). `decode_bags.py` and `analyze_bags.py` reproduce the analysis. All MCAP messages were decoded using their stored telemetry schemas and standard ROS message serializers. Original bag hashes were verified unchanged; no deployment runtime/configuration, policy, training, or model files were modified by this diagnosis.
- USER CONFIRMATIONS / LIMITS: the tests used **09171946/model_41050, not 09221945/model_91850**. During C the rear wheel was lifted clear of the ground, steering was free, and neither wheel/motor was touched or restrained; the rear wheel did not visibly vibrate, while the front wheel visibly turned. These are supported sensor/dry-run/fixed-command tests, **not a recorded unsupported policy-balancing/fall episode**. They cannot reconstruct every cause of the earlier 2–3 s failure or certify the policy after a hardware-control fix.
- STAGE A — SENSOR COHERENCE: motors remained gated off, with no recorded C0 control sends or motor faults. Recorded `dry_run=false`, contrary to the planned true setting; lack of enable commands kept this captured interval inactive, not the dry-run guard. Excluding the damaged interval, quaternions have unit norm and roll spans −35.96° to +31.48°. Quaternion-derived body-x rate versus gyro-x: correlation 0.978, gain 0.965 (40 ms rotation increments). A2 speed versus a 20 ms central difference of unwrapped A3 position: steering/rear correlations 0.991/0.992, gains 0.971/0.980. No gross roll-rate sign or degree/radian error is apparent, but physical mounting, absolute encoder-to-joint ratio, and torque direction are not independently certified. Pitch/yaw agreement is weaker (correlations about 0.70, gains 0.50–0.59); fusion/filtering and split-packet skew are hypotheses, not diagnoses. Complete marked body-axis checks remain necessary.
- STAGE A — RECORDING INTEGRITY: metadata claims 150,953 messages, but indexed and independent streaming MCAP reads find 150,000 (**953-record discrepancy**). At 106.31774 s, one IMU payload contains unrelated readable ROS-log text inside numeric fields, yielding impossible quaternion/gyro values; approximately 0.8 s of multiple-topic records is missing, including 159 motor-control sequence values. Excluded from sensor comparisons. The normal publisher normalizes/rejects invalid quaternions, so this is not evidence of a physical gyro excursion. Corruption origin is unknown; compare the original Pi file's checksum and re-copy/re-record if necessary. [Sensor plot](deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02/stage_A_sensors.png).
- STAGE B — POLICY REPLAY / COMMANDS: recorded status names 41050; independently replaying all **1,718** observation vectors through its bundled ONNX gives maximum absolute action error **4.6566e-08** (RMS 4.5361e-09). Reconstructed scaling matches enabled motor commands exactly. Nine RC-enabled intervals contain about 34.36 s of inference steps; the policy stream ends around 95 s, although recording continues to 436 s. Raw steering ranges −0.03563 to −0.03438, scaled steering −3.205° to −3.093°, requested rear torque −0.00522 to +0.00357 N·m. No target clipping or torque saturation. `dry_run=true`; **no C0 sends and no torque-feedback samples**. Calculated steering torque −0.575 to −0.533 N·m is a dry-run computation, not measured motor torque.
- STAGE B — TIMING / OBSERVATION LIMITS: active policy interval median/p99/max **19.98/22.41/24.01 ms**; ONNX inference **0.289/0.805/1.593 ms**. Active IMU callback ages **2.79/12.33/14.58 ms**; motor-state callback ages **4.07/15.48/33.94 ms**. All policy gyro channels are zero; gravity implies roll near −1.36°. This tests essentially a stationary supported state, not dynamic recovery; the lean alone does not distinguish actual stand-supported tilt from calibration bias. No policy fault latch; enabled periods end on RC-down. A brief motor-feedback-stale event occurs while already disabled. Export corruption, wrong selected checkpoint, and slow inference are therefore unlikely explanations of this bench instability, without proving unsupported policy robustness. [Policy/timing plot](deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02/stage_B_policy.png).
- CLOCK INTERPRETATION: control/inference timing uses node monotonic diagnostics, IMU intervals use header publication timestamps, and neither is hardware acquisition time. B's apparent 338.5 ms bag-record motor-topic gap advances only one control sequence and about 4.25 ms at the source: recording/transport delay, **not a 338.5 ms control stall**. Do not infer policy frequency by dividing 1,718 actions by the whole 436 s bag duration.

### Stage C — steering instability without policy

Times below are elapsed bag times; run-end estimates are approximate to one control interval. Angles/speeds are signed encoder feedback. Both motors are enabled by these fixed commands; the rear tests are not isolated rear-only experiments.

| Enabled interval (s) | Steering target (rad) | Rear target (N·m) | Steering position range (degrees) | Peak absolute steering speed (rad/s) | Finding |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 51.286–51.496 | 0 | 0 | +0.088 | 0.052 | Essentially stationary; only 0.21 s enabled |
| 60.677–62.736 | +0.020 | 0 | +0.088 to +1.187 | 1.142 | Settles near +0.901° versus +1.146° target; ≈0.245° steady error |
| 71.637–71.902 | −0.020 | 0 | −13.689 to +17.556 | 21.387 | Oscillation starts before watchdog disable |
| 72.173–74.001 | −0.020 | 0 | −18.918 to +19.556 | 22.092 | Continuous 1.83 s oscillation, dominant frequency ≈14.25 Hz |
| 85.907–86.972 | 0 | +0.050 | −21.951 to +22.017 | 22.309 | Steering oscillation recurs during positive rear command |
| 102.341–103.408 | 0 | −0.050 | −22.983 to +15.820 | 21.956 | Steering oscillation recurs during negative rear command |

- TORQUE PIPELINE: C has `dry_run=false`, no policy topics, **1,289 active control ticks**, and 1,289 successful socket writes for each motor. Recorded current-feedback samples number 1,295 per motor, consistent with the active C0 sends plus six zero-current disable sends. B0 constants stay about 0.78 N·m/A steering and 0.30 N·m/A rear. Feedback is **measured Q-axis current × B0 Kt with configured sign**, not requested current or a calibrated mechanical shaft-torque measurement; C0 reply layout is the protocol's A1 measured-current layout.
- CONTROL LAW / DISCONTINUITY: independently reconstructing all 1,289 active steering/rear torque commands gives **zero discrepancy** against `clip(10 × steering_error − 0.2 × steering_speed, ±5.6 N·m)` and `clip(bounded_rear_request − 0.05 × rear_speed, ±6.28 N·m)`. Fixed steering targets do not oscillate; feedback-driven PD commands do. P/D terms reach 4.01/4.46 N·m absolute, commanded steering torque saturates on 17/1,289 ticks (1.32%), and current-derived feedback reaches 5.237 N·m absolute. Consecutive active torque-command changes reach **8.72 N·m** (p95 3.52 N·m). This reproduces the discontinuous-torque symptom independently of raw policy variation. [Steering overview](deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02/stage_C_steering_overview.png) and [close-up](deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02/stage_C_steering_zoom.png).
- FEEDBACK / HOST TIMING: active control interval median/p99/max 5.01/7.79/12.00 ms; callback work 1.73/5.30/8.66 ms; steering A3 receipt age **5.64/18.44/26.23 ms**, A2 receipt age **3.76/15.78/24.83 ms**. Whole-C query-failure counter increases: steering A3/A2 **355/143**, rear A3/A2 **43/544**. The paired-update implementation retains both old measurements when either query fails. Active steering A3-derived versus A2 speed correlation 0.925, gain 1.006: no gross speed-unit mismatch apparent, but age/jitter remains. Per-motor ordered C0/current-reply matching gives median tick-start-to-reply receipt 3.62 ms steering / 4.64 ms rear; steering correlation improves from 0.879 against same-order command to 0.938 against preceding command. Delayed/filtered current response is supported, but these receipt timestamps and sequence-less replies do not identify an exact firmware time constant.
- WATCHDOG / SENSOR GAPS: six disable events use `command_timeout`, mostly expected at finite-publisher ends. Negative steering also has a recorded **352 ms internal command gap**, producing an unintended disable/re-enable. The first reversal exceeds 5° at **71.760 s**, before disable at **71.899 s**; oscillation continues during uninterrupted later control, so the gap is not its sole cause. C IMU publication gaps of about **336/218 ms** occur before the corresponding motor-enable intervals. Their cause is unknown; such controller-observed outages during balancing would violate the 60 ms IMU freshness threshold. B's active callback ages show no equivalent outage.
- FAULT COVERAGE: no recorded `motor_fault` or motor latch becomes true. The oscillation remains inside local ±90° steering-position and 26.29 rad/s steering-speed fault bounds; current checks do **not** detect persistent tracking error/oscillation. Socket-write success and false software fault flags do not prove healthy motor firmware or physical disable. Firmware diagnostic status, supply voltage, and calibrated mechanical torque were not recorded.
- REAR SIGN / SMALL-TORQUE WARNING: with requested +0.050 N·m, mean current-derived torque is **+0.05067 N·m** but encoder displacement is **−0.009587 rad (−0.549°)**; with −0.050 N·m, feedback mean is **−0.04737 N·m**, displacement **+0.008053 rad (+0.461°)**. Native encoded current averages −0.169/+0.167 A respectively, consistent with `drive_torque_sign=-1`. Current delivery is demonstrated; tiny encoder displacement has the opposite sign to expected policy-coordinate torque in both trials. **Do not blindly flip signs:** steering is simultaneously oscillating, raw A2/A3 CAN bytes/physical forward–back labels/effective Pi parameter dumps are missing, and direction configuration versus cogging/stiction/deadband/current ramp or limits cannot be uniquely identified. Repeat an isolated, supervised, physically labelled rear test before balancing; breakaway torque is not established. [Rear plot](deploy/reports/rosbag_ABC_2026-10-02/stage_C_drive.png).

### Diagnosis, evidence ranking, and next work

- PRIMARY CONCLUSION: **a deployed low-level steering-control/actuator problem is established without the policy**. Fix/calibrate that layer first, then assess policy transfer. The earlier §22 latency/control-rate suspicion now has measured supporting evidence, but the precise cause is still a hypothesis: explicit sampled-PD gains interacting with delayed CAN feedback, firmware current-loop dynamics, nonlinearities, scheduling, or power. Saved 41050 uses **100 Hz physics / 50 Hz policy and an implicit PhysX servo**, whereas deployment uses explicit 200 Hz PD/C0; equal gains do not imply equal actuator responses. These findings do not question the previously verified XML/USD geometry or establish a need to replace the bike.
- LIKELIHOOD / UNCERTAINTY: steering-loop instability is confirmed; feedback age/query failures are measured and likely contributors, not a uniquely isolated cause. Rear sign/small-torque behavior is an unresolved warning requiring verification. Policy sim-to-real robustness is a plausible secondary issue, not evaluated by these bags; wrong export/slow ONNX inference is low likelihood. IMU complete-axis calibration, data integrity, and freshness still need checks. Motor damage, mechanical defects, and power faults are not established, and missing firmware/power diagnostics prevent ruling them out. No numerical probabilities are justified.
- PRIORITIZED PLAN (**recommended, not implemented**): keep the bike independently supported; establish smooth repeatable steering tracking with conservative supervised torque/current limits and continuous commands after DDS discovery. Improve feedback timing and tune against the measured plant; do not blindly increase D gain on a delayed/noisy speed signal. Properly tuned firmware position control (C2/C3) is a candidate alternative, not a verified fix; retain 50 Hz policy inference and watchdogs. Isolate motors for direction/step-response/current-response/breakaway/delay tests; current C commands enable both motors. Capture effective parameters, passive raw CAN, diagnostic status/supply voltage, synchronized video, and existing telemetry. Re-check upright IMU zero and marked body axes, investigate actual sensor/RC gaps, and checksum/re-copy A.
- TRAINING FOLLOW-UP: only after stable actuator response and signs are measured, match training to identified delays/jitter, motor dynamics, friction/deadband, limits, and sensor noise, then randomize around measured values. 41050's saved encoder-channel noise is zero; B's stationary steering-speed observation standard deviation is ≈**0.0212 rad/s**. Training ground-truth body linear velocity versus deployment's wheel-speed × radius / zero lateral approximation remains unvalidated during real slipping/balancing. Switching to a DR policy alone will not repair the fixed-command bench instability. **No unsupported balance retry until the steering bench loop is stable.**

## 24. Policy, actuator-command, and physics clocks clarified (2026-10-02 → 03)

- ISAAC LAB: policy/action processing is **50 Hz** (`step_dt=0.02 s`). Current Trackstand configuration is **400 Hz physics**, `sim.dt=0.0025`, decimation 8; saved **09171946/model_41050** is **100 Hz physics**, `dt=0.01`, decimation 2. In the standard PhysX stepping path, `ManagerBasedRLEnv.step()` processes each new action once, then calls `apply_action()` and `scene.write_data_to_sim()` before every physics substep. Thus the held steering-position/rear-effort targets are submitted at 400 Hz currently, or 100 Hz for 41050's saved configuration. Steering/rear damping uses **ImplicitActuator**: PhysX integrates the servo, while Python's `computed_effort`/`applied_effort` is an approximate diagnostic, not an external C0/current loop. **Clarification of §9 shorthand:** 50 Hz describes new policy targets, not the inner solver/actuator calculations.
- MUJOCO: `run_deploy_viewer.py` infers at **50 Hz simulated time** and `NewBikeDeployEnv.step()` writes `data.ctrl` once per 0.02 s, holding it through `round(0.02/model.opt.timestep)` substeps. The augmented trackstand model has **400 Hz physics / eight substeps**, and MuJoCo evaluates the configured steering position servo/rear torque-plus-damping at those physics steps. It does not receive new policy targets at 400 Hz. The viewer's wall-time pacing is not a real-time guarantee, especially with multiple CPU environments. The stale “2 physics substeps” method comment is not the effective model-aware loop.
- DEPLOYMENT: `policy_controller.py` requires **exactly 50 Hz**; original `motor_io.py` has nominal **200 Hz control and 200 Hz A2/A3 polling**. While enabled, it recalculates steering PD/rear damping and submits C0 commands at that control rate: nominally **four torque calculations per policy target**, not four fresh encoder measurements or a guarantee of four motor executions. The new C2 node retains 200 Hz but sends steering position targets instead. Firmware-internal response frequency remains separate. Therefore policy rates agree across all three paths; low-level mechanisms and rates are **not identical**. Evidence: task/current and saved `env.yaml`, installed Isaac Lab `manager_based_rl_env.py`/PhysX articulation/`ImplicitActuator`, MuJoCo viewer augmentation and `env.step()`, deployment timers/parameters.
- MOTOR FIRMWARE FREQUENCY — **UNKNOWN**: the local CAN Rev.3.09b0, RS485, SDC300, ZE300 GUI documents and host-driver material do not specify the C2/C3 position→speed→Q-axis-current update frequencies. B6/B7 expose position PI gains, B8/B9 speed PI gains, B2/B3 speed/current limits; none supplies a loop period. CAN **1 Mbit/s**, the **200 ms** status-report interval, and the documented **50 Hz filter bandwidth** are not internal control frequencies. No numeric firmware-loop frequency or 200 Hz sufficiency claim is justified without manufacturer information or a measured response experiment.

## 25. Follow-up: do four 200 Hz torque updates provide adequate steering tracking? (2026-10-02)

- ARTIFACTS / METHOD: [tracking report](deploy/reports/steering_tracking_2026-10-02/README.md), [exact results](deploy/reports/steering_tracking_2026-10-02/tracking_results.json), `analyze_tracking.py`, [step/re-enable plot](deploy/reports/steering_tracking_2026-10-02/step_and_reenable_response.png), and [long-hold plot](deploy/reports/steering_tracking_2026-10-02/constant_target_long_hold.png). Reuses the original A/B/C bags; their hashes were rechecked, not changed. Times are host tick/receipt measurements, not firmware acquisition timestamps. A 90% displacement crossing is **not settling**; reported settling requires all remaining recorded samples in band and at least **250 ms** confirmation. Bands are analysis definitions, not certified balancing requirements.
- POSITIVE STEP (C interval 2): target **+1.146°**, initial **+0.088°**. First four ticks (**0–16.93 ms**) retain the initial position; first changed encoder value is available at **25.26 ms**; first 90% displacement crossing is **47.72 ms**, the tenth tick. **410** successful steering sends span **2.055 s**. Final 0.5 s position is **+0.901°**, with **0.245°** target error: never settles within ±0.1° of target; remains inside ±0.3° from 47.72 ms, and settles around the **biased final position**, within one encoder count, from **110.68 ms**.
- NEGATIVE STEP / LONG HOLD: C interval 3 targets **−1.146°** from **+0.901°**; first changed position **25.82 ms**, first 90% crossing **46.91 ms**, followed by overshoot/oscillation. Its displacement is about twice the positive step, so it is not a matched direction comparison. Interval 4 re-enables at **−11.382°** with target −1.146° and holds it for **364** successful sends / **1.824 s** (about **91** nominal policy periods), yet spans **−18.918° to +19.556°** and never settles inside ±0.1° or ±0.3°. First changed position after re-enable is **159.60 ms**. New policy targets cannot be the sole cause of this fixed-target instability.
- NEW CURRENT-DELIVERY EVIDENCE: interval 4's first **150 ms** has **29** steering commands requesting **1.185–1.794 N·m**, but **29** received current-derived torque samples indicate only **0–0.00546 N·m**, median zero. First received magnitude ≥0.01 N·m occurs at **153.85 ms**, versus **21.11 ms** for interval 2. This establishes a startup/re-enable delivery discrepancy, **not** a uniquely identified CAN transit delay, firmware enable time, current-loop constant, or stiction cause. No per-command reply-pairing assumption is needed for these initial-window statistics.
- FEEDBACK / TARGET BANDWIDTH: complete received-target groups in the positive step contain **3 ticks (15 groups), 4 (70), or 5 (13)**, excluding edge groups. **31.0%** of positive-step ticks reuse an A3 receipt timestamp; distinct A3 intervals median/max **6.21/24.25 ms**, position receipt age up to **26.23 ms**. The negative long hold also reuses A3 on 31.0% of ticks. B's dry-run 41050 target increments are median/p95/max **0.01084°/0.04126°/0.09327°** per contiguous enabled step, not repeated one-degree jumps; this supported dry run does not establish live-balancing bandwidth. A is unpowered, and C tests only 200 Hz: there is no matched powered 100/200/400 Hz comparison.
- CONCLUSION: the recorded controller does **not consistently attain a new target within 20 ms**, but the bags do **not prove that 200 Hz is intrinsically insufficient**, or that increasing it will fix tracking. Repeated target reception does not restart motion; a servo need not finish every move before the next target. Stable gain/lag/limits/noise compatibility with the implicit simulated actuator matters more than the nominal four-to-one ratio. Fix current-delivery transitions and fixed-target stability before blaming policy frequency.
- STAGE D — **PROPOSED, NOT COMPLETED**: [protocol](deploy/reports/steering_tracking_2026-10-02/STAGE_D_PROTOCOL.md) separates D0 disabled scheduling at 100/200/400 Hz with polling fixed at 200 Hz, D1 already-enabled equal ±0.25° steps held 2 s and repeated five times, D2 five startup/watchdog/explicit-disable re-enable comparisons, and D3 interleaved matched rate trials followed by **0.5/1/2/5 Hz** sine trajectories published at 50 Hz. Powered D1–D3 require an as-yet **unimplemented guarded steering-only mode**; production C0/C2 nodes enable both motors and do not supply those tighter guards. Proposed starting cap **0.1 N·m (~0.128 A at 0.78 N·m/A)**, ±3° actual-angle and 2 rad/s aborts are diagnostic choices, not certified safety limits. Preserve raw CAN, firmware A0/AE/B4/B6–B9, effective parameters, supply voltage and synchronized video. Analysis tests passed **5**, alongside the **11** existing motor-control tests.

## 26. Continuous sine command and recorded steering plots (2026-10-02 → 03)

- PUBLISHER: the current on-disk helper is [`deploy/scripts/steering_sine.py`](deploy/scripts/steering_sine.py), originally introduced as a `.sh` helper with embedded Python. It keeps one `rclpy` publisher alive at nominal **50 Hz**, sending `[1.0, amplitude*sin(2*pi*t/period), 0.0]` to `/trackstand/motor_command`; a literal `ros2 topic pub --rate 50` repeats one fixed YAML value, and restarting the CLI for every sample is not the implemented solution. Defaults: **±1 rad**, **10 s period**, duration **0** (run until stopped; preview prints one period). Explicit `--preview` or `--execute` is required; amplitude must be `(0,1]` rad, period `>0.04 s`, duration nonnegative/finite. It uses monotonic phase/steady-clock timing, 2 s graph discovery, requires exactly one motor-I/O subscriber and no competing publisher, checks the graph each second, and attempts **ten `[0,0,0]` disable messages at 20 ms spacing** on graceful exit; the motor watchdog remains the fallback for hard termination. Neither guarantees physical disable. Manual commands enable **both motors**; zero rear request still retains damping, and RC-down without the policy node is not an interlock. ±1 rad (57.3°) was not established safe by the earlier small-step tests.
- RECORDED SINE TEST: `rosbags/rosbag2_2026_10_02-11_01_09`, duration **54.24994234 s**, **55,453 decoded messages**, no metadata-count discrepancy. **2,712** commands all have enable=1 and rear target=0; command interval median/max **20.000128/21.072389 ms**, average **50.001081 Hz** from received timestamps. **9,222** motor states, **10,850** steering torque samples (**21,700** both-motor torque messages), and **10,847** control/fault samples each. This bag uses the original host-PD/C0 telemetry schema and does **not** test the later C2 implementation. It starts during an ongoing waveform; no start/stop-disable transition is certified by this captured interval.
- RANGE / EVIDENCE: commanded steering **−1.000000 to +1.000000 rad**; position feedback **−1.051160 to +1.068418 rad**; speed feedback **−3.766770 to +4.227537 rad/s**; steering current-derived torque **−0.266760 to +0.276900 N·m**. Torque remains measured Q-axis current × B0 Kt with sign correction, not direct shaft measurement. These extrema alone do not identify tracking bandwidth, root cause, firmware loop frequency, or C2 improvement. Provenance and bag SHA-256 `8ea2b0f99f87dbd81cc7ae23760ce81a8c1a1bddc779713aa1fc7ac098d0d872`: [plot_provenance.json](deploy/reports/steering_sine_2026-10-02_110109/plot_provenance.json).
- RETAINED GRAPHS: [combined position command/feedback and torque](deploy/reports/steering_sine_2026-10-02_110109/steering_command_torque.png), plus four separate [position-command](deploy/reports/steering_sine_2026-10-02_110109/steering_position_command.png), [position-feedback](deploy/reports/steering_sine_2026-10-02_110109/steering_position_feedback.png), [velocity-feedback](deploy/reports/steering_sine_2026-10-02_110109/steering_velocity_feedback.png), and [torque-feedback](deploy/reports/steering_sine_2026-10-02_110109/steering_torque_feedback.png) plots. PNG and SVG versions are kept, on the same bag-time axis, with **no smoothing, decimation or artificial time shift**. `plot_command_torque.py` reproduces them without initializing a ROS node; original bag data remains unchanged.

## 27. Firmware steering position control: C3 prototype superseded by C2 (2026-10-02 → 03)

- IMPLEMENTATION HISTORY / C3 LIMITATION: added the separate ROS executable `motor_io_position`, initially using C3. `/trackstand/motor_command` stayed `[enable, absolute_startup_zero_relative_steering_rad, drive_torque_nm]`; the prototype sent `target − latest_measured_position` as a relative displacement, converted to signed motor counts. The user's assumption settled native C3/A3 positive **direction**, not whether C3 is relative to actual position or previous commanded target. The local PDF does not explicitly resolve that repeated-command reference. If firmware uses the previous target, subtracting measured position is wrong; host feedback delay can also bias a current-relative conversion. The prototype therefore defaulted `c3_current_reference_verified=false`, gating powered outputs pending verification. Its software-only validation passed **169 total / 67 C3-node tests** plus an isolated build; this did not prove C3 firmware semantics or physical tracking.
- USER CHOICE / CURRENT STATE: replaced that prototype with **C2 absolute position control** in [`motor_io_position.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_io_position.py). The protocol's p.14 C2 payload is opcode `0xC2` + signed **int32 little-endian absolute encoder count**, DLC **5**, **16,384 counts/revolution**; reply has A3's position layout, not current. `C2_target = startup_A3_multi_turn_zero + round(position_sign * gear_ratio * slewed_target_rad * 16384/(2*pi))`, with count-level angle clamping and int32 overflow rejection. There is **no live measured-position subtraction or previous-target accumulation**. A held target yields identical C2 bytes as actual feedback moves. This removes the host conversion's live-feedback dependence and C3 reference ambiguity, **not** encoder/zero errors, transport delay, firmware tuning requirements, or mechanical tracking errors.
- SIGN / ACTION UNITS: user reaffirmed native increasing steering position turns **left from the rider's view**. The established policy coordinate is positive right, so `steering_encoder_sign=-1`, gear ratio **1**, means `C2_target = startup_zero − round(target_rad*16384/(2*pi))`; +0.30 rad maps to approximately zero−782 counts. The target is **processed steering angle**, not raw network output: the unchanged policy controller applies raw action × **1.57 rad**, then the hardware angle clamp; the motor node applies its existing slew, without a second action scale. Existing motor ID/sign settings are preserved: steer `0x102/0x002`, encoder/current signs `−1/−1`; rear `0x101/0x001`, encoder/current `+1/−1`. Historical user checks are positive C0→steer left/rear backward, positive A2/A3→steer left/rear forward. These reports are not a new powered C2 validation.
- ZERO / SAFETY: first valid paired A3/A2 acquisition latches the raw **multi-turn A3 count from bytes 3–6** as zero. Hold steering physically straight at startup; no B1 persistent-origin write occurs. Zero is retained across disabled ticks and RC down/up cycles; restart with steering straight after motor power/reset or origin changes. Live A2/A3 still supplies policy observations and freshness/angle/speed safety; uninitialized/stale/out-of-range unwrapped feedback cannot actuate. Absolute int32 overflow faults rather than wrapping. The old C3-verification parameter was **removed**, not defaulted true. `dry_run=true` suppresses actuation/configuration writes, not B0/A2/A3 sensor queries.
- POLICY SELECTION CHECKPOINT: this steering-mode change does **not** change the actor. Current on-disk `policy_controller.py` defaults to **09221945/model_91850**, `trackstand_rl_rsl_2026-09-22_19-45-06_model_91850.onnx`, checksum constant `9232a0723df8806e8b5f6fb4e39a7a11ccb5af8bf058d9026eddb57dab8fd806`; the **09171946/model_41050** filename/checksum pair remains as two commented alternative lines. This is the current source default, not evidence that the earlier Pi A/B/C tests used 91850: the user's explicit confirmation remains **41050**. Firmware position-loop gains still need validation whichever actor is selected.
- PRESERVED CONTROL / LIMITS: policy **50 Hz**, CAN control/polling **200 Hz**, steering target **±90°**, target slew **23.9 rad/s** (nominal **0.1195 rad/tick**), rear C0 `clip(request−0.05*rear_speed, ±6.28 N·m)`, original watchdog **80 ms**, paired-feedback freshness **50 ms**, steering fault-clear **5°**, angle/speed fault and zero-current/CF disable behavior. Before powered C2, B2 speed cap and B3 current cap are written and independently read back, rounded **down** to preserve **23.9 rad/s** and **5.6 N·m** using measured B0 Kt; missing/mismatched confirmation blocks both motors, retry at most once per second. Setup may lengthen a tick, so feedback/command ages are rechecked afterward. Firmware B6–B9 gains are **not changed** or asserted equal to host PD `10/0.2`; internal loop frequency remains unknown.
- TELEMETRY: extra nonblocking steering **A1** query each active cycle preserves current-derived `/trackstand/motor_torque`; C2 replies are ignored by the torque decoder. `/trackstand/motor_control` is now **`motor_control_position_v2:<fields>`**, with original fields first and C2 zero/absolute target/offset/encoded radians, B2/B3 confirmation/raw caps, A1 send results and gate-clock time appended. Old C3 reference/displacement fields are removed. Host steering PD/current/torque-command fields are **NaN**, not fabricated estimates; rear fields stay valid. `/trackstand/motor_status` reports **`C2_absolute_position`** and startup zero. Match recorded layout labels; CAN socket-send success does not confirm firmware execution.
- RUN / ALTERNATIVES: build under `deploy/` with `colcon build --symlink-install --packages-select trackstand_deploy`, source `install/setup.bash`, then run `ros2 run trackstand_deploy motor_io_position --ros-args --params-file source/trackstand_deploy/config/params.yaml -p dry_run:=true`; only a supported, supervised follow-up uses false. Do **not** run `motor_io` simultaneously: both use `/trackstand_motor_io` and the same topic/parameter names. Original `motor_io.py`, `params.yaml`, setup entry points and default launch remained unchanged by the conversion; default launch still starts **C0**, not C2. Use the current ROS-topic `steering_sine.py` to exercise this node. The separate raw-CAN [`steering_sine_position.py`](deploy/scripts/steering_sine_position.py) now emits C2 at **50 Hz**, default ±1 rad/10 s/host0x102/sign−1, but centers on **native zero**, not the latched startup zero, and has **no feedback, B2/B3 setup, watchdog, or explicit disable on exit**. It is not an equivalent safe substitute and must not run alongside a motor-I/O node.
- AUDIT / VALIDATION / OPEN WORK: C2 conversion, repeated-target independence, zero retention, signs/units, quantized limits, overflow, limit readback, unchanged rear laws, stale/watchdog/send-failure handling, telemetry and idempotent `rclpy.try_shutdown()` were checked. AST comparisons preserve copied non-steering logic. **208 deployment tests passed, including 106 C2-node tests**; Python compilation and an isolated `/tmp` colcon build passed and registered `motor_io_position`. A recording-time rerun including the five tracking-analysis tests passed **213 tests**, with one unrelated Matplotlib Axes3D availability warning. No bike hardware was commanded by validation. [Node instructions](deploy/docs/MOTOR_IO_POSITION.md) document operation; the current ROS sine filename is `.py` (older instructions may still mention `.sh`). **C2 tracking improvement is unmeasured**: supported-bike direction/zero, response, firmware-gain/current-limit and enable-transition tests remain required before unsupported balancing. Training should be adapted only after stable measured actuator behavior is available.

## 28. Standalone C2 steering sine test: tracking lag, attenuation, and final holding (test 2026-10-03; recorded 2026-10-04)

- SOURCES / PROVENANCE: original [`motor_feedback_202610031439.csv`](deploy/scripts/motor_feedback_202610031439.csv), SHA-256 **`5da80914e54d38968e256da78e8c221467c531907d5c4aef1972fb6444781e98`**, rechecked unchanged. Existing [report summary](deploy/reports/steering_sine_20261003_1439/summary.json), [full-record graph](deploy/reports/steering_sine_20261003_1439/steering_sine_report.png), [middle-second graph](deploy/reports/steering_sine_20261003_1439/steering_sine_zoom.png), and HTML/PDF/SVG views are retained. Recording **30.52834 s**, **16,662 rows**: **6,106** steering feedback rows, **6,106** rear feedback rows, and **4,450** outgoing steering C2 targets observed over **3.70372–26.26801 s**. The existing summary records ranges/rates, **not** the phase/settling calculations below; these were independently calculated from the CSV and rerun when recording this entry.
- USER-CONFIRMED SETUP: steering/front wheel was completely free to move and visibly followed the sine. Only `deploy/scripts/steering_sine_position.py` and `motor_feedback.py` ran; **neither `motor_io` nor `motor_io_position` nor policy inference was running**. User changed the test sine settings to **±2 rad at 10 Hz**, consistent with the recorded targets. This is a standalone actuator experiment, not a 41050/91850 policy test, unsupported balancing trial, or matched C0/C2 comparison. Firmware gain/limit settings and power/reset history were not captured sufficiently to certify their values.
- RAW HELPER UPDATE / HISTORICAL CORRECTION: the current [`steering_sine_position.py`](deploy/scripts/steering_sine_position.py) uses nominal **200 Hz** and reads **A3 once** to center C2 targets on the startup multi-turn count; no persistent-origin reset. This supersedes §27's earlier raw-helper description of **50 Hz/native-zero centering**; the ROS C2 node described there is a separate implementation. Current host defaults remain **±1 rad / 10 s period**, so they must not be substituted for the user's Pi test settings. The raw helper has **no continuous feedback control, ±90° clamp, target slew limiter, B2/B3 configuration/readback, watchdog, or disable-on-exit**. Stopping it leaves the final target active; stopping the read-only recorder also sends no disable command. Do not run it alongside a motor-I/O node or treat it as deployment's guarded C2 path.
- DATA FLOW / TIMING LIMITS: [`motor_feedback.py`](deploy/scripts/motor_feedback.py) sends only A3/A2/A1/B0 read queries and passively observes outgoing C2 through SocketCAN loopback. A3 zero **−60,758 counts**; checked position conversion **`-(count-zero)*2*pi/16384` rad**, gear ratio 1, with unwrapped A3 used for analysis. A2 uses signed **0.01 rpm**, converted to rad/s; torque is sign-corrected **A1 measured Q-axis current × B0 Kt ≈0.78 N·m/A**, not a mechanical shaft-torque measurement. Commands use recorder-thread receipt timestamps; feedback uses each query's own reply-receipt timestamp. These are **not hardware TX/firmware execution/acquisition timestamps**. A3/A2/A1 are sequential, with median A3→A2/A1 receipt skew **0.500/1.003 ms**, not simultaneous. No missing steering position/speed/current values in this CSV. Observed C2 rate **197.16994 Hz**, median/max interval **5.05637/5.80975 ms**; A3/A2/A1 rates about **199.993 Hz**, median intervals about **5.000 ms**; A3 maximum interval **6.41508 ms**. These host rates do not reveal the internal motor-loop frequency or guarantee timing under full ROS load.
- STEADY TRACKING (recorder seconds **5–25**): fitted command frequency **10.0000038 Hz**; observed targets **−2.000311 to +1.999544 rad**, approximately ±114.6°. Fit `a*sin(2*pi*f*t)+b*cos(2*pi*f*t)+c` to A3 at its own timestamps: position amplitude **0.354253 rad (20.297°)**, sine-fit **R²=0.99855**; actual steady extrema **−0.369689/+0.368155 rad**. Relative to the fundamental of the recorded held command (**1.991237 rad** amplitude), output gain is **0.177906 (17.8%)**. Actual position repeats at the command frequency without growing oscillation in this interval, **but does not reach the commanded stroke**. Against the latest held C2 at each A3 sample, position-error RMS **1.400255 rad (80.229°)**, p95 absolute **1.965068 rad**, maximum absolute **2.037510 rad**. Current-derived torque over the full record spans **−4.857840 to +5.789160 N·m** and remains visibly variable; repeatable position motion is not proof of perfectly smooth torque or general closed-loop stability.
- DELAY ESTIMATE / METHOD: reconstruct each observed C2 target as held until the next observed packet, fit its fundamental on a **0.1 ms** grid, then compare with the fitted A3 phase. At 10 Hz the phase lag is **81.5265°**, equivalent to **22.6462 ms**. Independent held-command/A3 correlation over **5.05–24.95 s**, testing **0–50 ms** lags at **0.25 ms** spacing, gives **22.75 ms**, correlation **0.99506**. A synthetic sine with known **23 ms** lag validates the phase convention. One-second-window estimates span **22.504–22.900 ms** (window variation, **not** a confidence interval). Fitting against the command sample values as an ideal continuous sine instead gives **25.1830 ms**; the approximately **2.54 ms** difference reflects command holding. These are **frequency-specific, amplitude-dependent phase-equivalent tracking lags**, not an identified pure transport delay, firmware processing time, settling time, or certified actuator bandwidth. Severe amplitude attenuation means the output is not merely a time-delayed copy; one frequency cannot identify a unique plant model or fixed delay.
- DEMAND / LIKELY LIMITATION: the fitted ±2 rad/10 Hz sine requires peak speed **`A*2*pi*f ≈125.642 rad/s`**, versus measured maximum **22.0634 rad/s** and deployment's **23.9 rad/s** configured limit: approximately **5.7× measured / 5.26× configured**. **42.43%** of recorded targets also exceed deployment's ±90° range. Strong attenuation and near-flat speed peaks support **speed-limited behavior as a likely contributor**, not a uniquely proven firmware-limit diagnosis; B2/B3/gains/status were not logged and this raw script does not apply deployment's caps. This over-demand trajectory cannot establish faithful policy-scale tracking, small-signal bandwidth, or that the motor is defective. The 5.6 N·m configured deployment cap must not be assumed enforced in this raw test merely because most current estimates lie below it.
- STARTUP / FINAL HOLD: relative to the first observed C2, first received **|position|>0.01 rad** is **16.855 ms**, **|speed|>1 rad/s** at **17.361 ms**, and **|current-derived torque|>0.01 N·m** at **17.873 ms**. These are threshold-dependent host observations, not exact dead times; they do not reproduce §25's approximately 154 ms current-delivery gap in this startup, but **do not test repeated disable/re-enable recovery**. Last C2 target is **−56,689 counts = −1.560442 rad (−89.407°)**; interpolated position then is about **+0.219248 rad**. During the following **4.25933 s**, the motor reaches the target. Final one-second median equals the target count exactly, with fluctuations within **±2 counts (±0.00076699 rad, about ±0.04395°)**. From the last C2, all remaining recorded positions stay within ±0.1/0.01/0.003/0.001 rad after **264.323/464.380/604.393/1009.476 ms**, respectively, with at least **250 ms** confirmation. This is finite-record settling after the sine stops changing, with nonzero initial position/speed, **not a controlled step-response test** or motor-disable event.
- CONCLUSIONS / PREVIOUS DOUBTS: C2 absolute-position execution, repeatable free-steering motion, and accurate final holding are supported; the hypothesis that the motor is fundamentally incapable of stable motion is weakened. This favors investigating the earlier external PD/C0 control path, feedback timing, firmware mode/gains/enable behavior before replacing hardware or blaming the policy, **without isolating one root cause or proving C2 fixes deployment**. The earlier fixed-target C0 instability remains independent of policy variation. A servo can continue moving across target updates; it need not attain each target before the next arrives. This standalone approximately 200 Hz C2 experiment **does not answer whether four 200 Hz external torque calculations per 50 Hz policy period are sufficient**, identify firmware loop frequency, certify ground-loaded behavior, or justify an unsupported balance retry. “C2 tracking improvement is unmeasured” in §27 is now qualified by this bench evidence, not replaced with a matched-comparison or policy-transfer claim.
- NEXT EXPERIMENT — **RECOMMENDED, NOT RUN**: supported, supervised small-amplitude C2 frequency sweep, e.g. **±0.05 rad at 0.5/1/2/5/10 Hz**, with documented/read-back firmware speed/current limits and gains. At 10 Hz the peak speed demand is only **3.142 rad/s**. Repeat with **50 Hz target updates**, separately from 200 Hz, keeping fixture/settings comparable and recording separate command/A3/A2/A1 timestamps plus firmware status/limits. This would distinguish ordinary tracking gain/lag from the present excessive-demand behavior and assess the actual policy interface. Suggested amplitudes are diagnostic choices, **not certified safety limits**; deployment's watchdogs/interlocks and a physical emergency stop must not be bypassed for a balancing trial. No runtime, policy, training, simulation, model, original CSV, or report file was modified to record these findings; only this progress entry was added.

## 29. C2 steering frequency/amplitude sweep: limited tracking bandwidth, not a fixed motor delay (2026-10-04)

- SCOPE / CURRENT PATHS: user supplied **11 additional CSVs**, completing **12 conditions**: **2/5/10 Hz × peak amplitudes 0.1/0.5/1/2 rad**, including §28's original 10 Hz/±2 rad record. Filename `01rad` means **±0.1 rad, 0.2 rad peak-to-peak**; `05rad` means ±0.5 rad, and `2rad` means ±2 rad. Every label was independently checked against the recorded C2 waveform. Current source location is `deploy/experiments/logs/`; helpers and reports are under `deploy/experiments/` and `deploy/experiments/reports/`. The original CSV is now named `motor_feedback_202610031439_10hz2rad.csv`, with the **same SHA-256 as §28**; historical `deploy/scripts/` and `deploy/reports/` references precede this relocation. User confirmed steering was free to move in all new tests and motor conditions were unchanged; exact firmware settings/status were not independently logged. These are C2 actuator experiments, **not a recorded 50 Hz policy-interface or balancing evaluation**.
- ARTIFACTS / WORKFLOW: [complete written analysis](deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/README.md), [HTML comparison/index of all conditions](deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/index.html), [comparison PNG](deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/c2_sweep_comparison.png) with PDF/SVG versions, and [small-amplitude overlays](deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/small_amplitude_tracking.png). [Comparison CSV](deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/comparison.csv) and [complete JSON](deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/comparison.json) retain exact metrics, receipt-clock windows, source filenames/hashes, timing, fit residuals and final-hold statistics. Generated **11 new per-condition bundles**, each with full-record PNG/PDF, middle-second PNG, **five SVG windows**, selectable four-panel HTML (C2 target/A3 position/A2 speed/A1 current-derived torque), README, `analysis.json`, plot `summary.json`, and a three-cycle command/feedback overlay. The original report at [steering_sine_20261003_1439](deploy/experiments/reports/steering_sine_20261003_1439/steering_sine_report.html) was **preserved byte-for-byte**; its embedded old source path is stale, while the sweep index links the relocated CSV correctly.
- ANALYSIS / UNITS: new offline [`analyze_c2_sine.py`](deploy/experiments/analyze_c2_sine.py) reuses [`plot_motor_feedback.py`](deploy/experiments/plot_motor_feedback.py). For each record, discard the first **max(1 s, 3 periods)** and last **max(0.25 s, 1 period)** of the observed command interval, then fit frequency and sin/cos/DC terms at each signal's own receipt timestamps. Gain is fitted A3 amplitude divided by the fundamental amplitude of the recorded zero-order-held C2 targets, reconstructed on a **0.1 ms** grid; it is not full-record peak-to-peak tracking. Error compares A3 with the latest held target without compensating phase. Below, feedback amplitude, RMS error, maximum absolute speed and maximum absolute current-derived torque all use the **same steady window**. Torque remains signed A1 Q-current × B0 Kt, not a shaft sensor. The original 10 Hz/±2 rad comparison row uses **4.703723–26.018014 s**, unlike §28's 5–25 s window; small numerical differences are expected, not contradictory.

| Sine frequency (Hz) | Command peak amplitude (rad) | Fitted feedback amplitude (rad) | Gain (%) | Phase lag (°) | Phase-equivalent lag (ms) | Position-error RMS (rad) | Peak absolute speed (rad/s) | Peak absolute torque estimate (N·m) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 2 | 0.1 | 0.0635 | 63.5 | 50.5 | 70.12 | 0.0546 | 1.09 | 0.325 |
| 2 | 0.5 | 0.3311 | 66.2 | 49.0 | 67.99 | 0.2675 | 4.48 | 0.593 |
| 2 | 1 | 0.6617 | 66.2 | 48.6 | 67.51 | 0.5314 | 8.48 | 0.619 |
| 2 | 2 | 1.3239 | 66.2 | 48.5 | 67.30 | 1.0593 | 16.95 | 0.948 |
| 5 | 0.1 | 0.0324 | 32.5 | 72.5 | 40.29 | 0.0675 | 1.25 | 0.355 |
| 5 | 0.5 | 0.1676 | 33.6 | 71.1 | 39.50 | 0.3349 | 5.45 | 0.760 |
| 5 | 1 | 0.3363 | 33.7 | 71.0 | 39.42 | 0.6694 | 10.74 | 1.531 |
| 5 | 2 | 0.6745 | 33.8 | 71.1 | 39.48 | 1.3398 | 21.93 | 2.480 |
| 10 | 0.1 | 0.0168 | 16.9 | 80.3 | 22.31 | 0.0698 | 1.30 | 0.370 |
| 10 | 0.5 | 0.0889 | 17.9 | 81.5 | 22.63 | 0.3497 | 5.86 | 1.417 |
| 10 | 1 | 0.1786 | 17.9 | 81.2 | 22.55 | 0.6996 | 12.07 | 2.566 |
| 10 | 2 | 0.3543 | 17.8 | 81.5 | 22.65 | 1.3998 | 22.06 | 5.789 |

- MAIN FINDING / QUALIFICATION OF §28: **repeatable but strongly frequency-dependent attenuation and phase lag persist at small amplitudes**. The ±0.1 rad tests demand only about **1.26/3.14/6.28 rad/s** at 2/5/10 Hz, below deployment's **23.9 rad/s reference**, yet achieve only **63.5/32.5/16.9%** gain. Similar gain/phase across a **20× amplitude range** means the earlier excessive ±2 rad/10 Hz speed demand **cannot explain the sweep by itself**; limited effective position-tracking bandwidth is strongly supported under these conditions. Speed/current limiting may still contribute at large demands, but no actual B2/B3 readback establishes which limits were active. The 0.1 rad conditions show modest additional attenuation/phase differences; **no specific stiction/deadband threshold or damaged motor is identified**. Position motion is largely sinusoidal and repeatable, while current-derived torque still fluctuates; this is not certification of smooth torque or stability under arbitrary loads.
- NO UNIVERSAL DELAY: phase-equivalent lag is approximately **67–70 ms at 2 Hz, 39–40 ms at 5 Hz, 22–23 ms at 10 Hz**, while phase lag increases from about **48.5–50.5° → 71.0–72.5° → 80.3–81.5°**. Shorter equivalent milliseconds at higher frequency do **not** mean better tracking. §28's approximately 23 ms value remains specific to 10 Hz, **not a constant motor/CAN dead time**. Outgoing C2 receipt rates are **197.026–197.176 Hz**, not firmware execution rates; all measurements retain the host-receipt/sequential-query caveats of §28. These records do not identify internal position/speed/current-loop frequency or settle whether four external C0 torque calculations per 50 Hz policy period suffice.
- DESCRIPTIVE RESPONSE MODEL — **FIT, NOT FIRMWARE IDENTIFICATION**: equal-weight complex-response fit to the **nine ±0.5/1/2 rad conditions**, excluding the 0.1 rad points, gives **`H(jω)=K/(1+jωτ)`**, **K=1.00525945**, **τ=0.090346423 s (90.35 ms)**, model-implied corner **1.7616076 Hz**; complex-response RMS residual **0.00457438**, maximum phase discrepancy **1.529°**, maximum gain discrepancy **0.00495419**. This summarizes measured effective closed-loop behavior; τ is **not pure delay**, and the fitted corner lies **below the lowest tested 2 Hz**, so it is **not a directly measured cutoff**. No independent validation dataset uniquely identifies this model or attributes it to firmware PI gains, filtering, electrical dynamics, or mechanics. Static holding supports an approximately unity eventual response, rather than a fixed dynamic angle-scale error.
- REPEATABILITY / FINAL HOLD: maximum relative gain spread across **three subwindows** of any record is **0.675532% (about 0.676%)**; this is within-record repeatability, **not** independent-repeat confidence. Across final recorded one-second hold windows, maximum absolute median target error is **0.000383495 rad (one encoder count, about 0.022°)**, maximum instantaneous error **0.001533981 rad (four counts, about 0.088°)**. C2 can hold a fixed encoder target accurately despite poor fast-trajectory tracking. Final-hold statistics assume no later control command outside the observed capture; these are **not controlled step responses, exact settling specifications, or motor-disable verification**. The raw helper still has no disable-on-exit.
- RECORDING INTEGRITY / GAP-PLOT CHANGE: all source hashes and count-to-angle/current-to-torque mappings were checked. Each steering measurement row contains its own timestamp/value, but the **2 Hz/±2 rad** record has A3 receipt gaps **0.555646–0.897387 s (~341.74 ms)** and **5.652611–5.758635 s (~106.02 ms)**, with corresponding A2/A1 gaps. Both precede its selected **5.998575–24.034600 s** steady window. No selected steady window has a >50 ms C2/A3/A2/A1 gap; maximum spacing across them is **11.67618 ms**. Updated the plotting helper to **break feedback lines and shade gaps >50 ms red**, not fabricate a response across outages. Gap cause/physical behavior is unknown; raw plots otherwise retain original clocks/values, without smoothing or phase shifts. Independent interpolated correlation uses only the selected window and refuses to bridge long A3 gaps.
- VALIDATION / IMPLEMENTED SCOPE: [validation record](deploy/experiments/reports/c2_sine_sweep_20261004/VALIDATION.md); **16 numerical-method tests passed** in [`test_c2_sine_analysis.py`](deploy/experiments/tests/test_c2_sine_analysis.py), covering known gain/phase, amplitude parsing, zero-order holding/extrapolation guards, missing measurements/gaps, finite-record settling, and recovery of a known first-order model. Independent correlation agrees within **1 ms** with the phase fit for **all 12 records**, without validating absolute hardware timestamp accuracy. Parsed **55 new SVGs**, checked PNG/PDF signatures and all five HTML views, verified **119 local HTML links**, and cross-checked all **12 CSV/JSON comparison rows**. Syntax, Pyflakes and `git diff --check` passed; existing Matplotlib Axes3D warning is unrelated to these 2D outputs. Analysis added the offline helper/tests and report bundles and changed only the offline plotter's gap reporting/rendering; **no runtime controller, motor configuration, policy, simulation, original CSV, or original reference report was changed**, and no hardware was commanded during analysis. Full generation refuses to overwrite existing report directories; numerical-test command: `MPLCONFIGDIR=/tmp/trackstand_c2_mpl python3 -m unittest discover -s deploy/experiments/tests -v`.
- NEXT WORK — **RECOMMENDED, NOT COMPLETED**: read back **B2/B3** speed/current limits, **B6–B9** position/speed PI gains, and **AE/status/voltage**; do not copy host torque-PD gains into firmware PI registers or blindly increase gains. Extend free-steering small-amplitude testing to **0.2/0.5/1 Hz** plus supervised small-step/enable-transition tests, repeat with the actual **50 Hz target-update interface**, and check ground-loaded steering with the bike independently supported. Match measured actuator behavior with simulation/training only after stable response is established. A 50 Hz policy does **not** demand 50 Hz physical steering motion; transfer depends on the spectrum of its actual commands and compatible actuator dynamics. These data favor characterizing/improving the actuator interface before judging policy transfer or replacing hardware, **not** proof that C2 fixes balancing or permission to retry unsupported balance.
