## 30. Tsinghua nominal bicycle model and LQR/PID explanation (review 2026-10-02; recorded 2026-10-04)

- PREVIOUSLY UNRECORDED ARTIFACT: [offline LQR/PID control-process page](tsinghua_bike_project/lqr_pid_control_process.html) documents the state/model, full controller-to-CAN paths, comparison, source references, and interactive signal calculations. Its primary comparison is `source/new_balance/new_balance/main_balance.py` versus `cascaded_main_balance.py`; older backup and teaching-package architectures are distinguished. This is a source explanation, not a successful hardware-run or balancing result.
- MODEL / INPUT: [`LinearBicycleModel`](<tsinghua_bike_project/assign4&5_bike_mujocosim_with_control/assign4&5_bike_mujocosim_with_control/nominal_bike_control/bicycle_model.py>) uses **`x = [steering angle δ, roll φ, roll rate φ_dot]^T`** and **`u = commanded steering angular velocity`**, in rad/s. Its `Bu` is the B in `x_dot = A*x + B*u`: **`A = [[0,0,0], [0,0,1], [a2,a4,0]]`, `B = [[1],[0],[a1]]`**. Coefficients are `a1 = m*a*h*v*cos(lambda)/(b*I)`, `a2 = (m*v^2*h - m*a*c*g)*cos(lambda)/(b*I)`, and `a4 = m*g*h/I`, where a is rear-contact-to-COM distance, b wheelbase, c trail, h COM height and I effective roll inertia. Forward speed schedules the matrices; rear-wheel effort/current is not another component of this model input. The page's LQR YAML example at **v=1 m/s** gives **a1=3.244411, a2=23.415889, a4=76.509041**. The node clamps its speed estimate to at least **1 m/s**, including at standstill, so this is not an independently established zero-speed trackstand model.
- LQR PATH: the registered `balance_node` selects `main_balance.py`. Continuous-time Riccati design gives `K = R^-1*B^T*P`; the controller solves a reference equilibrium and applies **`u = u_eq + K*(x_eq-x)`**, then clipping/sign conversion and **C1 steering-speed output**. The reviewed configuration uses **200 Hz control**, model/gain refresh every fourth tick (**50 Hz**), and **±10 rad/s** output limits. Normal `main_balance.py` tracks a steering-angle reference; `new_main_balance.py` instead uses a roll reference and is a separate source variant. Rear speed commands are a separate path.
- PID PATH / MAIN DIFFERENCE: `cascaded_balance_node` runs three serial scalar stages: **zero steering-rate error → roll reference → roll-rate reference → Q-axis current → C0**. Default stage limits are **±0.3 rad / ±5 rad/s / ±2 A**, and the declared default period is **0.01 s (100 Hz)**. Each stage uses rectangular error integration, a backward error difference for D, output clipping and integral reset on saturation; comments describing trapezoidal integration/back-calculation are inaccurate. All Ki/Kd defaults are zero. LQR combines the three state errors in one model-designed gain vector; the PID cascade passes each stage's output to the next. Their feedback variables and physical output units also differ: LQR uses steering **angle** and emits **rad/s**, while the intended cascade uses steering **rate** and emits **A**. Its heuristic mapping from absolute LQR/pole gains is not an equivalent LQR realization, and current cannot be inserted directly into the velocity-input B without actuator dynamics.
- SOURCE INTEGRATION DEFECTS REMAIN: the current `steer_publisher.py` publishes **`[angle, time.time()]`**, while `cascaded_main_balance._steer_cb` treats element 1 as **steering rate**. This contract mismatch was rechecked when recording. The earlier page also records disconnected optional PID gain scheduling, YAML node-name/default differences, and the missing `new_balance.lpf` dependency. These findings were documented, not repaired by the explanation page or this progress update; the page retains detailed evidence and variant-specific limitations.

## 31. What the nominal 200 Hz rates mean in each motor path (2026-10-03 → 04)

- RAW C2 SENDER: current [`deploy/experiments/steering_sine_position.py`](deploy/experiments/steering_sine_position.py) fixes **`RATE_HZ=200.0`**. After its one startup A3 read, each iteration sends one C2 frame and sleeps for the remainder of **5 ms measured from that iteration's start**. There is no cumulative deadline to recover sleep overshoot; scheduling/send overhead can lower the average rate. The **197.169944 Hz** observed in §28 is compatible with this nominal schedule, not an exact 5 ms transmission guarantee. Sine period controls the trajectory frequency independently of the packet rate.
- FEEDBACK RECORDER: [`motor_feedback.py`](deploy/experiments/motor_feedback.py) defaults to **`--rate 200` cycles/s per selected motor**, with both motors selected by default. Each cycle collects **A3, A2, A1 sequentially for each motor**; therefore the intended steady read-query traffic is **600 queries/s per motor, 1,200 for both**, plus B0 initialization/retries and replies. Its cumulative deadline advances by **5 ms** and resets after an overrun without a catch-up burst. The default **2 ms per-query timeout** is a maximum wait, not a fixed delay; slow/missing replies can make a cycle exceed 5 ms. Passive C2 capture uses a separate receive thread and is **event-driven**, not resampled onto the 200 Hz feedback cycle. The original CSV's rear feedback-row rate was **199.995141 Hz**; steering A3/A2/A1 rates and timestamp limitations are already recorded in §28.
- ROS C2 NODE: [`motor_io_position.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_io_position.py) declares **`control_rate_hz=200.0`**, matching [`params.yaml`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/config/params.yaml), and constructs `create_timer(1/control_rate_hz, _control_tick)`. An eligible tick calls `_send_position` **once even if the target is unchanged**; it does not wait for a new 50 Hz policy message before repeating C2. Powered transmission requires enable, fresh commands/feedback, initialized A3 reference, confirmed position-mode limits, no fault and `dry_run=false`. Otherwise there is no active C2 stream. Polling rate and policy rate are separate parameters/functions. This establishes the default configured send frequency (**200 Hz / 0.005 s**), not a measured ROS/CAN execution rate or motor firmware-loop frequency; overrides, callback work, scheduling and CAN send delays can change observed timing.

## 32. MIT CAN command format, control law, and supplied-driver encoding defect (2026-10-04)

- SOURCE / SCOPE: read the local [CAN protocol V3.09b0](<tsinghua_bike_project/assign_2_Hardwares/assign_2_Hardwares/3.RearSteer Servo【GIM3510-8】/CAN Protocol Doc/自定义CAN通信协议_3.09b0.pdf>), especially **pp.20–22** for MIT and **pp.13–14, 23–25** for C0–C3, including the graphical control diagrams. MIT is a different command format/control mode over the same standard CAN transport. No MIT command was sent to hardware and deployment was not switched to MIT.
- FRAME / MODE SELECTION: one standard **11-bit CAN data frame**, **DLC=8**, with ID bit 10 set: **`0x400 | device_address`** or **`0x500 | device_address`**. For motor address 2 these are **0x402 / 0x502**. The eight-byte payload has **no opcode byte**. Receipt immediately switches the driver into MIT mode and executes the command; **CF** disables output/exits MIT, and **B1** is the separate origin-setting command. F0 configures/reads encoding ranges, and F1 reads MIT feedback; neither is the eight-byte motion command.
- FIVE FIELDS / ENCODING: the payload contains **target position p (16 bits, rad), target velocity v (12 bits, rad/s), position gain Kp (12 bits, 0–500), velocity gain Kd (12 bits, 0–5), and feed-forward torque t (12 bits, N·m)**. Position codes **0–65535** map linearly to **−Pos_Max…+Pos_Max**; velocity and torque codes **0–4095** map to their symmetric ranges. Gains instead map **0–4095 to 0–500 / 0–5**. These are unsigned scaled codes, not float32 values or two's-complement signed fields. The documented F0 defaults are **Pos_Max=95.5 rad, Vel_Max=45 rad/s, T_Max=18 N·m**; actual persistent motor settings must be read rather than assumed. F0 stores them as little-endian uint16 values with units **0.1 rad / 0.01 rad/s / 0.01 N·m**, and settings survive power loss. These encoding ranges alone do not establish the motor's mechanical capability or the total PD-plus-feed-forward torque limit.
- EXACT PACKING: MIT motion fields are **high-bit-first**, an exception to the general little-endian C-command format. With p/v/kp/kd/t denoting encoded unsigned values, `{a,b}` concatenating two nibbles, the payload is:

```text
Data[0] = p[15:8]             Data[1] = p[7:0]
Data[2] = v[11:4]             Data[3] = {v[3:0], kp[11:8]}
Data[4] = kp[7:0]             Data[5] = kd[11:4]
Data[6] = {kd[3:0], t[11:8]}  Data[7] = t[7:0]
```

- CHECKED NUMERICAL EXAMPLE: assuming the documented default ranges and truncating the linear encodings, **p=1 rad, v=0 rad/s, Kp=10, Kd=0.2, t=0 N·m** gives raw codes **33110 / 2047 / 81 / 163 / 2047** and illustrative frame **`502#81567FF0510A37FF`**, DLC=8. Quantization means decoding yields approximately **0.998207 rad / −0.010989 rad/s / 9.890110 / 0.199023 / −0.004396 N·m**. This example was checked offline and was not transmitted. Zero physical position/velocity/torque is near the middle of its code range; all-zero payload bytes would not request all-zero physical targets.
- CONTROL LAW / MAIN DIFFERENCE: the p.20 diagram forms **`torque_ref = Kp*(p_des-p_measured) + Kd*(v_des-v_measured) + torque_ff`**, then converts the torque request into a Q-axis current reference. Kp supplies stiffness, Kd supplies velocity-error feedback/damping, and torque_ff adds feed-forward torque; zero Kp/Kd gives intended torque-only control. **C0** sends a single signed current target (**0.001 A/count**), **C1** a single speed target (**0.01 rpm/count**) through speed PI/current control, and **C2/C3** an absolute/relative position target (**16,384 counts/revolution**) through cascaded position PI → speed PI → current control. C0–C3 each use **opcode + little-endian int32, DLC=5**. MIT supplies position, velocity, both gains and torque together on every command and directly combines position/velocity errors into torque; its depicted outer law has no integral term. It is not equivalent to changing C2's opcode or copying B6–B9 PI gains into MIT Kp/Kd. C3's precise repeated-command relative reference remains undocumented as recorded in §27.
- FEEDBACK: a motion-command reply has F1's **seven-byte** format: F1 opcode, **16-bit position**, **12-bit velocity**, **12-bit torque**, and status byte. Status bit 0 indicates MIT mode and bit 1 a system fault. It reports motor state separately from the five desired command fields; no motor acquisition timestamp is specified in this payload. Torque interpretation requires a correctly configured motor torque constant.
- CONFIRMED PYTHON HELPER BUG — **UNFIXED**: [`GIM_Motor_Driver/motor_driver_python/motor.py`](<tsinghua_bike_project/assign_2_Hardwares/assign_2_Hardwares/3.RearSteer Servo【GIM3510-8】/GIM_Motor_Driver/motor_driver_python/motor.py>) lines **913–914** pass Kp and Kd through `_mit_encode`, whose lines **962–967** implement symmetric **−max…+max** scaling. The protocol requires nonnegative gain scaling instead. Requested **Kp=0 / Kd=0** each produce raw code **2047**, decoded by the documented protocol as **Kp=249.938949939 / Kd=2.499389499**, rather than zero. Thus using this helper for intended torque-only control can introduce substantial position/velocity feedback. The numerical mismatch was reproduced offline by extracting and evaluating the actual pure encoder function; no CAN/ROS initialization was needed. Correct gain encoding must map zero to zero and the gain maximum to 4095 separately from symmetric position/velocity/torque encoding. This is a source/protocol defect finding, not a measured firmware response; the helper was not modified in this task.

---

## 33. MIT steering experiment tools, recorder audits, and integrated 50 Hz control (2026-10-04 → 05; recorded 2026-10-06)

- IMPLEMENTED / HARDWARE EVIDENCE: [`steering_sine_mit.py`](deploy/experiments/steering_sine_mit.py) sends an absolute native-motor-position sine on **0x502**, with requested velocity/feed-forward torque **0**, Kp **10 N·m/motor-rad**, and Kd **0.2 N·m·s/motor-rad**. The user subsequently confirmed this sender **effective and functional on the bike**; §32's earlier statement that no MIT hardware command had been sent describes that earlier protocol review, not the later experiment. This confirmation is qualitative: no MIT-versus-C2 bandwidth, delay, torque-smoothness, or unsupported balancing result is established here. The standalone sender remains nominally **200 Hz**, default amplitude **1 joint rad**, period **10 s**, sign **−1**, gear ratio **1**, and needs no ROS/build.
- ABSOLUTE REFERENCE / PREFLIGHT: the current sender performs **CF/disabled acknowledgement → B1/origin acknowledgement → F0 read/write/exact acknowledgement/independent read-back → A3 zero check → stationary, inactive, fault-free F1 check**, then motion. Every powered execution sets persistent F0 **Pos_Max=3 native rad**, preserving the motor's actual velocity/torque encoding bytes; B1 also changes the persistent origin. The verified first F1 position defines the native sine centre: **`p_native = p_zero + encoder_sign*gear_ratio*A*sin(2*pi*t/period)`**. The native centre itself is never sign-inverted or gear-scaled. A3 independently verifies single-/multi-turn zero within **2 counts**; F1 position/speed tolerances include encoding quantization. The full waveform must fit its confirmed range; no guessed F0 defaults or silent clipping. Physical straight-ahead alignment remains the operator's responsibility. The standalone sender has no continuous feedback watchdog and only attempts exit CF without awaiting its shutdown acknowledgement; it was retained separately from the safer integrated tool.
- UNITS / QUANTIZATION: gains use the correct **nonnegative** encoding rather than the vendor helper's symmetric-gain defect in §32. Nearest encoding of requested zero v/torque gives code **2048**; with actual ranges ±45 rad/s and ±18 N·m this decodes to **+0.010989 rad/s / +0.004396 N·m**, and requested gains decode to **10.012210 / 0.200244**. At ±3 rad, the position increment is **0.00009155 rad (~0.005246°)**. These are encoding residuals, not intentionally nonzero target requests. Neither F0 nor these scripts establishes a total internal PD-torque safety cap.
- READ-ONLY RECORDER: [`motor_feedback_mit.py`](deploy/experiments/motor_feedback_mit.py) automatically creates a timestamped CSV without an output flag; `--output` selects a path and existing files are refused. It sends only A1/A2/A3/B0/F0/F1 reads, targeting **200 A3/A2/A1 cycles/s per selected motor**; it never enables/disables, resets origins, or writes configuration, even on shutdown. Passive capture includes MIT targets, F1, F0 requests/replies, CF requests/status, AE, B1 reference epochs, read queries/replies, and raw other selected-motor frames. Unknown ranges/measurements remain **NaN**, not assumed defaults or cached samples. A3 and F1 zeros are independent; native MIT coordinates remain available. Timestamps are host receipts, not motor acquisition times, and F1 has no transaction identifier.
- RECORDER AUDIT / FIXES: [strict audit](deploy/experiments/MOTOR_FEEDBACK_MIT_AUDIT.md) records the corrected Linux filter mask **0xC00007FF** (the old ERR-masked **0xE00007FF** selects the wrong receive-list path), broadcast/public capture, exact reply lengths, local-TX F0-write detection, bounded stale drains/CSV backlog/shutdown capture, and receive-overflow detection. The decoder was exercised with **all 256 opcode bytes at payload lengths 1–8, plus empty frames**. Compatibility with the user-confirmed sender now pauses reads from observed startup CF/B1 through its first MIT target, verifies all F0 fields before decoding, clears old-range F1 zeros, captures pre-motion A3/F1 zeros, and discards samples crossing reference/configuration epochs. Historical audit results: **59 tests passed** (38 recorder/21 sender), later **72 passed** (43 recorder/29 sender). An already in-flight query cannot be cancelled; absent protocol request IDs still limits reply attribution.
- INTEGRATION: [`steering_sine_mit_io.py`](deploy/experiments/steering_sine_mit_io.py) combines steering-only MIT control at fixed nominal **50 Hz** with automatic CSV capture, one CAN owner, asynchronous extended reads at nominal **200 Hz**, and a bounded CSV-writer thread. `--feedback mit` uses automatic F1 only during motion; `--include-drive` adds read-only rear feedback, never rear actuation/reset/disable. It preserves verified startup, absolute references and zero v/feed-forward requests; missing F1 beyond **0.25 s**, selected-motor command interference, reported overflow, logger failure/backlog, mode/fault or origin/range changes stop the experiment. Shutdown attempts steering CF and awaits disabled-mode confirmation for up to **0.25 s**, without claiming a successful send proves disable. Sparse rows retain separate command/A3/A2/A1/F1 times, requested versus encoded fields, raw traffic, query outcomes and events. Existing C2 plotting tools do not automatically understand this schema. [Usage and limitations](deploy/experiments/STEERING_SINE_MIT_IO.md).
- CURRENT DISK VALIDATION / UNRESOLVED MISMATCH (2026-10-06): sender **29/29** and recorder **43/43** tests pass again. Combined experiment discovery runs **101 tests: 99 pass, 2 fail**, both in the integrated tool's tests: `test_defaults_fixed_50hz_and_extended_200hz` and `test_payload_and_native_origin_match_proven_sender`. Current integrated `--period` default is **1.0 s**, while its documentation and these assertions still assume **10 s**; the second failure reaches a sine-value assertion after the packing comparisons pass. Current amplitude remains **1 rad**, so a bare integrated run requests **1 Hz**, not the documented 0.1 Hz. This configuration/documentation/test mismatch is recorded, **not fixed by this documentation task**; specify `--period` explicitly for reproducible tests. No quantitative MIT performance analysis was run here.

## 34. ROS deployment alternative: MIT steering plus sign-verified C0 rear wheel (2026-10-05; recorded 2026-10-06)

- IMPLEMENTED: [`motor_mit_io.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_mit_io.py), shared pure [`mit_protocol.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/mit_protocol.py), the `motor_mit_io` package executable, regression tests and [node guide](deploy/docs/MOTOR_MIT_IO.md). The user explicitly selected **MIT steering + existing C0 rear wheel**, not rear MIT: rear MIT torque direction has not been established. One SocketCAN-owning worker handles preflight, replies, polling and scheduling; it does not start the base node's independent CAN pollers. Steering position/zero-velocity/zero-feed-forward packets run nominally **50 Hz**, native Kp/Kd **10/0.2**; rear C0 and safety checks remain nominally **200 Hz**, with unchanged **`clip(request − 0.05*rear_velocity, ±6.28 N·m)`**. Both A3/A2 pairs target **200 Hz**, steering A1 **50 Hz**; rear C0 replies supply current feedback. Missed scheduling deadlines produce no catch-up burst. Firmware internal PD frequency remains undocumented; actual Pi/CAN rates need measurement.
- SIGNS / REFERENCES / LIMITS: steering feedback and torque signs remain **−1/−1**, rear **+1/−1**, gears **1**, motor addresses **2/1**; policy-positive steering is right, native-positive steering is left. Powered startup disables both motors, sends steering B1 **once per process**, writes/verifies ±3-rad F0 position encoding while preserving velocity/torque ranges, then verifies A3/F1/B0. RC down/up never repeats B1 or changes the latched reference. MIT absolute target is **`F1_zero + steering_encoder_sign*gear_ratio*slewed_policy_target`**; encoded endpoint rounding stays inside the **±90° joint target** range. Wrapped A3 position still feeds the policy, but **unwrapped A3** guards steering travel. Target slew remains **23.9 rad/s**, command timeout **80 ms**, paired-feedback/F1 timeout **50 ms**, and ordinary fault-clear centre **±5°**. Both valid B0 constants and fresh pairs gate enable. Speed faults use the existing 1.1× limits (**26.29 rad/s steering / 67.76 rad/s rear**).
- ROS CONTRACT: subscribes to **`/trackstand/motor_command`**, Float64MultiArray **`[enable, steering_target_rad, drive_torque_nm]`**; publishes the same six topics as the other motor nodes: **motor_state, motor_torque, motor_fault, motor_status, motor_control, motor_event**, all under `/trackstand/`. State remains signed/wrapped encoder position and velocity; measured torque remains A1/C0 Q-current × actual B0 Kt × torque sign, **not an independent shaft sensor**. `motor_control_mit_v1` preserves prior field order and appends native/encoded targets, gains, quantization residuals, F1 state/age/torque, actual MIT send intervals, unwrapped steering and disable diagnostics. Host steering PD/current-command fields are **NaN**, because this node does not compute steering torque externally. Interleaved safety/MIT ticks can publish more than 200 control records/s; use actual send flags/intervals, not topic count, to infer actuation rate.
- SELECTION / SCOPE: run **exactly one** of `motor_io`, `motor_io_position`, or `motor_mit_io`; stop standalone selected-motor CAN writers/recorders. The default launch **still selects `motor_io`**. Build/source from `deploy/`, then select `ros2 run trackstand_deploy motor_mit_io --ros-args --params-file source/trackstand_deploy/config/params.yaml -p dry_run:=true` for read-only validation. Dry run performs hardware reads but sends **no CF, B1, F0 writes, MIT or C0**; simulated disable confirmation is not a hardware acknowledgement. Policy choice, training/simulation/model assets, IMU/RC/servos, shared configuration and rear control law were not changed by the later MIT safety-fix task.
- TORQUE-CAP GAP / CONCLUSION: moving the PD calculation into firmware removes the host's delayed encoder-to-steering-current calculation, but **does not by itself prove better balancing or tracking**. The protocol establishes F0 T_Max as an encoding range and does not establish B3's position/speed current limit as a MIT total-PD-torque clamp. Consequently this node **cannot claim enforcement of the old ±5.6 N·m host steering clamp**. Firmware current/torque limiting, internal update rate, actual MIT tracking and supported deployment timing remain hardware validation requirements; no physical limit was invented to fill this evidence gap.

## 35. Urgent MIT motor-I/O audit findings and implemented safety fixes (2026-10-05; rechecked/recorded 2026-10-06)

- BOTH-MOTOR HARDWARE FAULTS: the audited implementation ignored rear-wheel AE/CF fault codes, allowing both active commands to continue after a rear driver fault. Now **either motor's nonzero AE/CF fault code hard-latches and stops both outputs**. Queued frames/faults are drained before new actuation or startup requests; a continuously full **64-frame** receive batch fails closed. Hard driver, MIT mode/F1, origin/range, receive/transport and disable-acknowledgement faults require restart; RC down cannot erase them. The first hard-fault reason survives later disable/shutdown errors.
- CONFIRMED DISABLE / RC RE-ENABLE: previously a successful socket send was treated as disable success, without confirming CF execution; a delayed old inactive response could also falsely trip a later enable. Steering **CF** and rear **C0 zero → CF** now create a pending transition; active MIT/C0 commands remain blocked until **both motors return CF mode=0**, received after their corresponding send. Failure/late acknowledgement beyond **250 ms** hard-latches **`disable_ack_timeout`**. Failed sends retry at **20 ms** intervals; successfully sent CF is not repeatedly flooded while awaiting its initial reply. After timeout, unconfirmed motors continue receiving disable retries, but a late acknowledgement cannot clear the restart-required fault. Shutdown also waits up to **250 ms**, reports unconfirmed disable, and closes the socket even on receive failure. Software still cannot guarantee physical disable after a CAN/power failure.
- QUEUED FEEDBACK AGE / QUERY MATCHING: old queued feedback previously received a fresh dequeue timestamp, potentially hiding a stall. SocketCAN now requires Linux **`SO_TIMESTAMPNS_NEW=64`**, using its 64-bit kernel timespec to preserve host arrival age on the monotonic safety clock. A3/A2 commits require the matching motor/opcode/exact length and arrival within **query-send…deadline**, including startup; the older A3 arrival bounds paired-sample freshness. Missing/malformed/future timestamps, reported RX overflow, excessive backlog, clock steps or uncertain clock conversion stop the node. Clock-sampling uncertainty above **1 ms** is rejected; settle system time synchronization before a powered test. Old F1 cannot refresh the watchdog, and pre-enable inactive F1/CF replies cannot falsely fault a new cycle. Kernel arrival time is **not motor acquisition time**; an older same-opcode reply arriving after a newer identical query still cannot be uniquely identified without protocol sequence numbers.
- STARTUP ZERO / RECOVERY / SLEW: an unsolicited startup A3 could previously latch **10,000 counts** before the verified zero, creating a false steering displacement; only the verified startup A3 now defines zero, with old queues drained first. RC down could previously clear a centred-steering latch in the same tick as an ongoing overspeed; recovery now additionally requires **no current angle/speed violation, fresh feedback, disabled outputs and no pending disable**. Time spent disabled previously allowed the first re-enable step to expand to **0.956 rad**; every enable now starts with at most **23.9/50 = 0.478 rad**, while preserving normal in-cycle slew behavior.
- TELEMETRY: appended **`disable_pending`, `disable_confirmed`, `steering_disable_confirmed`, `drive_disable_confirmed`** without changing existing control-column indices. Status adds **`motors_awaiting_disable`**; events include the transition state. **`outputs_active=true` while disable is pending means potentially still powered/unknown**, not proof of a confirmed disable. Existing `*_disable_send_success` continues to mean transmission only; dry-run confirmation is explicitly simulated.
- VALIDATION / EVIDENCE LIMITS: current MIT-node tests **103 passed, 1 skipped** (**104 cases**); applicable deployment suite **289 passed, 1 skipped**. The skipped test is the real Linux kernel-timestamp check: sandbox socket-option permission blocks it here; it **passed separately outside the sandbox during the prior fix validation**, using a local Unix socket pair and **no CAN interface/motor commands**. Pyflakes is clean. The full suite's already documented **3 failures/51 errors** belong to three script-test modules still referencing missing `deploy/scripts/` paths and were not fixed by the MIT task; they are excluded from the applicable-suite count. Prior isolated colcon build succeeded at **`/tmp/trackstand_mit_fix.9Z4zH8/`**; installed/current node SHA-256 both equal **`84da669e02f7d3f667137c6ca62b9815206c30405e3614c826cfe0ad3df2498a`**. These are protocol, software and local-socket checks, **not real-bike timing, firmware torque-limit, or balancing validation**. The urgent fixes changed only the MIT node, its tests and node guide; this recording changes only `progress_record.md`.

## 36. Cross-project motor-control compatibility refinements (2026-10-06)

- SCOPE / DEDUPLICATION: compared the current registered `Trackstand-Balance-v0` task, all three deployment motor executables, standalone experiment writers, and `new_balance` entry points. Protocol layouts, experiment numbers, nominal rates, the PID feedback-schema mismatch, and MIT startup/safety behavior are already recorded in §§12 and 27–35; this entry adds compatibility conclusions and more specific source defects, not another copy of those results.
- PHYSICAL ACTION SEMANTICS: RL training/deployment request **steering angle + rear torque**; nominal LQR/pole placement requests **steering angular velocity + separately generated rear speed**; cascaded PID requests **steering current + rear speed**. A common motor or CAN transport does not make these controller interfaces interchangeable. **MIT is a command format/control mode, whereas position/velocity/torque describes the requested behavior**: MIT can implement position-PD or intended torque-only control. Replacing C2 with MIT while retaining the angle target preserves the high-level action meaning, but changes actuator dynamics. Reinterpreting the existing policy's angle/torque outputs as velocity commands is invalid; a position-to-velocity outer-loop adapter adds dynamics requiring validation, while a velocity-output policy needs matching training and retraining.
- SIMULATOR / HARDWARE LOOP DISTINCTION: the active task uses IsaacLab **implicit** actuators: the simulator handles PD, rather than a Python host torque loop or a CAN/MIT implementation. Consequently matching numerical Kp/Kd, command rates, or action scales does not prove equality between solver-side PD, sampled host PD/C0, firmware PI/C2, and firmware PD/MIT. MIT gains are native-motor quantities; arbitrary gearing cannot be ignored when comparing them with joint-space training gains. Sources: [task actions](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/trackstand_bike/trackstand_rl_env_cfg.py), [actuators](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/bicycle_cfg.py), and [IsaacLab actuator documentation](https://isaac-sim.github.io/IsaacLab/main/source/api/lab/isaaclab.actuators.html).
- RAW ACTIONS / SATURATION: raw policy outputs are **dimensionless**, scaled by **1.57 rad / 6.28 N·m**. The task docstring's assertion that each raw action is in `[-1,1]` is not enforced by its action terms; default RSL-RL `clip_actions=None` is inherited, and the supplied SKRL configuration says `clip_actions: False`. Scaling constants therefore are not guaranteed output bounds. Deployment additionally bounds steering targets and **first clips the rear torque request**, then applies damping and clips the result again: **`clip(clip(request, ±6.28) − 0.05*rear_velocity, ±6.28)`**, in N·m. This is more precise than the shortened rear-law descriptions above; the unclipped simulation action mapping does not itself enforce that same pre-damping request bound. Sources: `contracts.py:scale_policy_action` and `motor_io.py:_command_callback/_control_tick`; C2/MIT retain the rear request/damping bounds.
- CROSS-PROJECT CAN ASSIGNMENTS: current [deployment YAML](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/config/params.yaml) selects **steering TX/RX 0x102/0x002, drive 0x101/0x001**; current [new_balance YAML](tsinghua_bike_project/source/new_balance/config/params.yaml) selects the opposite steering/drive addresses. Motor identity must not be inferred from the same numeric address across these configurations. The `new_main_balance.py` roll-reference variant also is **not registered as a console executable** in current `new_balance/setup.py`; `balance_node` selects `main_balance.py`. These are source/configuration findings, not an independent physical identification of the motors.
- OPTIONAL PID GAIN SCHEDULING — MORE SPECIFIC DEFECT: with `pid_gain_scheduling=true`, [cascaded_main_balance.py](tsinghua_bike_project/source/new_balance/new_balance/cascaded_main_balance.py) updates the bicycle model but calls **`self._ctrl.update_gain(wc=self._pole_wc)`**, without copying the new matrices into the controller. The imported [controller](<tsinghua_bike_project/assign4&5_bike_mujocosim_with_control/assign4&5_bike_mujocosim_with_control/nominal_bike_control/controller.py>) stores matrix copies, so pole-mode gains remain based on the construction-time model. In **LQR mode**, the supplied `wc` replaces the stored gain-options dictionary, discarding Qc/Rc; the LQR branch then raises **`'Qc' and 'Rc' are required for lqr.`** This refines §30's disconnected-scheduling finding. Scheduling is false by default; the defect is source-traced, not a recorded hardware failure. Current `main_balance.py` instead uses `update_system_and_gain(self._bike.sys)` and implements 200/50 Hz scheduling despite contrary YAML comments. No controller fix was made by this recording.

## 37. Recommended motor-control architecture for Trackstand-Balance-v0 (2026-10-06)

- RECOMMENDATION — **NEXT VALIDATION CANDIDATE, NOT A PROVEN WINNER**: prefer **MIT position-PD steering + C0 torque/current rear drive** for the existing position/effort policy. This is consistent with the already selected implementation in §34. Steering MIT makes the position/velocity-to-torque calculation local to the motor and allows explicitly requested gains, unlike relying on the uncharacterized C2 PI settings. Retain the training-style steering request of position with zero desired velocity and zero feed-forward torque; equal numerical gains alone do not establish equal physical response.
- EVIDENCE / LIMIT: §29's small-amplitude C2 attenuation/phase lag motivates prioritizing MIT characterization, but does **not** establish MIT superiority. §33 already records the user's qualitative confirmation that the MIT sine sender worked on the bike; no matched quantitative MIT-versus-C2 bandwidth, loaded tracking, or unsupported policy-balancing result was found in the reviewed experiment logs/reports. Do not confuse this evidence limit with a claim that MIT has never been exercised on hardware. The unresolved MIT total-torque-cap and firmware-rate gaps in §34 still apply.
- DRIVE CHOICE: retain C0 because it preserves the trained **torque** interface, measured-B0 torque-to-current conversion, host damping and bounds. C1 speed or C2 position control adds a different regulation objective and is not a drop-in interpretation of the rear torque action. Intended MIT torque-only control could instead use **Kp=Kd=0** and feed-forward torque, with damping/bounds accounted for, but no demonstrated benefit over the existing C0 path was established. Rear MIT torque direction remains unverified (§34), and the supplied vendor helper must not be used for zero-gain control without correcting §32's gain-encoding defect. No rear MIT implementation or hardware change was authorized by this recommendation.
- REQUIRED BEFORE BALANCING: independently establish actual MIT current/total-torque limiting, then compare supported, supervised C2/MIT response with matched gains/settings where applicable, **50 Hz held targets**, realistic small-angle demands, loaded steering and disable/re-enable transitions. These are proposed checks, not completed experiments or permission for unsupported balancing. Model measured response/delay/limits in simulation and retrain if the effective actuator differs materially from the trained one. The decisive criterion is actuator behavior compatible with training, not the command-mode label. This update changes only `progress_record.md`; no runtime, policy, model, motor configuration or original experiment data was changed, and no new hardware/performance test was run.

## 40. Why the independent MIT file is longer: quantified backend comparison (2026-10-06)

- COMPARISON SNAPSHOT: at the completed review, MIT/C0/C2 files contained **1,031 / 675 / 465 physical lines**. C2's 465-line file is not a self-contained equivalent: it additionally uses **403 function-span lines** of `MotorIo` methods, including parent constructor/parameter setup called via `super()`. Counts below include complete function definitions, internal comments/blank lines, and C2's used parent implementations counted once; they exclude imports, constants, dataclass/class-field declarations and inter-function separators. They are source-size metrics, not runtime-cost measurements. Total function spans were **848 MIT / 557 C0 / 349 C2-own / 752 C2 including used parent methods**.

| Function group | MIT | C0 | C2 including used parent methods |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| CAN transport, feedback, queries and scheduling | 293 | 134 | 134 |
| Dedicated preflight / firmware-limit configuration | 59 | 0 | 49 |
| Fault helper and disable state machine | 57 | 25 | 25 |
| Control tick, including diagnostic assembly | 131 | 119 | 150 |
| Command encoding and sending | 57 | 32 | 122 |
| Control/event/state/status publication | 97 | 87 | 93 |
| Initialization, ROS interfaces, commands and snapshots | 133 | 140 | 153 |
| Entrypoint and node destruction | 21 | 20 | 26 |

- LARGEST CONTRIBUTOR: CAN/feedback/query/scheduling has **159 more function-span lines** than either legacy path. MIT's custom `SocketCan` methods account for **75**, `_consume` **107**, `_send` **4**, `_drain_received` **8**, `_query` **19**, `_schedule_polls` **30**, `_run_io` **50** — **293 total**. This covers kernel timestamp/queue-age conversion, overflow/frame validation, routing encoder/current/F0/F1 replies and AE/CF faults, query deadlines, dispatch and shutdown confirmation. C0/C2 delegate transport to `python-can` and use simpler legacy polling/listening; their library implementation is outside these file counts. Extra checks are this implementation's choices, not proof that every MIT controller inherently requires 1,031 lines.
- OTHER DIFFERENCES / CONCLUSION: MIT's dedicated **59-line startup** verifies CF disable, B1 origin, F0 settings, A3/F1 consistency and B0. C0's zero/B0 acquisition lives inside `_poll_motor`, hence **0 dedicated-preflight lines does not mean no startup checks**; C2's 49 includes `position_mode_limits` and B2/B3 confirmation. MIT's larger disable helper tracks both CF acknowledgements, partial failures, retries and restart-required timeouts, unlike legacy socket-send-only confirmation. Its control tick is only **12 lines longer than C0 and 19 shorter than C2**; the standalone MIT PD computation is not the main reason for file length. Stronger transport/confirmation behavior plus self-contained infrastructure explain the size. Splitting transport into a **MIT-specific** module could shorten the node while preserving independence, but mostly relocates code rather than removing functionality. This comparison was read-only; no further simplification or safety-check removal was implemented.

The original five-field prefix is preserved. Each channel caches signed measured **A1/C0 Q-axis current × measured B0 torque constant**, in N·m; these are current-derived estimates, not an independent shaft-torque measurement or commanded torque. `fresh_torque_feedback_nm` returns the cached estimate only for a positive timestamp with age in **[0, feedback_timeout_s]**; missing/stale/future samples produce **NaN**, not a fabricated zero, without blocking an otherwise fresh encoder-state publication. MIT uses kernel receive time for the torque cache; legacy reception uses host monotonic receipt time. The standalone `/trackstand/motor_torque` publisher is absent from these current backend interfaces, so earlier topic descriptions/bag instructions referring to it describe the previous contract. A3/A2 still determine state freshness; torque arrivals do not refresh stale encoder pairs. `policy_controller.py::_motor_callback` continues consuming **only the first four fields**, so appended torque/NaN telemetry is not added to the 13 policy observations.

## 42. MIT naming, ROS recording, and repository-state clarifications (2026-10-06)

- MIT NAME / DOCUMENT LOCATION: **MIT = Massachusetts Institute of Technology (麻省理工学院)**; “MIT control/mode” names a motor-control style associated with MIT robotics, not a separate expanded algorithm acronym like PID. [CubeMars's official FAQ](https://www.cubemars.com/faq) explains that naming origin. The local [CAN protocol Rev.3.09b0](<tsinghua_bike_project/assign_2_Hardwares/assign_2_Hardwares/3.RearSteer Servo【GIM3510-8】/CAN Protocol Doc/自定义CAN通信协议_3.09b0.pdf>) explicitly uses **“运控模式：MIT类型协议控制方式” on page 20**, but does not spell out the institution's name; pp.20–22 describe the mode. Packet fields and the control law are already recorded in §32 and are not repeated here. The external naming source is not evidence of identical firmware behavior across manufacturers.
- PUBLISHING IS NOT RECORDING: reread the working-tree [`motor_mit_io.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_mit_io.py). It **does not write rosbags/CSV or start a recorder**; saving its ROS telemetry requires a separate **`ros2 bag record`** process. Following §41's consolidation, its five published topics are `/trackstand/motor_state`, `/trackstand/motor_control`, `/trackstand/motor_event`, `/trackstand/motor_fault`, and `/trackstand/motor_status`; it subscribes to `/trackstand/motor_command`. Record the input command alongside state/control/events/status to compare requested targets with encoded outgoing commands. The six-topic descriptions in §§34 and 39 describe the earlier interface, not the current one. No recording process or motor node was started by this clarification.
- GIT BRANCH STATE: an earlier read-only check found **`lmbike_4090`**, with no local `main`, and suggested **`git branch -m main`**; that command was **not executed in this chat**. The fresh read-only check during this recording now finds **current/only local branch `main`**, no configured upstream for it, one worktree at **`/home/lmbike/trackstand_rl`**, and HEAD **`2cd6ab7ff54a5fc7552ba9b758483f56a0b9962f`**. This establishes the current state, not who performed the rename or how; no remote/default-branch change is inferred or performed. Local branch renaming does not itself change commits or working-tree files.

## 44. Rear-wheel control audit: Isaac Lab, C0, and MIT feed-forward-only (2026-10-07)

- SCOPE / DEDUPLICATION: traced the registered **`Trackstand-Balance-v0`** rear action through the active task/actuator configuration, local Isaac Lab action/implicit-actuator/PhysX submission code, deployment `contracts.py`, `policy_controller.py`, `motor_io.py`, `motor_mit_io.py`, `can_protocol.py` and `mit_protocol.py`; checked the local CAN PDF and existing experiment reports/data. §§23–24 and 32–37 already record the rear direction warning, loop distinction, MIT packet format, exact deployment double-clipping law and architecture recommendation. This entry adds the feed-forward-only comparison, quantified command-resolution tradeoff, independent simulation-data recheck and fresh focused validation. **No rear MIT implementation or hardware change was made.**
- DAMPING IS PART OF THE TRAINING INTERFACE: for dimensionless raw rear action **`a_r`**, scaled policy request is **`tau_policy = 6.28*a_r` N·m**, without an enforced raw `[-1,1]` bound. The nominal Isaac rear law is **`tau_policy - 0.05*rear_velocity`**, before solver effects; rear stiffness is zero, but damping is not. Current policy/physics rates are **50/400 Hz**; opt-in gain DR scales damping by **U(0.9,1.1)**. Deployment C0 instead requests **`clip(clip(tau_policy, ±6.28) - 0.05*measured_rear_velocity, ±6.28)`** at nominal **200 Hz**, then sends **`I_q = torque_sign*tau_final/B0_Kt`**, with configured rear torque sign **−1**. Sending only the raw policy torque through MIT with **Kp=Kd=0** omits this added damping; sending the same host-damped/bounded final torque through MIT would preserve the nominal request law, subject to encoding and firmware differences. Illustrative, **not measured**, request **2 N·m** at **10 rad/s** gives **1.5 N·m** with nominal damping versus **2 N·m** for raw MIT feed-forward-only. Pre-damping clipping also matters: request **12.56 N·m** at **10 rad/s** produces **5.78 N·m** in deployment versus **6.28 N·m** for a single clip of the combined nominal torque. Equal coefficients do not establish equal response: Isaac damping is implicit, deployment uses delayed sampled CAN feedback, and `0.05` is not a measurement of the real mechanism's natural friction. Sources: [task](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/trackstand_bike/trackstand_rl_env_cfg.py), [actuators](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/bicycle_cfg.py), [C0 backend](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_io.py).
- WHAT MIT ZERO GAINS DO / CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION LIMIT: the [CAN Rev.3.09b0 PDF](<tsinghua_bike_project/assign_2_Hardwares/assign_2_Hardwares/3.RearSteer Servo【GIM3510-8】/CAN Protocol Doc/自定义CAN通信协议_3.09b0.pdf>) **p.20 diagram** reduces `Kp*(p_des-p) + Kd*(v_des-v) + torque_ff` to **`torque_ref = torque_ff`** when both gains are zero, then converts torque to a desired Q-axis current internally. **P.13** specifies C0 current in **0.001 A** units and `torque = I_q*Kt`; C0 performs the torque-to-current conversion on the host. With equivalent requests and correctly configured constants/signs, these aim for the same output; zero-gain MIT has **no motor-local position/velocity feedback advantage**, although the overall policy remains feedback control. Firmware current-loop identity, bandwidth, ramping and physical torque accuracy are not proven equal. Current [`motor_mit_io.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_mit_io.py) still controls **rear via C0**, using MIT only for steering. Current `mit_motion_payload()` hardcodes velocity/feed-forward torque requests to **zero**; changing its steering gain parameters to zero would not implement rear MIT torque control. Correct unsigned zero-gain encoding is separate from §32's unfixed vendor-helper defect.
- REAR TORQUE COMMAND RESOLUTION — **NUMERICAL ENCODING, NOT PHYSICAL ACCURACY**: with the historical measured rear **B0 Kt≈0.30 N·m/A** from §23, C0's **0.001 A/count** represents about **0.000300 N·m/count**, with exact encoded zero. MIT has **12 torque bits**, so **`delta_tau = 2*T_Max/4095`**. At the protocol-default **±18 N·m**, the increment is **0.008791209 N·m**, about **29.3× coarser** than C0; at an illustrative **±6.28 N·m**, it is **0.003067155 N·m**, about **10.2× coarser**. The symmetric mapping has no exact zero: nearest decoded values are **±0.004395604 N·m** at ±18 or **±0.001533578 N·m** at ±6.28; deployment's nearest-code encoder chooses code **2048**, the positive native residual. Gains themselves can encode exact zero. These are protocol/decoder results, not observed shaft-torque biases or proof of firmware zero handling. The actual rear F0 range was **not read from hardware** and is not assumed to be ±18; reducing an encoding range limits representable requests, not proof of a mechanical/total-torque safety cap. §23's supported 41050 dry-run requests of only **−0.00522…+0.00357 N·m** show why this resolution can matter near standstill, but are not a complete dynamic-policy demand envelope.
- SIMULATION SATURATION CAVEAT — INDEPENDENT SAVED-DATA RECHECK: local `ImplicitActuator.compute()` clips the **diagnostic approximate** `applied_effort`, but returns the original action unchanged; the PhysX articulation submits that original feed-forward effort buffer. [NVIDIA's tensor API](https://docs.omniverse.nvidia.com/kit/docs/omni_physics/107.3/extensions/runtime/source/omni.physics.tensors/docs/api/python.html#omni.physics.tensors.ArticulationView.set_dof_actuation_forces) also distinguishes these external efforts from implicit PD forces. Reread the existing **2026-10-01 `model_91850`, 64 environments, 3,000 policy steps, seed 42, full-DR** [audit](isaaclab_train/reports/motor_limits_09221945_91850_2026-10-01/README.md) and independently checked its NPZ arrays: policy shape **3,000×64×2**, physics shape **3,000×8×64×2**; rear requests exceeded **6.28+1e-5 N·m** on **171/192,000 policy samples (0.0890625%)** and the submitted buffer on **1,368/1,536,000 physics samples**, reaching **132.002426 N·m**. The clipped diagnostic estimate peaked at **6.280000210 N·m**. Submitted rear effort equals the held unbounded request with maximum difference **0**; reconstructed damping law and diagnostic clipping errors are also **0** at stored precision. All **171 excessive requests** coincide with the **171 episode-start actions**; after those starts, maximum absolute rear request is **2.765089750 N·m**. Recorded rear damping spans **0.045300215…0.054935809 N·m·s/rad**. Original [`motor_limit_samples.npz`](isaaclab_train/reports/motor_limits_09221945_91850_2026-10-01/motor_limit_samples.npz) SHA-256 is **`f47602b0491f43ae642725ee455665775d94e369650098fccf22eb33476d35d9`**. These results reproduce existing data, **not a new simulation run**, and do not measure total solver torque or diagnose the startup burst. The clipped torque plot therefore cannot establish a hardware-equivalent ±6.28 N·m total limit; deployment's outgoing request is explicitly bounded, and actuator saturation alignment is a transfer issue independent of choosing C0 or MIT.
- FIRMWARE / REAL-BIKE EVIDENCE GAPS: the PDF **pp.9–10** documents **B4 current-output slew** for Q-axis current control, with units **0.001 A/s**. The audited deployment nodes neither read nor configure B4; its actual setting and whether MIT uses an equivalent path remain unknown. Current-derived feedback is not a calibrated shaft-torque measurement. §23's tiny rear movement/opposite expected policy-coordinate direction remains an unresolved, coupled-test warning; neither changing protocols nor the existing steering-only MIT sine confirmation proves it resolved. No matching isolated rear C0-versus-MIT torque-response experiment was found in the reviewed records, and firmware update rate, actual current/torque limits, friction/breakaway and loaded response are not established by this audit.
- RECOMMENDATION — **BASELINE, NOT PROVEN HARDWARE SUPERIORITY**: retain **C0 rear torque/current with existing damping/bounds** for the current policy: it preserves the implemented interface and has finer command resolution, while **no demonstrated benefit of MIT feed-forward-only** was found. This refines, rather than replaces, §37. MIT is a candidate if supervised, supported, matched-request/limit/timing tests demonstrate better torque response; sending raw policy torque with zero gains is not training-equivalent because damping is missing. **Kp=0, Kd≈0.05, v_des=0** could separately investigate moving damping inside firmware, but is **not the Kp=Kd=0 case** and requires native-unit, sign, gain-quantization and total-limit verification. Prioritize isolated rear direction/low-torque characterization and explicit simulation/hardware saturation matching; account for real natural friction rather than assuming the code's damping represents it. These are proposed checks, not permission for unsupported balancing or an implemented controller change.
- VALIDATION / CHANGE BOUNDARY: from `deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/`, **`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider test/test_motor_control.py test/test_motor_mit_io.py test/test_can_protocol.py test/test_contracts.py -k 'not real_linux_socket' --tb=short`** passed **184 tests / 1 deselected**, both during the investigation and again during this recording. The deliberately excluded case is the local Unix-socket/kernel-timestamp test, **not a CAN/motor test**; no fresh full-suite or hardware result is inferred. Pure offline encoding calculations and saved-array checks above were rerun successfully during recording. No ROS executor, CAN socket, motor command, new simulator evaluation, build, rosbag or balancing trial was started. **Only `progress_record.md` is changed by this recording**; earlier entries and all pre-existing runtime/test/documentation changes, original logs, arrays, checkpoints and policies are preserved.

---

## 45. Motor-control diagnostics reduced to 50 Hz; state-rate coupling clarified (2026-10-07 → 08)

- IMPLEMENTED AT USER REQUEST: `/trackstand/motor_control` now uses the backend-neutral **`MotorControlPublishGate`**, fixed at **50 Hz** in [`telemetry.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/telemetry.py). C0 and independent MIT emitters use it; C2 inherits the C0 emitter. The first sample is immediate, subsequent samples retain a **20 ms phase**, and missed slots are skipped without catch-up bursts. Numeric message construction/serialization/publication is gated, not motor commands or safety processing. No new ROS parameter was added. Runtime files: `telemetry.py`, `motor_io.py`, `motor_mit_io.py`; regression coverage includes new [`test_motor_control_rate.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/test/test_motor_control_rate.py) and updated backend tests. Deployment README and telemetry/backend/diagnostic-test guides were updated. This supersedes earlier descriptions of control diagnostics publishing every 200 Hz tick.
- PRESERVED / COUPLING: rear C0 commands, CAN polling and safety/control remain nominal **200 Hz**; MIT steering remains nominal **50 Hz**. Fault/disable and `/trackstand/motor_event` processing still runs on every control tick, including unsampled ticks. Field order, schema, gains, limits, watchdogs and policy/kickstand logic were unchanged. Diagnostic `sequence` still counts control ticks: **1, 5, 9, …** is expected at regular 200 Hz, not message loss. `cycle_interval_ms` still measures consecutive motor-loop starts, normally **5 ms**, not the topic's approximately 20 ms interval. Records are sampled ticks, **not averages**; per-tick send flags or brief saturation on intervening ticks need not appear in numeric telemetry. The diagnostic gate is not synchronized with policy inference or MIT sends. See [current telemetry contract](deploy/docs/TELEMETRY.md).
- IMPLEMENTATION-STAGE VALIDATION — HISTORICAL SNAPSHOT: full deployment/experiment checks passed **581 tests / 1 skipped** (**464 + 117**); the focused four-file set passed **279 / 1 skipped**. Pre/post inspection found **53 unchanged motor methods** (**18 C0 / 10 C2 / 25 MIT**), C2 source byte-identical, and unchanged previous constants/schemas. One-second active mocks retained **200 C0-steering + 200 rear**, **200 C2-steering + 200 rear**, or **50 MIT-steering + 200 rear** sends while all three emitted **50 diagnostic records**; faults were checked each tick and stale-command/RC-down/send-failure responses remained immediate on unsampled **5 ms** ticks. Pyflakes/whitespace checks passed; isolated `colcon build --symlink-install` succeeded (**one package, 30.1 s**) under `/tmp/trackstand_telemetry_50hz.RSqrxr/`, leaving repository build/install untouched. These are software/mocked results, not measured Pi rates or CPU savings.
- `/trackstand/motor_state` WAS **NOT** CHANGED TO 50 Hz: its nominal **200 Hz timer** only publishes when both encoder pairs are initialized, fresh within the default **50 ms**, and newer than their last published pair timestamps. Asynchronous replies/scheduling can skip attempts, hence “up to 200 Hz,” not guaranteed exact 200 Hz. Reducing publication alone is a possible future experiment, but **changing `poll_rate_hz` also changes CAN polling**, because it currently determines both rates. A separate publisher gate/timer would be needed to preserve internal polling/control/safety at 200 Hz. Independently scheduled 50 Hz state/policy timers can reuse observations or add roughly **20 ms** age; the policy's motor-message timeout is **60 ms**, and loss of readiness while balancing latches its fault and disables output. Policy consumes the first four state fields; **seven of its 13 observations** depend on encoder position/speed. Low-level safety uses internal CAN snapshots, not this ROS topic. Lower state publication would also downsample measured torque and could hide fast transients. This was a read-only assessment: **no state-rate reduction or real-bike validation was implemented**.

## 46. Eight MIT sine tests versus recorded C2: quantified tracking and safety findings (2026-10-07)

- NEW OFFLINE WORK / CONDITIONS: added [`analyze_mit_sine.py`](deploy/experiments/analyze_mit_sine.py), **13** new numerical regression tests in [`test_mit_sine_analysis.py`](deploy/experiments/tests/test_mit_sine_analysis.py), eight per-condition report bundles, and the [aggregate HTML](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_sine_sweep_20261007/index.html), [report](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_sine_sweep_20261007/README.md), [comparison graph](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_sine_sweep_20261007/mit_c2_comparison.png), [tracking grid](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_sine_sweep_20261007/tracking_comparison.png), [CSV](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_sine_sweep_20261007/comparison.csv), [complete JSON/source hashes](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_sine_sweep_20261007/comparison.json), and [validation](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_sine_sweep_20261007/VALIDATION.md). Input files are under `deploy/experiments/logs/`; all eight are **±1 rad peak**, Kp requested **10**, Kd requested **0.1 or 0.2**, and user confirms steering/front wheel fully free and unconstrained. Per-condition directories use `steering_sine_<date>_<time>_mit_10_<01-or-02>_<frequency>hz1rad`, preserving the previous report workflow: five selectable four-panel SVG views, full PNG/PDF, zoom PNG, three-cycle overlay, HTML, README, summary and analysis JSON. No measured C3 baseline was identified; **C3 performance cannot be quantified from these logs**.
- METHOD / SOURCE VALIDATION: re-decoded **123,803 motion target/A3/A2/A1/F1 rows** out of **335,948 CSV rows**, plus F0/B0 calibration and CF/status payloads. Each sparse feedback row uses its own receipt `elapsed_s`, not cached/interpolated samples. Reject inconsistent units, signs, ranges/references, malformed payloads, raw/CSV disagreements or missing clocks/traces. Exclude the first **max(1 s, three periods)** and last **max(0.25 s, one period)**; fit sine/cosine/DC on each trace's own timestamps. Input gain/phase uses the **encoded zero-order-held target fundamental**, integrated on a **0.1 ms** grid; RMS error uses the latest held command without smoothing or phase alignment. Three subwindows measure within-record variation, not independent-repeat confidence intervals. Recomputed the three comparable **±1 rad C2** recordings; hashes and numerical results match prior reports. All **20 input CSV hashes** and earlier report files were checked unchanged after generation.
- ENCODING / REFERENCES VERIFIED: steering ID **2**, gear ratio **1**, encoder/torque signs **−1**, A3 zero **0**, and independent fixed native F1 zero; MIT target is **absolute native zero + signed sine offset**, not accumulated relative increments. F0 readback is **3 rad / 45 rad/s / 18 N·m**, B0 Kt approximately **0.77999997139 N·m/A**. Encoded Kp is **10.0122100122**, Kd **0.1001221001 / 0.2002442002**. Requested zero velocity/feed-forward torque encode as code **2048**, decoding to **+0.010989011 native rad/s / +0.004395604 native N·m**; quantization residuals are retained, not fabricated as exact zeros. F0 ranges describe encoding, **not a verified total-PD torque cap**.

The MIT source stem is `<stem>.csv`; C2 rows are the existing ±1 rad baselines. Amplitude is the **fitted A3 fundamental**, gain is relative to the **held encoded input**, and torque is the **steady-window absolute peak of A1 current × B0 Kt**, not a shaft sensor. Full precision, other metrics and hashes remain in the linked JSON.

| Mode / source stem | Hz | Requested Kd | A3 amplitude rad | Gain % | Phase lag ° | Held-input lag ms | RMS error rad | Peak speed rad/s | Peak torque N·m | Position R² |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

- TRACKING VERDICT: MIT clearly improves the recorded **1–2 Hz** interface; at 2 Hz RMS error falls **63–77%** versus C2, with gain near unity instead of **66.2%**. No matching 1 Hz C2 result exists. At **5 Hz**, MIT gains **79–82%** versus C2 **34%**; Kd=0.2 reduces RMS to **0.606** versus **0.669 rad**, but Kd=0.1 is slightly worse at **0.681 rad**. At **10 Hz**, MIT produces more motion but **worse total tracking error**, phase over **90°**, and substantially distorted waveforms; amplitude alone is not a successful-tracking criterion. Kd=0.1 improves low-frequency lag but worsens high-frequency error/torque and slightly exceeds unity low-frequency gain. **No deployment gain change was made**; one unloaded run per condition does not justify silently replacing the training's requested Kd=0.2.
- SPEED / TORQUE / TIMING: ideal ±1 rad waves demand peak speeds **31.4 / 62.8 rad/s** at 5/10 Hz; A2 instead plateaus around **20.6 rad/s**. At 5 Hz, |speed| exceeds 20 rad/s on **61.41% / 46.44%** of samples for Kd=0.1/0.2. This supports an effective speed limitation, **not identification of its firmware/current/back-EMF/power cause**. At 10 Hz, current-derived torque exceeds the training **5.6 N·m** reference on **7.805% / 8.722%** of samples; Kd=0.1 at 5 Hz exceeds it on **2.072%**, while Kd=0.2 at 5 Hz does not. These are not calibrated shaft torques or damage evidence. Commands run **49.718–49.728 Hz**, maximum logged lateness **0.335477 ms**, with **zero skipped command ticks**; A3/A2/A1 receipts run approximately **196.8–196.9 Hz**, no trace gaps over **50 ms**, query RTT p99 below **0.89 ms**, and reported socket overflows zero. Host setpoint starvation is a weak explanation for **these** free-wheel tests, not a retroactive dismissal of the old C0-loop findings. MIT at a 50 Hz host interface can track 1–2 Hz motion; these logs still do not reveal the firmware-loop frequency or prove the old external 200 Hz PD loop adequate/inadequate.
- UNRESOLVED SHUTDOWN SAFETY — ALL EIGHT: every run stops with KeyboardInterrupt, sends CF, then logs **`CF sent but disabled mode not confirmed before shutdown deadline`**. Startup CF reports mode **0**; shutdown replies report byte 6 **5**, fault **0**, approximately **0.46–0.69 ms** after CF, while the script waits **250 ms** for mode 0. In source-stem order **1628 / 1632 / 1634 / 1637 / 1639 / 1641 / 1642 / 1643**, shutdown payloads are **`CFE40708002A0500` / `CFE5070100280500` / `CFCE078000280500` / `CFE50700002A0500` / `CFE60702002A0500` / `CFE3070B002C0500` / `CFDB070D002C0500` / `CFE0070700300500`**. Local CAN PDF **p.7** defines modes 0–4, not 5; **p.16** says CF closes motor output and returns AE-format status; **p.22** uses CF to exit MIT. **Neither failed physical disable nor harmless reply/transition semantics is established.** Do not guess that mode 5 is safe or bypass acknowledgement. With independent support and power isolation available, collect subsequent AE/F1 and verify physical free state after CF before trusting deployment disable confirmation. No runtime/firmware fix was implemented by this analysis.
- LIMITATIONS / NEXT STEP: MIT commands were approximately **50 Hz**, C2 approximately **197 Hz**, on different days with unrecorded C2 PI/firmware limits. This is not a strictly matched protocol-only experiment, ground-loaded tracking test, or policy-balancing success. Prioritize CF/AE semantics and actual firmware total-torque/current protection; then matched **50 Hz C2/MIT**, amplitudes **±0.1/0.2 rad**, frequencies **0.5/1/2/5 Hz**, recorded gains/limits/voltage/temperature/status, followed by independently supported ground-loaded steering. Inspect genuine balancing-policy command spectra and model measured tracking/limits in simulation before deciding on policy adaptation. C3 is **not an evidenced cure**. Recommendations are proposed experiments, not authorization for unsupported balancing.
- ANALYSIS VALIDATION: **130 experiment tests passed**, including **29 C2/MIT analysis tests**. Independent correlation agrees within **0.5 ms** with all eight held-input phase-equivalent lags; F1 fundamental gain differs by at most **0.002413** (**0.242 percentage points**) and phase by at most **2.738°**. F1 shares motor/host-clock limitations, so is not independent calibration. Validated **41 SVGs / 26 PNGs / 10 PDFs**, **79 local HTML links**, and **11 comparison rows**; visually checked comparison/tracking plots and a representative 10 Hz/Kd=0.2 zoom. Pyflakes and whitespace checks passed; the pre-existing Matplotlib Axes3D warning does not concern 2-D outputs. Reproduction requires a fresh output directory: `MPLCONFIGDIR=/tmp/trackstand_mit_mpl PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 deploy/experiments/analyze_mit_sine.py --reports /tmp/new_mit_reports`. The offline task added analyzer/tests/reports only; raw CSVs, prior reports, runtime/configuration/policy/simulation were preserved and no CAN/ROS node was started.

## 47. MIT full-amplitude and millisecond-lag follow-ups (2026-10-07 → 08)

- FULL-AMPLITUDE ANSWER: actual encoded target extrema are approximately **−0.999954223 / +0.999954223 rad**. Steady A3 extrema below were checked against raw records and report JSON. At 1–2 Hz the motor nearly reaches full stroke; Kd=0.1 at 2 Hz slightly overshoots, while Kd=0.2 undershoots by approximately **1.2%**. **Neither 5 Hz nor 10 Hz achieves full ±1 rad steady stroke.** Kd=0.1 at 5 Hz does briefly reach **+1.053461306 rad** at **0.086496–0.096925 s**, but this is startup, excluded before the **1.004576747 s** steady window; it is not successful sustained tracking. Actual extrema differ from the fitted fundamental: at 10 Hz/Kd=0.1 peaks are about **±0.60 rad**, but fundamental amplitude only **0.3663 rad** because the waveform is distorted. Reaching an extremum also does not mean arriving at the correct time.
- LAG DEFINITIONS: §46 reports **`phase_lag_deg / (360*frequency_hz) * 1000`** relative to the actual encoded held input. Relative to the ideal continuous requested sine, the report's `lag_against_ideal_command_ms` adds approximately **10.05 ms** from the 50 Hz hold. Both references are provided below to prevent conflating their numbers. These are frequency-specific **phase-equivalent tracking lags on host send/receipt clocks**, not separately measured wire latency, sensor acquisition delay, firmware execution delay, per-target settling time or a universal fixed motor delay. At 10 Hz the poor sine fit particularly limits a single-lag description; C2's smaller 10 Hz phase-equivalent lag does not imply better amplitude tracking.

## 48. Bag inventory: genuine inference exists, but no confirmed closed-loop RL deployment recording (2026-10-07 → 08)

- REPOSITORY-WIDE CHECK: searched current disk, including ignored/hidden files, for MCAP, DB3, ROS1 bags and `metadata.yaml`. Found **41 MCAP files**, **38 metadata files**, and **three vendor ROS1 `robot_localization/test/test1–3.bag` assets**. Of **39 readable MCAP summaries**, only Stage B contains `/trackstand/raw_action` and `/trackstand/observation`. Two older Tsinghua MCAP files — `2026_08_12-07_14_30` and `2026_08_18-02_34_00` under `tsinghua_bike_project/rosbags/` — fail summary reading with **`RecordLengthLimitExceeded`**; no repair or complete classification of those contents is claimed. Older nominal-controller records are not proof of current Trackstand RL deployment. The six root `rosbags/` recordings are classified below using metadata/messages and the previously recorded experiment context.

| Directory under `rosbags/` | Duration s | What was recorded | Genuine policy inference? | Confirmed motor-driven RL balancing? |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| `rosbag2_2026_09_25-08_56_47` | 34.07 | IMU check, **3,396** IMU messages | No | No |
| `rosbag2_2026_09_25-09_24_26` | 40.34 | Corrected-bias IMU check, **4,032** IMU messages | No | No |

- STAGE B DIRECT RECHECK, NOT A NEW DEPLOYMENT TRIAL: CRC-validated MCAP decoding with standard ROS serializers confirms **1,718×2 raw actions**, **1,718×13 observations**, **31,073 motor-command messages**, of which **1,718** enable policy output, **4,750 policy-timing**, **190 `/trackstand/status`**, **20 policy-event** and **870 motor-status** messages. ## 49. Standalone MIT sine dependencies, 200 Hz feasibility, and telemetry responsibilities (2026-10-07 → 09)

- SCOPE / DEDUPLICATION: reread the current record and relevant working-tree sources before appending. §§33 and 38 already describe the integrated experiment, its corrected **1 s** default period and previous validation; §§41–45 cover unified state and diagnostic publication. This entry adds the explicit dependency check, the **200 Hz in-memory experiment**, the explanation of `telemetry.py`, a newer dated deployment-rate snapshot and fresh focused checks. The previous recording request was interrupted before any edit; this recording preserves all earlier entries rather than replacing those historical snapshots.
- STANDALONE DEPENDENCY CHECK / COMPARISON: the complete [`steering_sine_mit.py`](deploy/experiments/steering_sine_mit.py) imports **only Python standard-library modules**; it does not import or launch another experiment script, deployment module, ROS node, `python-can`, or vendor driver. CAN startup, encoding, sine generation and sending are implemented inside that file. It needs an operational Linux SocketCAN interface and connected motor, not `motor_feedback.py` or `steering_sine_mit_io.py`. The integrated [`steering_sine_mit_io.py`](deploy/experiments/steering_sine_mit_io.py) is independently stdlib-only and also does not import `telemetry.py` or the original sender/recorder. At the inspected defaults, sender/integrated **command rates are 200/50 Hz**, and **sine periods are 10/1 s**; both use **±1 joint rad, Kp=10, Kd=0.2**. As detailed in §33, the integrated tool adds motion-time F1 and optional extended feedback, automatic sparse CSV recording, runtime monitoring and CF acknowledgement checks. These are **not a new host PD loop**: feedback is recorded/monitored, not used to alter the sinusoidal target; MIT feedback control remains in motor firmware. B1 sets the present angle as zero, not independently verified straight-ahead alignment. Only one selected-motor command writer should run.
- CHANGING THE INTEGRATED CONSTANT — **ASSESSMENT, NOT AN IMPLEMENTED EDIT**: setting **`COMMAND_RATE_HZ = 200.0`** automatically makes **`COMMAND_DT_S = 0.005`**, because it is derived as `1.0/COMMAND_RATE_HZ`; command scheduling, preview times/counts and lateness calculations use that derived interval. No inherent Python/scheduling error was found from this one-constant change. Sine period/amplitude, gains, startup and nominal extended **200 Hz query cycles** remain unchanged. The period condition automatically becomes **`period > 0.01 s`** rather than >0.04 s, but its error text, help/docstrings and startup/preview banners still hard-code **50 Hz / 25 Hz / 0.04 s** and would become misleading; rate-specific regression assertions also need updating for an actual implementation. **The repository's integrated script remains at 50 Hz**: the user asked whether the hypothetical edit would work, not to implement it. Changing its command rate is independent of the ROS deployment node and the motor's undocumented internal firmware update rate.
- OFFLINE 200 Hz VERIFICATION (2026-10-08; REPEATED 2026-10-09): compiled the actual integrated source **in memory**, changing only the constant's AST value before module execution, and reused existing fake-CAN/virtual-clock fixtures. **Five cases**—normal extended feedback, missing all A3/A2/A1 replies, **30 ms** F1 delay, MIT-only feedback, and read-only drive collection—each produced **200 targets in one virtual second**, all consecutive intervals **0.005 s**, **zero skipped command ticks**, and fake CF disabled acknowledgement. Missing auxiliary replies were logged as timeouts without stopping setpoint scheduling in that fixture. A real CSV writer with fake CAN saved **32 targets in 0.16 virtual seconds** plus shutdown acknowledgement; preview produced **20 targets in 0.1 s** without opening CAN. Period **0.02 s** was accepted and **0.01 s** rejected, while the error message still referred to 50 Hz. **22 other integrated tests passed** against the in-memory 200 Hz module; **seven rate-specific tests were excluded** from that run, with selected timing/preview/CSV cases replaced by the direct checks above—not a claim that the entire unchanged 50 Hz test suite passes at 200 Hz. The preceding 50 Hz baseline passed **29 integrated tests**. Actual Pi/CAN/firmware timing, sustainable CSV throughput, loaded response and safe physical disable remain unverified by these virtual tests; more traffic/logging can cause lateness or trigger existing overflow, writer/backlog or F1-timeout errors. Inspect recorded `command_interval_s`, `command_lateness_s` and skipped ticks instead of assuming exact hardware 5 ms delivery. Fake CF success does **not** resolve §46's real mode-5 shutdown uncertainty.
- WHAT `telemetry.py` DOES: [`telemetry.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/telemetry.py) is a **ROS-independent shared helper**, not a node/controller/recorder. It defines ordered, unit-explicit state/control/policy-timing fields; `telemetry_values()` converts dictionaries to numeric arrays, inserts **NaN** for absent fields and rejects unknown names; `age_ms()` reports monotonic sample age; `fresh_torque_feedback_nm()` returns the measured cache only within its allowed age, never a substitute command. The seven-field state schema and current-derived-torque limitations are already in §§41–43. `MotorSendResult`, `DisableResult` and `send_result_values()` carry transport outcomes, while `motor_disable_reasons()` constructs diagnostic reason strings. The module neither computes steering PD/policy outputs nor performs CAN sends, ROS publication or CSV/rosbag recording. The caller nodes publish its formatted messages. `MotorControlPublishGate` limits **diagnostic construction/publication only**, publishes immediately on its first tick, retains phase and skips missed slots without catch-up bursts; it does not itself set CAN actuation, state publication or safety-processing rates.
- NEWER DEPLOYMENT RATE SNAPSHOT — **OBSERVED, NOT IMPLEMENTED HERE**: during the telemetry explanation and this recording, the gate's default remains **50 Hz** and legacy C0/C2 use it; [`motor_mit_io.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_mit_io.py) now explicitly passes **`MIT_RATE_HZ=200`**. Its source constants/documentation identify **50 Hz policy-originated input**, **100 Hz state/query rounds and state/status/fault publication**, and **200 Hz MIT steering, rear C0, safety and control diagnostics**. [`TELEMETRY.md`](deploy/docs/TELEMETRY.md) agrees. These are nominal host schedules, not measured rates or firmware-loop frequencies; state still requires new/fresh encoder pairs. This later disk state supersedes §45's all-backend 50 Hz diagnostic / 50 Hz MIT-steering / 200 Hz MIT-state snapshot **for MIT only**; C0/C2 retain their separate defaults. It does not change the standalone integrated sine's 50 Hz constant. Authorship, rationale, build/install state or complete hardware validation of these already-present deployment edits is not inferred or claimed.
- FRESH VALIDATION / CONCURRENT WORKSPACE CAVEAT (2026-10-09): `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m unittest discover -s deploy/experiments/tests -p 'test_*sine_mit*.py' -v` passed **58 tests (29 sender + 29 integrated)**. From `deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/`, `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider test/test_motor_control_rate.py test/test_motor_state.py --tb=short` passed **70 tests**. A broader run adding `test/test_policy_timing.py` initially encountered files disappearing from their package paths: **50 passed, 3 failed, 25 errors**, with `FileNotFoundError` for `motor_mit_io.py`/`policy_controller.py`; a read-only status check briefly showed deletions at those paths and files under `deploy/`, then both package paths returned. This is an observed changing-workspace state, not a lasting-move claim or an action taken by this chat. Repeating the three-file check after they returned gave **77 passed / 1 failed**: `test_roll_safety_diagnostics_match_existing_gravity_condition` expected `["roll_or_inversion_safety"]` but received `[]`. That test/source mismatch is **recorded, not diagnosed or fixed here**; no complete deployment-suite certification is claimed. ## 50. Fresh 200 Hz MIT sine targets versus 50 Hz: partial improvement, not recovered motor bandwidth (2026-10-08; recorded 2026-10-09)

- INPUTS / CONDITIONS: analyzed `deploy/experiments/logs/202610081312_200Hz_10_01_2Hz.csv`, `202610081313_200Hz_10_01_5Hz.csv`, and `202610081314_200Hz_10_01_10Hz.csv`, matched to §46's `202610071639_mit_10_01_2Hz.csv`, `202610071641_mit_10_01_5Hz.csv`, and `202610071642_mit_10_01_10Hz.csv`. All request **±1 rad, Kp=10, Kd=0.1**; the user confirms fully free steering, the same power supply, and unchanged firmware speed/current limits. These are one unloaded recording per condition on different days, not a policy-balancing test or controlled repeat-trial study. Requested gains decode to **10.0122100122 / 0.1001221001** in all six; raw F0/B0/IDs/signs/references were checked, including the older 2 Hz run's distinct fixed F1 zero. The six source hashes were rechecked against the saved comparison during this recording and match.
- ARTIFACTS / WORKFLOW: generated the [aggregate report](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_rate_comparison_20261008/README.md), [HTML index](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_rate_comparison_20261008/index.html), [rate graph](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_rate_comparison_20261008/rate_comparison.png), [raw tracking comparison](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_rate_comparison_20261008/tracking_comparison.png), [full-precision CSV](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_rate_comparison_20261008/comparison.csv), and [JSON/provenance](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_rate_comparison_20261008/comparison.json). Each new condition has the established selectable four-panel HTML/five SVG views, full PNG/PDF, zoom, three-cycle overlay, README, summary and analysis JSON. The specifically requested [5 Hz report](deploy/experiments/reports/mit_rate_comparison_20261008/steering_sine_20261008_1313_mit_200hz_10_01_5hz1rad/steering_sine_report.html) is complete, not just a comparison-table row; its steady window is **1.004629–18.569763 s**, **18,489** motion rows were re-decoded, and source SHA-256 is **`0eaf6f0225a999327409dc57cba727a807546bc54d406d474cb01940f19f34ca`**.

Values below are **50 → 200 Hz nominal command rate**. Amplitude is the A3 fitted fundamental, gain uses the actual encoded held-input fundamental, and RMS is against those held targets without phase alignment. Lag is frequency-specific phase-equivalent host-send/receipt time, not a universal transport/firmware delay.
