- VERDICT / CAUSAL LIMIT: ideal-sine lag improves **7.26–7.90 ms**, largely explained by reducing setpoint-hold lag from approximately **10.05 to 2.55 ms**. Lag relative to the commands actually sent changes by **less than 0.40 ms**: the intrinsic response has not been shown to become faster. At 2 Hz held-target RMS improves **4.55%**, versus worsening **2.37% / 1.28%** at 5/10 Hz; ideal-sine RMS improves **35.09% / 13.66% / 6.88%**. The 5 Hz missing amplitude is unchanged, and 10 Hz amplitude improves only about **5.4%** despite a substantially cleaner sinusoidal shape. **Neither rate achieves full ±1 rad steady stroke at 5/10 Hz.** The effective **20.5–20.6 rad/s** speed plateau remains versus ideal demands **31.4 / 62.8 rad/s**; its physical/firmware cause is unmeasured. Fractions with |speed|>20 rad/s change **61.41 → 57.99%** at 5 Hz and **23.18 → 37.00%** at 10 Hz.
- TORQUE / SAFETY: peaks fall approximately **64.12 / 25.64 / 17.42%** at 2/5/10 Hz, consistent with smaller command-step ripple but not a uniquely identified cause. At 10 Hz, torque RMS instead rises **5.32%**, and above-5.6 N·m samples increase; higher dispatch is **not a verified torque-protection fix**. Estimates remain A1 measured current × B0 Kt, not calibrated shaft torque; F0 T_Max does not establish total internal-PD limiting. All three new shutdowns repeat the historical **mode 5 / fault 0 / unconfirmed-disable** result: payloads **`CFE4070100260500` / `CFDE071000260500` / `CFE1070B002C0500`** for 2/5/10 Hz. These CSVs contain no subsequent confirmation proving physical free state. Later deployment CF/F1 handling is recorded separately in §53, not retroactively inferred from these experiments.
- HOST / CONDITIONS: new maximum command lateness is **2.889 / 2.909 / 2.476 ms**, versus older **0.335 / 0.278 / 0.252 ms**; new maximum intervals are **7.48–7.91 ms**, with zero logged skipped ticks or socket drops. A3/A2/A1 receipts remain approximately **196.6–196.9 Hz**, no >50 ms trace gaps; new query RTT p99 is **1.085 / 1.081 / 1.072 ms**, versus **0.882 / 0.872 / 0.880 ms**. Voltage startup/end, old → new: 2 Hz **20.19/20.22 → 20.20/20.20 V**, 5 Hz **20.20/20.19 → 20.20/20.14 V**, 10 Hz **20.20/20.11 → 20.20/20.17 V**. Temperature startup/end: **36/42 → 32/38°C**, **36/44 → 32/38°C**, **38/44 → 34/44°C**, respectively. These are endpoint samples, not under-load traces; current-limit/PI registers and CPU utilization were not measured. The 5 Hz record retains four A3 timeouts, one A1 timeout, and late A3/A2/A1 replies rather than claiming perfect reception.
- DEPLOYMENT IMPLICATION: more than **99.94%** of consecutive encoded targets change in the new logs: a **fresh sine is evaluated each ~5.1 ms**, not a 50 Hz policy target resent four times. Keep policy inference at 50 Hz; this experiment does not establish the benefit of 200 Hz resending held policy targets, identify firmware-loop frequency, certify loaded steering, or prove successful balancing. Proposed next comparison: identical **50 Hz-held target sequences** sent at 50 versus 200 Hz, first with small independently supported ±0.1/0.2 rad motion and documented gains/limits/supply, then supported loaded response. This remains a recommendation, not a completed hardware experiment.
- ANALYSIS VALIDATION — HISTORICAL RESULT: **244,987** CSV rows read, **100,161** motion rows re-decoded, including **52,469** new rows; existing analyzer functions used unchanged with temporary in-memory filename adaptation `_200Hz_` → `_mit_`. Three older baselines reproduce saved metrics/hashes. **29 numerical tests passed**, new F1/A3 gains agree within **0.001**, correlation lag within **0.25 ms**; checked **17 SVG / 11 PNG / 5 PDF**, **36 local HTML links**, six comparison rows, JSON metrics and visual aggregate plots. **381** pre-existing file fingerprints remained unchanged. Offline generation changed reports only, not raw inputs, runtime, policy, simulation, or configuration, and started no hardware node.

## 51. MIT deployment frequency split implemented and audited (2026-10-08)

- IMPLEMENTED CHANGE / RELATION TO §49: the user requested three independent divisions in [`motor_mit_io.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_mit_io.py): **50 Hz policy-originated `/trackstand/motor_command`**, **100 Hz state queries and state/status/fault topics**, **200 Hz MIT steering, rear C0, safety and `/trackstand/motor_control`**. §49 records a later observed snapshot; this entry records the preceding implementation and validation. Merely changing the old MIT constant would also couple dispatch/slew/deadlines while leaving old polling/diagnostic schedules unchanged, so the complete profile was implemented. ROS input callbacks remain unthrottled, especially disable requests; the motor node does not create the policy's 50 Hz producer.
- QUERY / PUBLICATION DETAILS: each 100 Hz round serializes **steering A3/A2, rear A3/A2, steering A1, rear A1**—six requests, not 100 total CAN frames/s. Continuous receive still consumes automatic F1 and rear C0 replies potentially at 200 Hz. Missing B0 retries and F0 verification retain **1 s** spacing; startup queries are separate. State remains seven-field `motor_state_v2` and **up to 100 Hz** because both encoder pairs must be fresh and newer; status/fault are attempted every **10 ms** even if state is unavailable or the CAN worker stopped. Safety/disable checks and transition events do not wait for this ROS publisher. Worst-case six **2 ms** query timeouts already exceed a 10 ms round; the scheduler skips missed rounds rather than issuing catch-up bursts, so nominal rates are not guaranteed under failed replies/load.
- RATE / COMPATIBILITY CONTRACT: MIT-specific parameters **`command_rate_hz=50`, `state_rate_hz=100`, `actuation_rate_hz=200`** are validated against the fixed profile. Shared YAML legacy `poll_rate_hz`/`control_rate_hz` keys are not adopted by MIT; legacy C0/C2 schedules remain unchanged. `MotorControlPublishGate` accepts a selected rate, retains a **50 Hz default** for legacy nodes, and MIT explicitly selects **200 Hz**. Its sequence still counts actuation ticks; field order/schema labels are preserved. Dispatch follows the common worker and actual last-send deadline, without extra MIT-only ticks or catch-up bursts. Repeated MIT sends do not refresh the **80 ms ROS-command** watchdog; paired feedback/F1 freshness remains **50 ms**. Gains, signs, target/torque limits, rear damping, policy/RC/kickstand logic were not changed by this frequency work. The standalone integrated sine's 50 Hz constant was also not changed.
- IMPLEMENTATION-STAGE VALIDATION: **486 deployment tests passed, 1 skipped**, including 50/100/200 scheduling with nonzero mock CAN-send work, absent query replies, deadline skipping, prompt disable between state publications and watchdog independence; **130 experiment tests passed**. Pyflakes/whitespace checks and an isolated colcon build/installed ROS import passed under **`/tmp/trackstand_mit_rates.U9pAJI/`**; repository build/install directories were not created. Files affected by the rate implementation include MIT node/tests, shared telemetry gate/tests and README/MIT/telemetry docs. These were mocked/offline results, not measured Raspberry Pi rates or an unsupported balancing test; subsequent fixes and newer disk states have separate validation below.

## 52. Urgent MIT safety fixes and behavior-preserving consolidation (2026-10-08)

- PRE-FIX AUDIT / REPRODUCED PROBLEMS: virtual-clock tests showed a feedback outage at **5 ms** could disable and recover before the next 100 Hz fault publication, yielding **two false fault messages** and automatic re-enable at 10 ms without RC down. A **90 ms** pause after the initial gate could still submit steering MIT and nonzero rear C0 using stale ages or a subsequently withdrawn enable. Runtime parameter metadata could claim `dry_run=true` while cached execution remained powered; worker exceptions could omit a terminal event. With target 1 rad and ticks at 0/8 ms, powered/dry-run slew differed **0.3107 / 0.239 rad**. These are demonstrated software defects in that dated snapshot, not measurements of the bike's timing. Audit reproducer: **`/tmp/trackstand_mit_audit.dMJ2XW/audit_checks.py`**; its old-behavior assertions are not expected to pass after fixes.
- ACTIVE WATCHDOG LATCH: active command timeout or lost paired encoder/B0 readiness now latches **`command_timeout` / `active_feedback_unavailable`**, rather than automatically resuming when data returns or allowing a transient to disappear between fault publications. Incomplete startup readiness only gates enable. Ordinary motor faults clear with enable off, confirmed disable, no active/pending outputs, fresh feedback/constants and steering **strictly inside ±5°**; at the validated snapshot, present angle/speed violations also prevented clearing. Hard transport/MIT/origin faults still require restart. The motor node receives policy-forwarded enable, not a new direct RC subscription.
- LAST-MOMENT SEND GUARDS: shared `_gate_state()` and `_motion_allowed()` revalidate **after payload encoding, immediately before each socket submission**, with no intervening ROS operation. They sample ages after lock waits, retain the original packet's command timestamp despite a newer policy message, and reject stopped-worker/withdrawn enable. `_disable_generation` remembers down/up within or between ticks; a canceled active disable latches **`enable_withdrawn_during_control`** until safe down/reset. Successful steering submission immediately marks possible active output; if the gate closes before rear submission, nonzero rear output is withheld and both motors enter disable confirmation. A failed steering send likewise withholds active rear C0. These checks cannot guarantee execution through arbitrary host freezes/preemption or broken CAN; motor-side deadman protection remains separate.
- PARAMETERS / TERMINAL EVENTS / DRY-RUN: all **21** MIT-owned parameters now use **`ParameterDescriptor(read_only=True)`**, with explicit `rcl_interfaces` package dependency. Startup CLI/YAML overrides remain valid; runtime changes are rejected instead of falsely advertising adopted settings. **`ros2 param set ... dry_run true` is not a stop command.** Worker cleanup attempts confirmed disable/close before terminal **`mit_io_stopped`** publication; reporting exceptions cannot skip prior CAN cleanup, and per-motor disable confirmations/partial transitions appear in events/status. First hard-fault reason is retained. Separate logical `_slew_last_at` makes real/dry jittered slew agree (**0.3107 rad** in the example) without inventing actual MIT send/F1 timestamps. First enable step remains **0.1195 rad**, delayed-step cap **0.239 rad**.
- REDUNDANCY REDUCTION / CHANGE BOUNDARY: consolidated receive/consume, scalar initialization, ROS publisher/schema setup, gate decisions, query/send diagnostics, state freshness filtering and disable-event details; tiny command finite/clamp work uses scalar math rather than NumPy allocation. This does not remove the repository's NumPy dependency or establish measured CPU savings. MIT remained independent of both legacy backends and `python-can`; existing state/control field ordering, topic/QoS contracts, gains and healthy command behavior were retained. **1069 → 1066 lines**, net **3 fewer** after safety additions—not a large line-count reduction. Only six files were changed in that implementation: MIT node, `test_motor_mit_io.py`, `test/conftest.py`, `package.xml`, `docs/MOTOR_MIT_IO.md`, and `docs/TELEMETRY.md`; no policy, training, simulator/model, IMU, RC, shared YAML or legacy-backend edits.
- VALIDATED SNAPSHOT / RESULTS: **515 deployment tests passed, 1 skipped**, plus **130 experiment tests**; the skip is the sandbox-restricted local Unix-socket kernel-timestamp test, not CAN. New regressions cover transient latch, encoding/lock/between-send pauses, rapid down/up, terminal faults and dry-run jitter; **17** new cases failed before initial fixes, with **3** additional follow-up cases reproduced before their corrections. Across **1,000 healthy synthetic powered/dry-run ticks** (seeds 0–4), pre/post CAN packets, slew, state/fault outputs, defaults and schema order match; **13** pre-existing methods remained AST-identical. Real local ROS 2 Humble parameter methods, without Node/DDS/CAN initialization, confirmed startup overrides and rejected live mutations. Pyflakes/whitespace checks, isolated colcon build (**one package, about 30.1 s**) and installed import passed. Saved source/install at **`/tmp/trackstand_mit_fix.XE3eEl/`** is **1066 lines**, SHA-256 **`61bdc5f6433080b28a2174152ccfd5d371a7329f5e832943a509c1a3cef254a4`**. These results certify that earlier offline snapshot only; current disk differs, as §53 explicitly records. No hardware executor/socket was started by this fix/validation.

- SNAPSHOT / PROVENANCE: the current MIT file is **1138 lines**, SHA-256 **`7384cc8e3f8f035ea1bbf100975f22e6ee7fc0b38418bbcc53d196370f58521d`**, not §52's validated 1066-line version. Current policy-controller SHA-256 is **`8a8e8bcd29312d032869d18637cd4a1ee69fb527b6c30179f4b4b640f3f5473d`**. These newer edits were present on disk and are preserved; this recording neither implements them nor infers their authorship/authorization. Existing §49, appended during the broader workspace activity, is also preserved. Historical passing results must not be promoted to certification of this newer source/test combination.
- CF CONFIRMATION IMPLEMENTATION: current startup/runtime/shutdown no longer treats every nonzero CF selected-mode byte as failed physical disabling. A fault-free **mode 0** acknowledgement directly confirms; retained modes **1–5** require a subsequent fresh **F1 status byte 0**, meaning MIT inactive and fault-free. Unknown status bits, active MIT, driver/system faults, invalid retained modes, stale or missing confirmations still fail closed. `_disable_f1_wait` tracks per-motor verification, and `_confirm_disable()` clears that motor's pending records; new active commands remain blocked pending confirmation. Runtime uses the existing **250 ms** disable deadline and **20 ms** failed-send retries. This addresses the distinction between retained selected mode and active MIT; **F1 is not an independent output-enable sensor for all control modes**, so physical free-state verification remains necessary. The old sweep CSVs themselves still do not contain the additional confirmation.
- ON-DISK LIVE-PROBE RECORD — **ATTRIBUTED, NOT REENACTED HERE**: [`MOTOR_MIT_IO.md`](deploy/docs/MOTOR_MIT_IO.md), “2026-10-08 live CF confirmation fix,” reports CF **mode 2 / fault 0**, followed by **F1 status 0**; it states bike firmware retains mode 2 after C0/CF and mode 5 after MIT/CF. That document records **210 MIT tests passed**, a **538 passed / 1 failed** deployment run attributed to missing `onnxruntime`, a successful ROS 2 Jazzy build/source match, and a live non-dry-run **CF/F1 plus rear C0-zero shutdown probe** confirming both motors with no pending/hard fault. It explicitly reports **no B1, F0 writes or MIT motion targets**, and no active balancing validation. Those are the saved document's claims, not fresh local hardware observations; the current local source/test pairing does not reproduce its all-applicable-tests-pass result below.
- TRANSPORT / ROS SHUTDOWN: current `SocketCan` checks interface **IFF_UP** with `SIOCGIFFLAGS` before motor preflight and rejects a down interface with **ENETDOWN**; it does not configure bitrate or bring CAN up. Failed sends include interface, CAN ID, payload and original errno. Startup logging precedes worker launch. Cleanup still attempts CAN disable/close after SIGINT, but terminal ROS event publication is skipped once **`self.context.ok()` is false**; a dead ROS context cannot carry a promised terminal event.
- SAFETY SEMANTICS CHANGED ON DISK: `_gate_state()` no longer triggers motor faults from measured steering beyond **±90°**, steering speed beyond **1.1×23.9**, or rear speed beyond **1.1×61.6 rad/s**. Those measurements remain diagnostic; **the steering command clamp remains ±90°, target slew 23.9 rad/s, and ordinary fault-clear angle remains strictly ±5°**. A target clamp is not an actual-position/speed stop. Current `policy_controller.py` also removes `max_roll_rad` and the former **30° roll** readiness check, retaining only **`gravity_body[2] > 0` inversion safety**, besides input freshness/motor faults and inference handling. Thus “roll over 30° disables the motors” is **not the current policy behavior**, and these later changes invalidate earlier blanket claims that all safety cutoffs were unchanged. No cutoff was restored or altered during this recording.
- FRESH CURRENT TEST RESULT: from `deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/`, **`PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider test --tb=short`** gives **443 passed / 72 failed / 1 skipped** (**6.58 s**). Many failures are stale test fixtures: `mit_node` uses `object.__new__`, bypasses the constructor, and omits the new **`_disable_f1_wait`**; the ROS stand-in also lacks **`context`**. The real constructor does initialize the map, and a real ROS Node supplies a context, so these AttributeErrors alone do not prove runtime absence. Other failures genuinely expose changed expectations: tests still require the removed unwrapped-position/speed faults and the **31° → `roll_or_inversion_safety`** check. Source/test reconciliation and new CF/F1 regressions are needed before claiming current full validation; this documentation-only task does not fix or classify every failure as harmless. The existing §49 focused result is not a complete-suite pass.
- FRESH EXPERIMENT RESULT / REMAINING HARDWARE GAPS: from repository root, **`MPLCONFIGDIR=/tmp/trackstand_mit_fix.XE3eEl/mpl PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider deploy/experiments/tests --tb=short`** gives **130 passed**, with only the existing Matplotlib Axes3D warning. Firmware PD-loop frequency, actual total-MIT torque/current protection, loaded tracking and real policy balancing remain unestablished here. Runtime **AE reports are consumed but not actively queried at 100 Hz**; 100 Hz fault/status publication is not proof of 100 Hz hardware-fault sampling. The node does not read/configure **CD**: local protocol p.15 specifies nonpersistent communication-timeout enable (documented default disabled), default **5000 ms**, timeout fault/disable and optional extra actions; the bike's live setting, MIT applicability and which traffic refreshes it remain unknown. Host software cannot guarantee disabling through a process/OS freeze, power loss or broken CAN. No undocumented firmware setting or physical motor command was changed during recording.

## 54. Raspberry Pi “No executable found”: packaging diagnosis and recovery instructions (recorded 2026-10-09)

- USER REPORT / CAUSE BOUNDARY: `ros2 run trackstand_deploy motor_mit_io` on the Pi returned **`No executable found`** despite sourcing `install/setup.bash`. The local ROS 2 Humble launcher implementation emits this when no matching executable exists under the resolved package prefix's **`lib/trackstand_deploy/`**; this happens **before node Python/CAN initialization**. Sourcing selects an installation but does not regenerate console-entry-point wrappers. An outdated build/source, another selected overlay or a missing/nonexecutable wrapper are plausible; the Pi's exact installation state was **not remotely inspected**, so no unique root cause or completed on-Pi fix is claimed.
- DISK EVIDENCE: repository [`setup.py`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/setup.py) already registers **`motor_mit_io = trackstand_deploy.motor_mit_io:main`**; [`setup.cfg`](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/setup.cfg) installs scripts under **`$base/lib/trackstand_deploy`**. The previous isolated build has an executable **`/tmp/trackstand_mit_fix.XE3eEl/install/trackstand_deploy/lib/trackstand_deploy/motor_mit_io`** wrapper. That proves local packaging can generate the entry point, not that the Pi received/built it. Repository `deploy/build` and `deploy/install` are absent locally; validation installations under `/tmp` must not be confused with the Pi workspace.
- PROVIDED RECOVERY / VERIFICATION: on the Pi, synchronize the complete source package, including `setup.py` rather than only the node, then run the following from `~/trackstand_rl/deploy`. Confirm the prefix belongs to this intended install and executable listing contains **`trackstand_deploy motor_mit_io`**. If build succeeds but registration remains absent, inspect build output/prefix/executable listing instead of assuming a CAN fault. These commands are instructions, not actions performed on the Pi by this recording.

```bash
cd ~/trackstand_rl/deploy
rg -n 'motor_mit_io =' source/trackstand_deploy/setup.py
colcon build --symlink-install --packages-select trackstand_deploy
source install/setup.bash
ros2 pkg prefix trackstand_deploy
ros2 pkg executables trackstand_deploy
ros2 run trackstand_deploy motor_mit_io --ros-args -p dry_run:=true
```

- SAFE INTERPRETATION / RECORDING BOUNDARY: begin independently supported with exactly one motor-I/O backend; dry-run reads hardware but sends no MIT/C0/CF/B1/F0 writes and is not a substitute for physical emergency stopping or proof an already-running motor is off. Use **`--ros-args -p dry_run:=true`**, not a remapping rule. Startup/package registration does not certify the newer code's safety or resolve §53's test mismatch. This append changes **only `progress_record.md`**, preserves previous Parts I–III and evolving unrelated source/test/docs work, and starts no real ROS executor, CAN socket, hardware experiment, simulator, build or new rosbag; the fresh checks above are offline tests.

## 58. First analyzed powered-policy deployment bag: runaway, startup demand and containment gaps (2026-10-12)

- RECORD / CONDITIONS: analyzed `deploy/experiments/logs/rosbag2_2026_10_12-12_06_32/rosbag2_2026_10_12-12_06_32_0.mcap`, **85,540,864 bytes**, SHA-256 **`262c9510421a9efd477b3171ec018c71cc17ca08ba894edf6a3eb2df1fc3c06f`**. The user observed forward rear-wheel spin/travel, steering near 90°, then falling; confirms both wheels grounded, no holding/lifting/restraint in all four attempts, physically straight steering at each motor-node start/restart, unchanged settings and synchronized Pi/local repositories. Straightness at restart does **not** establish centering at every later RC-up. There is no independent body-velocity/video or physical servo-position trace.
- READABILITY: the MCAP lacks `metadata.yaml`, a completed footer and terminal magic. Streaming CRC-checked decoding recovers **227,892 messages over 155.296 s**, with **no decoded-message errors**, before EOF in the unfinished tail. Expected/final completeness and truncation cause remain unknown; original bytes were not repaired or modified. This is an identifiable powered RL deployment recording, superseding §48's earlier inventory finding of no such bag.
- VERIFIED POLICY / PIPELINE: status identifies **91850**, `dry_run=false`, MIT steering/C0 rear and nominal **50/100/200 Hz**. There are **249 observations/raw actions/enabled policy commands/successful inferences** and **839 successful rear C0-send ticks**. ONNX replay maximum error **2.3841858e-7**; original checkpoint actor maximum error **1.5258789e-5**. Scaling/clamping and the rear clipping/damping equation reconstruct with **zero error**. Encoded MIT Kp/Kd **10.012210/0.200244**, B0 approximately **0.78/0.30 N·m/A**. Wrong model, broken export, missing normalization or extra action scaling are not supported by these comparisons; no effective parameter-event payload was recorded.

| Attempt | First inference, bag s / count | Initial unclipped steer / rear request | Powered steering range / peak rear speed magnitude | Control ends because |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 7.489 / 76 | +220.78° / +142.37 N·m | +2.20°…+90.18° / 61.72 rad/s | Rear feedback stale → motor and policy fault |
| 2 | 48.008 / 109 | +153.38° / +107.91 N·m | −35.51°…+95.38° / 62.54 rad/s | RC down |
| 3 | 79.590 / 2 | +368.74° / +222.08 N·m | Initial reported steer ≈178.95°; no powered rear sends | MIT target encoding rejected |
| 4 | 117.810 / 62 | +154.09° / +108.83 N·m | 0°…+89.87° / 62.77 rad/s | Inversion → policy/motor disable |

- COMMAND SATURATION: steering requests exceed ±90° on **188/249 (75.5%)** samples; rear requests exceed ±6.28 N·m on **200/249 (80.3%)**. Powered attempts 1/2/4 reach rear speed magnitude >50 rad/s **54.74/61.74/56.64 ms** after the first rear send and steer >85° after **188.19/102.11/180.29 ms**. Rear final commands lie within 0.001 N·m of saturation on **90.4/70.4/100%** of powered ticks. Near-90° steering is largely requested behavior, not unexplained MIT drift; large steering alone is not a failed trackstand criterion.
- REAR SIGN EVIDENCE — BEFORE THE MANUAL CONFIRMATION BELOW: positive policy torque/native current around **−20.933 A** coincides with negative reported rear speed on **91.6/78.9/98.1%** of powered ticks, although the user sees forward motion. Attempt 4 median speed **−60.30 rad/s**, net rear rotation **−70.62 rad**, command approximately **+6.28 N·m throughout**. Under the then-current encoder sign, `−0.05*speed` adds about **+3 N·m** instead of damping physical forward rotation; proxy vx is about **−4.2 m/s**. Offline flipping observation indices **[5,8,10]** (vx/rear position/rear speed) reduces rear raw `abs(action)>1` from **200 to 40/249**, and attempt-4 median action **+4.479→−0.138**. This is fixed-record sensitivity, **not a corrected closed-loop trajectory or proof of balancing**. Read-only `mj_forward` kinematics of the verified XML confirms policy-positive rear rotation is toward the bike's front; models were not changed or challenged.
- STARTUP DEMAND — SEPARATE FROM THE SIGN ERROR: an ideal upright, motionless, straight-steering observation produces 91850 raw **`[1.599545, 16.720318]`**, scaled **+143.886° / +105.004 N·m**. Small IMU tilt, wheel-speed sign or inference delay is not needed for that burst. Reanalysis of the saved Isaac motor-limit dataset finds **all 171 episode starts** requesting **63.887–132.002 N·m**, median **103.572 N·m**; subsequent rear requests peak at **2.765 N·m**. After 20 ms, **167/171** starts have positive rear speed, median **+60.311 rad/s**. PhysX feed-forward submissions retained unbounded requests; the clipped implicit-actuator diagnostic is not proof of a hardware-equivalent total solver cap. Learned reliance on that impulse is plausible, but requires a strictly bounded, matched simulation experiment; none was run here. The archived versus deployment exceedance rates (**0.659/0.0891%** steering/rear versus **75.5/80.3%**) describe different datasets, not matched failure probabilities.
- OBSERVATION / HARDWARE BOUNDARIES: simulator observations contain actual body-frame vx/vy; deployment uses **rear speed×0.07 / vy=0**. Slip/turning can invalidate that estimate even with both wheels grounded. Ground-loaded steering response remains unmeasured independently; attempt 2 overshoots to **95.38°**, while unloaded sine tests do not certify loaded tracking. Torque feedback is **signed measured Q-axis current×B0**, not shaft torque: powered-attempt rear medians are only approximately **+0.611/+0.365/+0.276 N·m**, with initial peaks up to **+6.30 N·m**. Saturated requests do not establish continuously delivered saturated torque. Firmware loop rate, total MIT protection, supply sag/current ramp and actual translational speed remain unknown.
- TIMING / SECONDARY FAILURES: policy interval median/p99/max **19.99/21.76/22.44 ms**, inference **0.192/0.561/1.558 ms**; MIT send interval **5.42/9.42/17.55 ms**, mean **5.83 ms (~172 Hz)**. Attempt 1 faults at **8.990 s** with rear pair age **51.88 ms**, after the initial runaway. Worker stops at **16.014/123.522 s** report **clock-sampling delay/feedback-age uncertainty**, after the preceding powered attempts; this is not proof of a clock jump or the initial failure's cause. RC averages **~494 Hz** because every parsed frame is published; RC-down callbacks generate **77,091 disabled commands**, a traffic/load candidate, not measured CPU overload. Enabled policy commands still run at 50 Hz. Quaternion norms are one and stationary pre-start gyro is zero; quaternion/gyro rate correlations **0.766/0.693/0.649** do not show a gross sign inversion, but do not certify full mounting/calibration.
- SAFETY / ARMING GAPS: the policy's removed 30° cutoff means attempt 4 crosses training's `abs(gravity_y)>0.5` at **118.522 s**, reaches inversion at **119.040 s**, and stops policy at **119.047 s**—about **0.52 s after the first threshold crossing**, not necessarily continuously beyond it. Attempt 2 crosses at **48.380 s** but runs until RC-down near 50.18 s. Powered attempts exceed **7.2 rad net rear rotation after ~162–166 ms**, without an equivalent deployment runaway guard. Attempt 3 proposes native MIT **−3.004114 rad**, outside ±3 and rejected before either powered motor send, but kickstands-up has already been requested: an arming/readiness gap, not proven origin corruption. Protocol disable confirmation is not independent physical stop/support proof; policy faults do not automatically lower stands.
- CONCLUSION / ORDER: at diagnosis, likelihood order was **rear sign mismatch → excessive startup/action-limit mismatch → velocity-estimator/slip mismatch → loaded steering mismatch → feedback/host scheduling**; the first is subsequently confirmed in §59, while the other causal mechanisms remain partly hypothetical. No basis to replace the bike/motors from this bag. First correct/verify signs and supported startup behavior, then compare hardware-bounded actuator/estimator/release simulations with identical seeds/initial states, followed by supported loaded tracking, arming/watchdog and Pi CPU/CAN timing tests. Restore a validated fall/runaway envelope and physical emergency stopping before unsupported retries; none of those additional fixes/tests was implemented by the diagnosis.
- ARTIFACTS / VALIDATION: [report](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_120632/README.md), [HTML](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_120632/index.html), [summary.json](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_120632/summary.json), **249-row policy / 839-row motor CSVs**, four PNG/SVG plot pairs (`attempts`, `policy_requests`, `startup`, `timing`) and reusable streaming-decoder/analyzer scripts. Independent decoder reproduction matched arrays including NaNs and message details/counts; input/source hashes, scripts, CSV schemas, plots and **27 local links** passed checks. Only new offline analysis files were created; no dynamic simulation, training, motor command or model/runtime edit occurred during diagnosis. The report and source hashes remain the **pre-sign-correction snapshot**; its conditional sign recommendation is superseded below, not silently regenerated with corrected defaults.

## 59. Physically labelled CAN measurements confirm the rear encoder sign error (2026-10-12)

- USER MEASUREMENTS / PROTOCOL DECODING: manually rotating the rear wheel **forward** produced these four-byte A2 fields. Local protocol defines signed little-endian int32 speed in **0.01 RPM**, independently confirming negative native forward velocity:

| A2 speed bytes | Native RPM | Native rad/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| `02D7FFFF` | −104.94 | −10.9893 |
| `64FAFFFF` | −14.36 | −1.5038 |
| `88F6FFFF` | −24.24 | −2.5384 |
| `87F0FFFF` | −39.61 | −4.1479 |
| `F4E2FFFF` | −74.36 | −7.7870 |

- POSITION CONFIRMATION: forward manual rotation changed the six A3 data bytes from **`232C236C5200` to `630C634C5200`** (excluding opcode). Single-turn counts **11,299→3,171** and signed multi-turn counts **5,401,635→5,393,507** both decrease **8,128 counts**, equivalent to **−178.59375° / −3.117049 rad** at 16,384 counts/revolution. Thus A2 **and** A3 need the same **−1 rear encoder sign** to make forward motion positive. Published rear position remains startup-relative and wrapped to ±π; positive forward displacement can wrap across the boundary and is not globally monotonic.
- CURRENT DIRECTION IS INDEPENDENT: `cansend can0 101#C000200000` requests **+8.192 A native C0** and physically turns the rear wheel **backward**. The user corrected the reported current opcode from A0 to **A1**: field **`96020000`** is **+0.662 A measured** at one instant. A0 is version information, not Q-axis current. This supports keeping **rear torque sign −1**, independently of the encoder correction; the one A1 sample does not establish tracking quality, why measured current differs from requested current, or calibrated shaft torque.
- VERDICT / HISTORICAL SUPERSESSION: rear native forward position/speed is **negative**, native positive C0 is backward; therefore **rear encoder/torque signs must be −1/−1**. Steering stays **−1/−1**, policy-positive steering right and rear forward. This supersedes earlier recorded rear **+1/−1** A2/A3 assumptions and resolves the bag's strongest sign inconsistency; those dated reports/old CSV metadata remain historical evidence, not current defaults. It does not establish that the startup-demand, estimator, loaded-response or safety issues are solved.

## 60. Rear sign correction applied across deployment and feedback tools (2026-10-12)

- IMPLEMENTATION: changed **only rear encoder default +1→−1** in [params.yaml](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/config/params.yaml), [motor_io.py](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_io.py), independent [motor_mit_io.py](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/motor_mit_io.py), [motor_feedback.py](deploy/experiments/motor_feedback.py), [motor_feedback_mit.py](deploy/experiments/motor_feedback_mit.py), and the read-only rear collector in [steering_sine_mit_io.py](deploy/experiments/steering_sine_mit_io.py). `motor_io_position.py` inherits C0 defaults and needs no direct edit. YAML and bare-node/tool defaults agree. **Rear torque −1, steering signs, IDs, gearing, model, rates, gains/laws, limits, fault logic, IMU, RC and servo behavior are unchanged.** Corrected A3/A2 propagate consistently into rear position/speed and proxy vx; the existing damping law now opposes forward motion.
- DOCS / TESTS: synchronized four guides (`deploy/README.md`, `docs/AUDIT.md`, `docs/HARDWARE_CHECKLIST.md`, `experiments/README.md`), qualified the historical sign assumption and updated four existing test files' default expectations. Added [test_rear_motor_signs.py](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/test/test_rear_motor_signs.py), **27 cases across C0/C2/MIT** covering YAML/defaults, the actual A2/A3/A1 samples, observation channels, damping direction and mocked C0 current submissions. No original logs/reports, checkpoints, generated build trees or unrelated submodule changes were rewritten.
- COMPLETED SIGN-CHANGE VALIDATION: **73 focused tests passed**, including the 27 new cases; **130 experiment tests passed**. Full deployment suite **470 passed / 72 failed / 1 skipped**, versus pre-change **443 passed / 72 failed / 1 skipped**; exact failed-test IDs are unchanged, with no new failures. The existing fixture/removed-safety-expectation failures remain unresolved. Runtime Python files were checked to differ from their pre-change versions by only the encoder-default literal, parse checks and `git diff --check` passed. ## 62. Corrected-run observation audit: units mostly consistent, training-equivalent velocity and rear phase remain unresolved (recorded 2026-10-13)

- GRAVITY / RATES: the 13-term order and SI conversion match current contracts. Quaternion norm is **1**; gravity is inverse rotation of `[0,0,-1]`, dimensionless, with pure roll `gy=-sin(roll)`, `gz=-cos(roll)`—opposite roll/gravity-y signs are expected. Vendor gyro conversion includes **π/180** once; policy channels equal published rad/s. Pre-start gyros are exactly zero and roll medians **−0.998/−1.564/−0.851°**; actual stand lean versus bias cannot be separated without an external upright reference. These unlabelled motions do not independently certify every physical IMU axis.
- DYNAMIC IMU COHERENCE: whole-record quaternion-increment/gyro x/y/z correlations **0.502/0.596/0.721** improve to **0.787/0.701/0.721** when gyro sample times shift **+18/+12/+1 ms**. These are descriptive alignments over ~40 ms rotations, not universal firmware delays or a proven calibration/scale inversion. Split vendor packets and any-valid-packet freshness can mix field ages; filtering, vibration and orientation fusion remain possible contributors. No extra axis reversal or bias subtraction was implemented.
- ENCODER CONSISTENCY: steering positive remains right, rear positive forward, with both native encoder signs **−1**. Signed F1/A3 position correlation **0.998/0.996/0.994**, slope **1.002/0.992/0.990** across attempts. Comparing A3 angle change against **interval-averaged A2 velocity over the same ~40 ms** gives steering correlation/slope **0.989/0.950** (934 samples) and rear **0.956/0.846** (1,029); excludes gaps and avoids comparing an averaged derivative to instantaneous velocity during fast oscillation. This supports consistent rad/rad/s direction and scale, not independently calibrated gearing or perfect dynamic accuracy. Steering sin/cos errors are **5.10e-8/7.86e-8**.
- VELOCITY OBSERVATION GAP: deployment vx equals **0.07×rear speed**, residual **3.24e-7 m/s**, range **−4.229…+4.266 m/s**; vy is always zero. Training supplies actual body-root vx/vy. Grounded wheels do not establish rolling without slip, especially with steering near 90°, turning or stand support; physical translation/slip is unmeasured. Fixed-record zero-vx replay increases rear request exceedances **341→604**, so merely replacing the estimate with zero is **not a validated fix**.
- REAR REPRESENTATION — QUALIFIES EARLIER EXACT-PARITY CLAIMS: deployment wraps startup-relative A3 to **[−π,π)** and retains that process reference across RC cycles; rear starts are **0/−1.801/−2.082 rad**. Training resets joint position to zero each episode and reads backend `joint_pos` directly, without explicit wrapping. The archived 91850 evaluation's **192,000 policy-boundary samples** range **−0.081578…+3.521434 rad** (median **1.601505**); **four exceed π**. Thus unconditional exact ±π parity is not secure. This does **not** prove unlimited multi-turn backend behavior; older wrapping comments are insufficient evidence. Define the branch/reference contract through a known-angle/reset probe before modifying deployment.
- PHASE SENSITIVITY — OFFLINE ONLY: **69 ±π branch jumps** occur. Attempt 2 at **14.139745 s** changes rear observation **+2.793→−2.743 rad**, raw rear **−0.150→+56.856** (other inputs also change). Holding that recorded state otherwise fixed and choosing the equivalent positive branch gives raw **+7.694**, still over the torque cap. Branch-only replay reduces exceedances **341→263**; per-attempt continuous unwrapping instead raises them to **713**, continuous-plus-zero-start to **726**. These replays are not alternative physical trajectories, proof that a wrap caused a fall, or validated remedies. A nonzero start reference cannot alone explain attempt 1, which starts at zero. [Phase graph](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/rear_phase.png), [replay CSV](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/rear_phase_sensitivity.csv).

## 63. Corrected-run action magnitude and motor tracking: submitted MIT motion differs substantially from policy targets (recorded 2026-10-13)

- REQUESTS / LIMITS: raw steer spans **−6.036…+25.030**, rear **−2.508…+106.251**; maximum scaled requests **+2251.54°/+667.26 N·m**. Steering exceeds ±90° on **594/894 (66.4%)**, rear exceeds ±6.28 N·m on **341/894 (38.1%)**. In the first second of each attempt (51 samples each), counts are steering **32/36/34**, rear **18/25/23**; those intervals can already include stand support. The excessive ideal-zero 91850 request **+143.886°/+105.004 N·m** and archived 171 startup impulses from §58 persist independently of the rear sign correction. Recorded whole-run extrema are not all free-balance demands, but early saturation is also present.
- TARGET TRAJECTORY / REALIZABILITY: **520/891** adjacent clipped steering changes imply speed above **23.9 rad/s**, including **115 full ±90° reversals** between 50 Hz frames (7/53/55). At that slew limit a 180° change requires **π/23.9≈131 ms**, not 20 ms. Slew limiting occurs on **148/178, 1,017/1,601, 922/1,243** active motor ticks. This is not a requirement to settle each target before the next inference; it shows why the exact actuator dynamics must be in the policy's training/evaluation contract. MIT signed absolute position encoding error is at most **4.58e-5 rad (0.00262°)**; rear C0 quantization contributes at most **0.000150 N·m**, too small to explain these tracking differences.

| Attempt | Simultaneous steering RMS versus clipped policy target | Simultaneous RMS versus submitted MIT target | Submitted MIT→published A3 best alignment / correlation | Time-aligned RMS, no gain/bias correction | Rear C0→current-derived torque alignment / correlation / aligned RMS |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|
| 1 | 80.16° | 22.26° | 24 ms / 0.995 | 4.32° | 5 ms / 0.935 / 1.52 N·m |
| 2 | 69.57° | 17.59° | 24 ms / 0.981 | 4.99° | 5 ms / 0.919 / 1.66 N·m |
| 3 | 76.45° | 19.63° | 25 ms / 0.985 | 5.58° | 4 ms / 0.889 / 1.92 N·m |

- STEERING INTERPRETATION: first-second MIT/A3 alignments remain **24/23/25 ms**. Estimated direct-F1 kernel reception gives **22/21/20 ms**, whereas clipped-policy/A3 alignment is **49/50/39 ms**, with weaker correlations **0.646/0.548/0.496** because slew changes the input shape. These are fitted closed-loop recorded-response alignments, **not pure firmware dead times, measured internal-loop rates or motor-defect proof**; F1 reception time is estimated from ages sampled after loop entry. Steering largely follows its actual MIT input with lag, not the rapidly reversing policy target at the same instant. Current-derived steering torque ranges **−3.143…+3.625 N·m**, no recorded sample above 5.6; this does not establish an enforced total-PD cap or exclude missed peaks.
- REAR MOTION / CURRENT: signed speed is approximately **−60…+62 rad/s**, with **26/226/164** >5-rad/s direction reversals and median spacing **39/39/40 ms** (crossing statistics, not a unique oscillation frequency). Estimated net angle **+10.85/+137.77/+97.07 rad** crosses training's 7.2-rad envelope **447/409/437 ms** after first rear send; these unwrapped-feedback estimates require adequate sampling and are **not travelled distance**. For aligned command magnitudes ≥3 N·m, current-derived torque is below half the request on **6/40, 108/372, 86/282** samples. Cached asynchronous A1/C0 ages, filtering, current ramps, speed capability and supply are not separated; the 4–5 ms alignment/RMS above is **not shaft-torque accuracy or internal-current-loop latency**.
- DAMPING VS POLICY REVERSALS: **312** strong adjacent final-torque reversals occur (both magnitudes ≥0.5 N·m); only **12** retain the exact same policy rear request. Example **14.464999→14.470650 s**: request remains **+0.054329 N·m**, speed **+11.190→−10.689 rad/s**, final torque **−0.505→+0.589 N·m**, exactly from sampled damping. Most strong flips accompany policy changes; this does not prove host damping harmless, but does not support blaming damping alone or random motor commands as the main oscillator. Current feedback remains measured Q-axis current×B0, not calibrated shaft torque. [Tracking scans](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/tracking_alignment.png), [rear decomposition](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/rear_control.png).

## 64. Corrected-run host timing, ranked diagnosis, remaining tests and analysis artifacts (recorded 2026-10-13)

- MEASURED RATES / AGES: policy period median/p99/max **20.00/22.19/26.28 ms**, inference **0.210/0.614/4.177 ms**. Policy-tick→first matching successful dispatch-tick medians **4.37/4.03/4.36 ms**; actual write occurs within that tick, so this is not wire-level latency. Two final samples have no matched successful send before stopping. Within active episodes MIT interval median/p99/max **5.48/10.40/22.92 ms**, mean **5.90 ms (~169.5 Hz)** rather than exact 200; first re-enable sends/multi-second inactive gaps excluded. IMU/motor callback ages median **8.94/3.95 ms**, max **17.39/36.75 ms**; CAN steering/rear pair ages median **6.62/6.22 ms**, max **42.66/48.02 ms**. Do not simply add overlapping/differently defined delays; callback age is not sensor acquisition age.
- SECONDARY STOPS / LOAD CANDIDATES: attempt 1's steering feedback gate latches around **7.2465 bag s** (~1.07 s after inference starts); policy reports `motor_state_timeout`. Their monotonic timestamps establish ordering despite cross-topic recorder order, with confirmed disable by **7.2684 s**. Attempt 3's worker stops around **40.0825 s**, reporting **`system clock sampling delayed; CAN feedback age is uncertain`**, with confirmed-disable terminal event **40.0924 s**; no worker restart appears. Neither stop explains the preceding rapid early lean, and timestamp uncertainty is not proof of a clock jump. RC averages **493.9 Hz**, producing **32,239 disabled commands**; motor-state publication averages **~68.6 Hz while the worker is alive**, not guaranteed 100. CPU/thermal/CAN load is not measured, so overload is a candidate, not established cause.
- LIKELIHOOD ORDER — QUALITATIVE, NOT NUMERICAL CAUSAL PROBABILITIES: **(1) policy–actuator bounds/trajectory/delay mismatch, very high**; **(2) wheel-based vx/zero-vy and IMU field-timing mismatch, high-to-medium**; **(3) rear branch/reference discrepancy, medium and not a demonstrated sole cause**; **(4) sampling/transport robustness, medium as a deployment defect but lower for the initial lean**; **(5) rear current/damping and ground-loaded actuator differences, medium-to-lower as a distinct hardware cause**. Excessive startup, clamping, requested rates, lag and software stops are measured; learned dependence on an unbounded startup impulse, slip, supply sag and specific firmware mechanisms require tests. Good submitted-MIT/A3 correlation argues against replacing the steering motor first. No evidence requires altering the verified XML/USD geometry or replacing the bicycle; prioritize the controllable interface and realistic policy training rather than arbitrary gain/sign flips.
- PROPOSED VERIFICATION — NOT IMPLEMENTED/RUN: independently supported, policy-disabled labelled IMU axes and known wheel revolution/fork angles, with passive CAN and parameter/firmware dumps; a simulator rear observation probe at **+2.8/+3.5/−3.5 rad**, π crossings and resets; guarded rear-only low-current/speed-capped pulse and zero-request damping comparisons; safely scaled loaded steering replay separating clipped/slewed/submitted/measured targets; then matched headless evaluations adding genuinely enforced hardware force bounds, actual estimator, sample holds, measured field/transport delays and release conditions. Current node has **no CLI damping/total-effort-cap controls**, so the low-risk rear experiment needs an audited harness, not invented parameter flags or assuming a small request bounds damping torque. Do not use a second querying CAN owner alongside MIT or remove limits to improve a graph. Retraining is a recommendation after interface reconciliation, not work completed here.
- SAFETY / NEXT RECORDING: independently support/tether before further closed loop; stop active tests promptly at loss/contact. Restore a validated fall/net-motion/centering envelope and physical emergency stop before unsupported retries; current policy has no 30° cutoff, MIT no measured-angle/overspeed stops, and policy faults do not automatically lower stands. Preserve protection rather than extending watchdogs blindly. Record effective parameters/model/source hashes, synchronized translation and servo-release/contact video, passive CAN, supply/firmware limits and the existing IMU/state/control/command/observation/action/timing/event/status/fault/RC topics plus `/rosout` and `/parameter_events`. Body motion/slip, physical support and total motor protection remain unmeasured.
- DELIVERED ARTIFACTS / HISTORICAL VALIDATION: [report](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/README.md), [HTML gallery](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/index.html), [summary.json](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/summary.json), reproducible [analyzer](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/analyze_corrected.py) reusing the previous streaming decoder, six CSVs (including **894-row policy/phase** and **3,022-row active-command** datasets), and **seven PNG/SVG pairs**: attempts, startup, unclipped requests, rear control, rear phase, tracking alignment and timing. ## 65. Raw-output versus downstream limits: training/deployment distinction and recommendation (2026-10-13; recorded 2026-10-14)

- RAW OUTPUT / SHARED SCALES: Trackstand-Balance-v0's RSL wrapper defaults to **`clip_actions=None`**; saved 09221945 [agent.yaml](isaaclab_train/logs/rsl_rl/trackstand_rl_rsl/2026-09-22_19-45-06/params/agent.yaml) has `clip_actions: null`, and [env.yaml](isaaclab_train/logs/rsl_rl/trackstand_rl_rsl/2026-09-22_19-45-06/params/env.yaml) has `clip: null` for both action terms. Deployment likewise does not clip raw ONNX output; `/trackstand/raw_action` publishes the original two values. Both scale steering by **1.57 rad** and rear by **6.28 N·m**. Scaling is not normalization into [-1,1]; the task's [-1,1] docstring and PPO loss `clip_param=0.2` do not enforce an action bound.
- THREE EXTRA PRE-CHANGE COMMAND RESTRICTIONS: deployment explicitly clipped the steering target to **±90°**, slewed its submitted target at **23.9 rad/s**, and clipped rear torque to **±6.28 N·m both before and after damping**. Training has no equivalent explicit position-target clipping or target slew in its action path, despite approximately ±90° joint travel and simulator velocity/effort settings. Travel limits are not target limits; an action-rate reward is a soft penalty, not a hard slew cap. Training's implicit-actuator clipped diagnostic is not proof of a deployment-equivalent total feed-forward torque cap. MIT total steering-PD ±5.6 N·m protection also remains unverified; legacy C0 explicitly clamps that torque. These comparisons concern software command processing, not proof that every actual simulator force/motion bound is weaker.
- RECOMMENDATION GIVEN BEFORE THE USER'S NEXT REQUEST: retain the defined steering operating-range cap and a validated final rear current/torque boundary; steering target slew is not fundamentally required by a position controller and was a candidate for supported, identical-target A/B testing after verifying motor current protection. A position setpoint may step even though physical motion cannot. Existing traces did not prove removing slew would improve balancing. **6.28 N·m is the recorded stall reference, not a demonstrated safe continuous torque.** The pre-damping rear clamp is optional if an effective final clamp remains, but is not mathematically redundant: request **10 N·m**, speed **60 rad/s**, damping **−3 N·m** gives **3.28 N·m** with double clipping versus **6.28 N·m** with damping then only final clipping. Any retained transformations should also be represented in training/evaluation. No such A/B hardware test or retraining was performed; the user subsequently explicitly requested disabling all three restrictions, recorded below.

## 67. Complete historical pre-disable command flow: inference through actual CAN submission (2026-10-13; recorded 2026-10-14)

- SNAPSHOT / REACHABILITY: traced original Git **`c4b2df7d63bf978ff784f909f872c7545e10ea58`**, whose ten relevant source SHA-256 values match the corrected-run report's `source_hashes`. This is the **pre-disable code**, not today's commented blocks. The active policy calls [scale_policy_action](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/contracts.py) and [publishes motor_command](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/policy_controller.py); the selected MIT node subscribes to that topic, stores separately requested/bounded values under a lock, and its worker calls `_control_tick`. The normal control path reads **bounded `_steering_target_rad`/`_drive_target_nm`**, not unbounded requested fields kept for diagnostics. The final send functions do not substitute raw policy values again. All links point to current files; the original active statements are recoverable from the named Git snapshot and retained comments.
- COMPLETE FLOW — NORMAL RC-UP, READY, ENABLED PATH; RC-DOWN/FAILED GATES WITHHOLD MOTION AND USE CONFIRMED DISABLE:

```text
/trackstand/imu + /trackstand/motor_state (first four signed kinematic fields)
  -> policy_controller: build 13-term observation -> OnnxPolicy.act -> raw [a_s,a_d]
  -> /trackstand/raw_action publishes unchanged raw values (not motor commands)
  -> scale_policy_action:
       steering q_ros = clip(1.57*a_s, +/-pi/2); rear tau_ros = 6.28*a_d
  -> /trackstand/motor_command = [enable, q_ros(rad), tau_ros(N m)]
  -> motor_mit_io._command_callback:
       retain requested_* for telemetry; store q_t=clip(q_ros,+/-steering_limit)
       and tau_t=clip(tau_ros,+/-6.28); stamp command, retain disable generation
  -> 200 Hz _control_tick: lock snapshot of bounded targets -> freshness/fault gate
       steering, when MIT send due:
         step_max = 23.9*min(elapsed_since_logical_send,0.010) (first elapsed=0.005)
         q_send = q_previous + clip(q_t-q_previous,+/-step_max)
         -> native = MIT_zero + encoder_sign*gear_ratio*q_send
         -> absolute MIT packing; round/clamp encoded position inside travel range
         -> recheck send guard -> CAN 0x502 -> motor internal position PD
       rear:
         tau_damping = -0.05*reported_rear_speed
         tau_final = clip(tau_t+tau_damping,+/-6.28)
         -> native current = torque_sign*tau_final/B0_Kt
         -> round to signed int32 milliamps in C0 payload (opcode + four LE bytes)
         -> recheck send guard -> CAN 0x101 -> motor current control
  -> /trackstand/motor_control: requested, bounded, slewed, encoded and send flags
  -> motor feedback A3/A2/A1/C0/F1 -> signed state/current telemetry -> next observation
```

- HOLD / SIGN / QUANTIZATION DETAILS: raw actions are not [-1,1] clipped. Policy updates at **50 Hz**, while nominal **200 Hz** dispatch holds the latest input and slews steering; it does not infer four new actions. The first slew increment is at most **0.1195 rad**, delayed increments at most **0.239 rad**; `q_previous` is the stored pre-quantization target, with disabled initialization from measured steering. Both sends recheck the original command timestamp/disable-generation and feedback readiness after packing. This run uses gear **1**, steering encoder sign **−1**, rear torque sign **−1**, B0 rear **0.3000000119 N·m/A**, MIT absolute zero **+4.577706569e-5 rad**, requested Kp/Kd **10/0.2** and zero velocity/feed-forward (quantization residuals remain). Target limiting is upstream of motor PD, not a clamp on its total torque.

| First recorded command stage | Steering | Rear drive |
|---|---:|---:|
| Raw policy action (`raw_action` bag time 6.161969160 s) | 1.7144215107 | 17.2883148193 |
| Scaled, before hardware clipping | 2.6916417718 rad (154.22°) | 108.5706170654 N·m |
| Actual `/trackstand/motor_command` | 1.5707963268 rad (90°) | 108.5706170654 N·m |
| Motor callback's stored bounded targets | 1.5707963268 rad | 6.28 N·m |
| Slew / damping at first successful paired dispatch, 6.179994832 s | 0.1195000000 rad | 6.2808901179 N·m |
| Final protected values before encoding | 0.1195000000 rad | 6.28 N·m |
| Encoded values, reconstructed in policy joint units | 0.1194781415 rad | 6.2799002495 N·m |

- FIRST DISPATCH END-TO-END: control sequence **3449**, monotonic **614.836674190 s**. Steering's first step **23.9×0.005=0.1195 rad** becomes native **−0.1194542229 rad** before packing. Rear speed **−0.0178023584 rad/s** contributes **+0.0008901179 N·m**, so the second clamp reduces **6.2808901179→6.28**. Current conversion requests **−20.9333325015 A**, encoded **−20.933 A**. Executing the original functions with these recorded inputs and **mock transport** reproduces transformation/encoded values within **1e-12** and reconstructs **`0x502#7AE78000520A4800`** steering and **`0x101#C03BAEFFFF`** rear payloads. These bytes are **reconstructed, not captured raw CAN**. A separate mock bypassing policy with steering **2.8 rad** still hits the callback's ±90° cap; directly calling original `_send_mit(2.8)` produces a travel-bounded encoded joint target **1.5707942321 rad**. These are software-path tests, not new hardware experiments.

## 68. Fresh bag proof that pre-disable limits actually bound commands, and evidence boundaries (2026-10-13; recorded 2026-10-14)

- INDEPENDENT RE-READ: checked the §61 original MCAP's **39,914,531 bytes / SHA-256 `24be09c76b25d338c58ca6ae3d95bd468107a4afb4fa5b38139a1d8e568c3e78`**, parsed its structure and freshly deserialized `/trackstand/raw_action`, `/trackstand/motor_command` and `/trackstand/motor_control` using installed ROS serializers. All **112,856 topic message counts**, start/duration and metadata agree. The file has **49 uncompressed chunks with stored CRC=0**, so there are **no available chunk CRCs to validate**; earlier “CRC-checked reader” wording describes configured validation, not independent verification of nonexistent stored checksums. This follow-up's integrity evidence is the hash, structural/deserialization checks, metadata agreement and independent value comparisons.
- OBSERVED BINDING, NOT JUST CONSTANTS: on **894 enabled policy commands**, steering clipping changes **594**; each recorded steering ROS command equals `clip(1.57*raw,+/-pi/2)` and rear equals `6.28*raw`, **exactly**. On **3,022 successful paired motor-send ticks**, pre-damping rear clipping changes **1,141**, final clipping changes **533**, and steering slew is flagged active on **2,087**. Preclip/damping/final equations reconstruct with **zero error**. The backend's `steering_target_clipped` flag changes **zero** ticks because steering was already limited by the policy; a false downstream flag is **not evidence the upstream restriction was disabled**. Counts at 50 Hz and at motor-dispatch ticks have different denominators.
- SLEW / WIRE CHECKS: all **3,019 consecutive within-episode transitions** satisfy the pre-encoding **23.9 rad/s / 0.239-rad** step bounds; maximum floating-point excess **1.11e-16 rad**. Encoded steering ranges **−1.314259556…+1.570794232 rad**, inside ±90°; encoded rear torque **−6.279900250…+6.279900250 N·m**. Maximum steering/C0 encoding deviations **4.577303984e-5 rad / 0.0001499593 N·m**. Native steering `zero−q_send`, signed torque-to-current and encoded current-to-torque reconstruct with **zero error**. The first three steering targets in attempts 1/2 are **0.1195/0.2552894513/0.4240569113** and **0.1195/0.2698999037/0.4016146918 rad**; attempt 3 starts **0.1049271825/0.2273806489/0.3604120880**, because its measured initialization is slightly negative, not because slew is off.
- STRONG SECOND-CLAMP EXAMPLE: at **6.265894526 s**, sequence **3463**, ROS rear request **14.1965955162 N·m** is stored as **6.28**; speed **−15.7592759480 rad/s** adds **+0.7879637974**, producing **7.0679637974** before the final clamp, then **6.28 N·m**. This separates the two rear clamps rather than assuming one makes the other redundant. All fields in the existing [3,022-row active-command CSV](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/active_motor_commands.csv), including NaNs and timestamps, match fresh bag deserialization exactly; calculated columns in [policy_samples.csv](deploy/experiments/reports/deployment_20261012_180607/policy_samples.csv) alone were not treated as proof of actual ROS publication.
- VERDICT / PROOF SCOPE: **all three restrictions were effective in the pre-change host command path and this recording**, not dead/unreached code or nominal configuration alone. There is no later normal-path replacement of protected values with raw requests. This does **not** prove actual steering never overshoots, physical velocity is capped by a target-slew rule, actual/shaft torque never exceeds a command cap, or that every Pi file is cryptographically attested: raw CAN captures and installed-source/parameter dumps are absent. Host-send success is not per-frame firmware execution acknowledgement; current-derived feedback is not calibrated shaft torque. The proof is specific to the audited snapshot/recording, not unrecorded trials or successful balance.
- ARTIFACTS / STATE: the read-only trace created temporary `trace.py`, `data.npz`, `labels.json` and `verification.json` under **`/tmp/trackstand_prelimit_trace.7t351a/`**, not a new repository report. ## 70. Raw-action meaning, correct scales and genuine excessive outputs (2026-10-14; recorded 2026-10-15)

- NETWORK / UNIT CONTRACT: inspected original **09221945/model_91850**: **13→32→32→2**, ELU between hidden layers, **unbounded final Linear**, no tanh/sigmoid/action normalization; all saved actor tensors finite. State keys are `distribution.std_param` and `mlp.{0,2,4}.{weight,bias}`, with **no observation-normalizer state**. Installed RSL-RL **5.0.1** samples the unbounded Gaussian during PPO training but returns its mean unchanged for deterministic evaluation/export. The [exporter](isaaclab_train/scripts/deployment/export_rsl_rl_onnx.py) does **not** bake in action scales. Together with the saved `clip_actions: null` / action `clip: null`, this proves the task's “each in [-1,1]” docstring is not true of this model; §65's scaling-versus-normalization distinction remains valid.
- SCALE INTERPRETATION: raw `[a_s,a_d]` is **dimensionless**; steering target is **`1.57*a_s` rad**, rear feed-forward effort **`6.28*a_d` N·m**, applied once outside ONNX. The factors are the trained interface, not a mathematical bound or universal physical constants. Keeping this checkpoint unchanged requires preserving that interpretation; dropping the factors changes the controller rather than repairing it. **1,500° = 26.17994 rad → raw steering 16.67512024**; **400 N·m → raw rear 63.69426752**, not raw degrees/N·m. Incorrectly treating raw as physical-unit commands still leaves excessive requests. Post-hoc scaling/tanh on 91850 is not an equivalent bounded policy.
- REPLAY / IDENTITY: fresh CPU replay of the existing **894** observation rows reproduces raw outputs with maximum error **3.814697266e-6** (ONNX) / **1.525878906e-5** (original checkpoint), confirming §61 rather than relying on a different actor. Deployment and MuJoCo copies of `trackstand_rl_rsl_2026-09-22_19-45-06_model_91850.onnx` have identical SHA-256 **`9232a0723df8806e8b5f6fb4e39a7a11ccb5af8bf058d9026eddb57dab8fd806`**; checkpoint SHA-256 **`f77aadf7c021a1d20603a636daf757601dca2fe4faf264d5bba99c2dcf2b5c5e`**. Wrong export, missing normalizer, duplicated scale or stochastic exploration during deployment are not supported explanations.
- ARCHIVED-SIMULATION / REAL-BAG COMPARISON: re-read the [2026-10-01 Isaac experiment](isaaclab_train/reports/motor_limits_09221945_91850_2026-10-01/README.md), **64 envs ×3,000 steps, seed 42, full DR**, not a new Isaac evaluation. Raw absolute maxima are **2.642248869/21.01949501** (steer/rear) there versus **25.02981186/106.2511749** in the bike bag. Scaled maxima **237.68184°/132.00243 N·m** versus **2251.54105°/667.25738 N·m**; steering travel exceedances **1,265/192,000 (0.658854%)** versus **594/894 (66.4%)**, rear effort exceedances **171/192,000 (0.0890625%)** versus **341/894 (38.1%)**. New extreme-threshold counts in the bag: **40/894 (4.47%)** above 1,000° steering and **42/894 (4.70%)** above 400 N·m rear; **zero** of either in the archived Isaac samples. These are different operating datasets, not matched failure probabilities. All 171 excessive Isaac rear requests were startup actions; the other **191,829** samples have maximum rear request **2.76509 N·m**. Thus repeated extreme demands are not representative of that archived balancing trajectory, while the already-recorded ideal-zero **143.886°/105.004 N·m** startup is genuine learned behavior, not necessarily a sensor fault.

## 71. Complete Trackstand-Balance-v0 action-to-physics flow and torque-estimate boundary (2026-10-14; recorded 2026-10-15)

- TRACED IMPLEMENTATION: current task action/robot configuration, saved run parameters, `play_eval.py`, local Isaac Lab **7.2.0** `JointAction`, `ActionManager`, `ImplicitActuator`, and the PhysX articulation backend. Steering is an **absolute joint-position target relative to default joint position 0**, not an incremental action; rear is feed-forward joint effort. The nominal enabled path is:

```text
13 observations -> actor (Gaussian sample in training; deterministic mean in evaluation)
 -> RslRlVecEnvWrapper.step: clip_actions=None, leave raw [a_s,a_d] unchanged
 -> ActionManager.process_action: store raw, split steering/rear terms
 -> JointAction.process_actions: processed = raw*scale + offset
      steering q_target = default_joint_pos(0) + 1.57*a_s
      rear tau_ff = 6.28*a_d; action clips=None, no target clip or target slew
 -> JointPositionAction / JointEffortAction.apply_actions
 -> articulation position/effort target buffers
 -> ImplicitActuator.compute:
      diagnostic tau_s = kp*(q_target-q) - kd*steering_speed
      diagnostic tau_d = tau_ff - rear_damping*rear_speed
      diagnostic applied_effort = clip(computed_effort, configured effort limit)
      RETURN ORIGINAL position/velocity/effort action UNCHANGED
 -> articulation staging buffers -> PhysX:
      set_dof_position_targets(q_target), set_dof_velocity_targets(default 0)
      set_dof_actuation_forces(tau_ff), not the clipped diagnostic estimate
 -> physics integration / configured drive, speed and joint-travel constraints
 -> updated state -> reward / termination / reset -> next observations
```

- RATES / NOMINAL SETTINGS: **50 Hz** policy, **400 Hz** physics, **8** substeps per held action. Steering kp/kd **10/0.2**, configured effort/speed **5.6 N·m / 23.9 rad/s**, physical joint travel approximately **±π/2**; rear stiffness/damping **0/0.05**, configured effort/speed **6.28 N·m / 61.6 rad/s**. DR varies gains; it does not create an action clip. The action-rate reward is a soft penalty, not a hard bound. Actual motion constraints are not equivalent to clipping the requested position or imposing a host target slew.
- VERIFIED / UNVERIFIED FORCE BOUNDARY: independent NPZ recheck gives **zero** float32 error for processed targets versus scaled raw actions and **zero** error between held rear target and submitted PhysX effort buffer. That buffer reaches **132.002426 N·m**, while diagnostic maxima are only **5.599999905/6.280000210 N·m**. As already cautioned in §§44/58, this proves no equivalent Python feed-forward clamp, **not** that total solver torque actually equals 132 N·m or that all total torque is capped at 6.28. Configured PhysX maximum-force settings and clipped `applied_torque` estimates alone are not a measurement of total implicit-plus-external solver torque. The ordinary evaluator torque graph must not be treated as such proof. No new Isaac dynamics run or measurement of solver torque was performed in this follow-up.

## 72. Complete MuJoCo action-to-actuator flow: three restrictions remain active (2026-10-14; recorded 2026-10-15)

- ACTUAL VIEWER PATH: [run_deploy_viewer.py](mujoco_sim2sim/scripts/run_deploy_viewer.py) → `DeployedPolicy.act_batch` → viewer environment group → [BikeBalanceEnv.step](mujoco_sim2sim/src/newbike_mujoco/env.py). ONNX returns the same raw means, without an output squash/normalization. For the augmented trackstand model, the nominal path is:

```text
13 observations -> ONNX raw [a_s,a_d], unchanged -> BikeBalanceEnv.step at 50 Hz
 steering:
   desired = clip(1.57*a_s, +/-1.5708 rad)
   q_cmd += clip(desired-q_cmd, +/-23.9*0.02 rad)  [0.478 rad/update]
   data.ctrl[steering] = q_cmd
   -> engine control-range clip +/-1.5708 rad
   -> PD = 10*(effective_ctrl-q) - 0.2*steering_speed
   -> engine force-range clip +/-5.6 N m
 rear:
   data.ctrl[rear] = 6.28*a_d  [stored value may remain enormous]
   -> engine control-range clip +/-6.28 N m
   -> effective_ctrl - 0.05*rear_speed
   -> engine force-range clip +/-6.28 N m
 -> 8 physics steps at dt=0.0025 s; after each, explicit speed-state clips:
      steering +/-23.9 rad/s, rear +/-61.6 rad/s
 -> observations / termination / reset
```

- COMPILED / MEASURED CHECK: invoked the viewer's existing augmentation **in memory**, compiled the unchanged verified XML, and checked gear **1** for both joints, control-limited **true/true**, ranges **±1.5708 rad / ±6.28 N·m**, force-limited **true/true**, ranges **±5.6/±6.28 N·m**. Nominal gain/bias coefficients reproduce the laws above; DR randomizes gains, not these ranges. A direct static actuator probe deliberately bypassed the Python target processing to test the engine: stored controls **[1,000 rad, 400 N·m]** remain unchanged, but `mj_forward` reports steering torque **5.6 N·m** and rear torque **6.28/3.28/6.28 N·m** at rear speeds **0/+60/−60 rad/s**. These are simulated actuator forces, not hardware measurements. `data.ctrl` is therefore not the post-clamp torque; unit gear makes `actuator_force` joint torque here.
- IMPORTANT QUALIFICATION OF THE LIMIT DISCUSSION: §65 compares deployment with the **Isaac action-processing path**, not with every simulator's complete actuation. **MuJoCo retains all three restrictions recently disabled in deployment:** steering target clip, target slew, and rear saturation before/after damping; it additionally force-limits steering and directly clips simulated joint velocities. Removing those host restrictions does **not** reproduce the current MuJoCo path, and the historical 200 Hz deployment slew is not temporally identical to MuJoCo's 50 Hz slew. Simulator success is compatible with extreme raw requests because downstream actuation differs; it does not establish literal 1,000°/400 N·m commands as achievable. No model-geometry change, limit restoration or runtime edit was made by this audit.

- NEW DIAGNOSTIC EXPERIMENT — DISTINCT FROM ARCHIVED ISAAC RESULTS: ran model 91850 through the **current viewer augmentation and actual environment/policy classes**, in memory/headless, **one episode per seed 42–49 per mode**, stopping at termination or **1,500 steps / 30 s**, with current DR off/on and normal observation noise. Compiled the existing `trackstand_rl_0916_bike/trackstand_rl_0916.xml`; did not call the loader that writes an augmented XML, open a GUI/CAN socket, edit geometry/parameters, or rerun the original Isaac evaluator. Runtime versions: **MuJoCo 3.12.0, Torch 2.12.1+cu130, ONNX Runtime 1.23.2**; CPU inference. All **16** one-episode cases are retained, including failures, rather than selecting only survivors.

| Seed | DR off: policy steps / outcome | DR on: policy steps / outcome |
|---|---|---|
| 42 | 390 / fallen | 53 / fallen |
| 43 | 180 / fallen | 1,500 / timeout |
| 44 | 1,233 / fallen | 1,500 / timeout |
| 45 | 509 / fallen | 60 / fallen |
| 46 | 261 / fallen | 46 / fallen |
| 47 | 1,215 / fallen | 1,500 / timeout |
| 48 | 545 / fallen | 20 / fallen |
| 49 | 127 / fallen | 60 / fallen |

- AGGREGATE RESULTS: off **0/8 timeouts, 4,460 samples**; on **3/8 timeouts, 4,739 samples**. Maximum scaled raw requests off **975.753°/105.90998 N·m**, on **1099.114°/142.82152 N·m**. Steering/rear request exceedances off **395/24**, on **434/26**; only DR-on has **2** steering samples >1,000°, and neither mode has rear >400 N·m. Excluding each episode's first step, rear exceedances off **16/4,452**, on **18/4,731**, maxima **67.57526/60.18467 N·m**. Maximum stored steering ctrl is **1.570799947 rad** in both modes despite huge raw targets; rear stored ctrl retains the respective 105.91/142.82 maxima. Observed maximum absolute `actuator_force` off **3.590834/5.842693 N·m**, on **5.415896/6.192037 N·m** (steer/rear). These force traces sample the **last physics substep of each policy interval**, not all-substep peaks; compiled bounds/static probes provide separate saturation evidence.
- SURVIVOR / GENERALIZATION CAVEAT: the three DR survivors (43/44/47) have maximum full-episode scaled steering requests **6.835796/6.448605/6.949322 rad**, rear **55.20330/89.02911/142.82152 N·m**. After startup their rear maxima are **3.572817/28.10675/3.959032 N·m**, with **0/3/0** rear exceedances. Thus even surviving trajectories need not have physically attainable literal raw requests. This small seed-dependent check is **not** a matched reproduction of every prior successful viewer run, evidence of universal transfer reliability/failure, proof that DR is better, or grounds to doubt the already-verified XML/USD geometry. Preserve the user's prior simulator-balancing verification alongside these newly measured outcomes.
- DIAGNOSIS / RECOMMENDATION: genuine unbounded actor outputs and nonidentical downstream actuator contracts are confirmed; repeated huge real-bike outputs are unlike the archived Isaac trajectory. Wheel-based vx/zero-vy, rear branch/reference discrepancies, field timing and post-fall stand support from §§62–64 plausibly drive different closed-loop inputs; their individual causal contributions remain unproved. Initial ideal-zero excess is learned and does not require a sensor error. Do **not** fix this by blindly removing scales/protection or post-hoc squashing the existing actor. Establish one physically safe, validated actuator/observation contract in training, MuJoCo and deployment, evaluate it, then retrain if bounded actions are required. Dependence on an oversized startup impulse is a hypothesis, not an established mechanism. No new hardware test, matched bounded-policy retraining or implemented remedy is claimed.
- PROVENANCE / RECORDING SCOPE: the preceding audit's **`/tmp/trackstand_raw_action_audit.CCjCiy/check_actions.py` / `summary.json`** contain the replay, archived-NPZ checks, compiled static probes, all rollout outcomes and source/model hashes; these are **transient**, not a committed repository report. Key results, conditions and complete flows are embedded here and in the concise ledger so persistence does not depend on `/tmp`. On **2026-10-15**, re-read these files, graph provenance and both records; verified unchanged recorded source/XML/checkpoint hashes and identical deployment/MuJoCo ONNX copies, then added only missing §§69–73 / corresponding text entries. Prior records, graph artifacts and staged user work are preserved. **This recording edits only root `progress_record.md` and `progress_record.txt`**; no fresh inference/dynamic simulation, package build, Pi installation, CAN command or runtime/configuration/model modification. The local MIT restrictions remain **commented out** as in §66; the graphs still show the earlier capped run, with no post-disable bike data analyzed.

DR still defaults off; observation white noise is independent. **41050** was saved with **100 Hz physics / decimation 2**, versus current **400 Hz / decimation 8** (§24); changing checkpoint/omitting `--dr` alone does not reproduce every saved setting.
- USER-REPORTED STANDARD EVALUATION, NOT A NEW RUN: the supplied 91850 terminal summary was **64 envs × 3,000 steps, 129 completed episodes**, reward **190.026**, mean/min/max/std episode lengths **1369.0 / 18 / 1500 / 236.4 steps**, mean **27.38 s**, timeout/fall **58.9%/41.1%**. Completed-episode death shares: timeout **58.9%**, yaw deviation **41.1%**, fallen/diverged/drifted/wheel-net **0%**. Mean/max/RMS roll **1.19°/7.71°/1.43°**, mean worst lean **4.09°**; drive mean/max **0.14/65.47 rad/s**, steer **0.65/24.09 rad/s**, drift **0.051/0.175 m**, yaw **12.14°/30.58°**, turn **40.9°/episode**. Thus that reported fall-rate field counts non-timeout endings, not exclusively the `fallen` term. Its complete CLI/DR provenance is not present in the pasted summary; do not invent it.
- IMPLEMENTED EARLIER: [play_eval.py](isaaclab_train/scripts/rsl_rl/play_eval.py) accepts **`--pure_survival`**, using [evaluation_options.py](isaaclab_train/scripts/evaluation_options.py) before `gym.make()`. Keeps **only `time_out` and `fallen`** (current 30 s / 30°); disables every other non-null termination, including yaw_deviation, drifted, wheel_net and diverged. Default behavior, observations, rewards, metrics and independent `--dr` switch are unchanged. Missing required survival terms cause a clear error; disabling divergence emits a warning, and the final summary labels the mode. ## 76. Current DR versus the CycleRL paper: related categories, different experiments (2026-10-15 → 16; recorded 2026-10-16)

- CROSS-ENGINE RECHECK: current Isaac reset events and MuJoCo `DR_*` constants retain the six categories recorded in §§11/21: per-body mass/inertia **×U(0.8,1.2)**; shared per-environment COM offsets **±0.005 m/axis**; **32 U(0.5,0.8)** friction buckets; independent controller stiffness/damping multipliers **U(0.9,1.1)**; initial roll **±0.10 rad**, roll rate **±0.25 rad/s**; constant episode gyro/gravity residuals **0.0002 rad/s / 0.002 g**. DR is opt-in, sampled per environment/reset, not a single draw imposed on every bike. Always-on per-step observation noise is separate. Matching categories/ranges is **not** identical random sequences or identical engine contact mechanics: Isaac has static/dynamic material properties, while MuJoCo applies the selected static bucket to its sliding-friction coefficient. This tire/ground friction is also distinct from motor-joint `friction` below.
- PAPER EVIDENCE: local [cyclerl.pdf](isaaclab_train/papers/cyclerl.pdf), **arXiv:2603.15013v2, 3 May 2026, printed p. 4 / Table I**, describes mobile balancing/tracking, not our stationary task. Its dynamics/noise terms: total mass **U(15,45) kg**, COM **height U(0.50,0.80) m**, tire friction **U(0.5,1.2)**, actuator **force gain U(0.9,1.1)**, observation noise **N(0,(rho·s_max)²), rho~U(0.01,0.20)**. Its initial states: body velocity **U(1,2.5) m/s**, lean **±10°**, servo position **±20°**, hub speed **U(0,3) rad/s**. Task randomization: target velocity **U(1,5) m/s**, target heading **±10°**, each resampled after **U(3,5) s**. These are **not our DR terms/ranges**: absolute total mass/COM height are not per-body scales/3-axis offsets; force gain is not automatically the same as independent PD gains; relative observation noise is not our fixed white noise/episode residuals; our stationary task has no moving target-command randomization. The paper also uses **64 s / 3,200 steps at 50 Hz and 45° fall termination**, not our 30 s/30° envelope. No DR or task setting was changed by this comparison.
