## 79. Reduced motor equations, inertia and actuator-model choice (2026-10-15 → 16; recorded 2026-10-16)

- MODEL EXPLAINED, NOT IDENTIFIED: a reduced averaged brushless/FOC model with approximately zero d-axis current can be written `Lq*diq/dt = vq - Rs*iq - Ke*omega`, `tau_e = Kt*iq`, `J*domega/dt = tau_e - b*omega - tau_load`, `dtheta/dt = omega`. These separate electrical/current dynamics, torque production, passive mechanical resistance/inertia and kinematics. They are an approximation, not an exact firmware implementation or a fitted model of this bike. All quantities must refer consistently to rotor or output shaft; phase-to-phase datasheet electrical values cannot be inserted blindly as dq parameters. Under C0 the firmware closes a current loop; a first-order lag/deadtime approximation would need identification, not substitution of a sine phase lag as a universal delay. Protocol Rev.3.09b0 printed **p. 13** defines C0 as signed **0.001 A** Q-current with torque=current×constant; **p. 23** shows the current-control chain, not an outer `-0.05*speed` controller. Bare C0 does not make physical resistance zero, nor establish its numerical value.
- WHERE `J` COMES FROM: PhysX uses the articulated bodies' USD masses, COMs, inertia tensors/principal axes and joint transforms to build coupled dynamics; `ImplicitActuator` does not infer rotor inertia from its torque/speed ratings. The active [physics.usda](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/assets/payloads/Physics/physics.usda) contains explicit mass/inertia (rear mass **0.12332977 kg**, principal diagonal **0.00017797352 / 0.000178 / 0.0003290265 kg·m²**). An individual principal component is **not automatically the complete effective joint `J`**. Optional `armature` adds unrepresented joint-space inertia; a rotor behind an ideal ratio `N=omega_rotor/omega_joint` contributes `N²*J_rotor`, avoiding double counting. Current bicycle actuator config does not explicitly set armature or joint friction; `None`/omission inherits defaults and does not prove either zero inertia or zero passive losses. The already-verified geometry was not changed or challenged.
- ACTUATOR CHOICE QUALIFIED: the task currently uses `ImplicitActuatorCfg` for both motors. It is a valid solver PD/effort model; **stiffness=0 and damping=0 still permits feedforward torque control**. Steering implicit PD is a reasonable MIT baseline, not a demonstrated best hardware-response model. Explicit `IdealPDActuator` gives direct software PD/torque clipping; `DCMotor` adds a linear four-quadrant torque-speed envelope, **not** the complete four-equation electrical model; delayed/learned variants require appropriate delay/data. A class switch alone does not identify current-loop dynamics, physical friction, sampled host damping or loaded bandwidth. No comparative experiment proves another actuator class superior for these motors; retain old checkpoint settings for reproduction and validate/retrain a revised model separately.

## 80. Controller damping versus passive friction: correction to earlier deployment advice (2026-10-16)

- FUNDAMENTAL DISTINCTION: actuator `stiffness=Kp` and `damping=Kd` belong to the controller: `tau_request=Kp*(q_target-q)+Kd*(omega_target-omega)+tau_ff`. Passive viscous resistance belongs to the mechanical plant: `J*domega/dt=tau_actual-b*omega-tau_load`; requested and actual electromagnetic torque may differ through current-loop dynamics/saturation. `Kd` and `b` have the same rotational SI units **N·m·s/rad**, but are **not the same physical parameter**. At zero target velocity in an ideal unsaturated model they have the same algebraic `-coefficient*omega` form; that does not establish equal implementation. For example, `clip(controller torque)-b*omega` differs from `clip(controller torque-b*omega)`.
- CURRENT CODE / DOCUMENTED INTENT: [bicycle_cfg.py](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/bicycle_cfg.py) sets rear **stiffness=0, damping=0.05**, commented **“light bearing friction; tune”**. Given that intended meaning, placing a passive `b` in controller `Kd` conflates controller and plant. A conceptually separated pure-torque model would set rear **stiffness=0, damping=0, viscous_friction=b_measured**, with any Coulomb friction modeled separately. **No measurement establishes that this bike's `b` is 0.05.** This is a modeling correction/recommendation, not a completed config change, calibrated model or proven cause of deployment failure.
- IMPORTANT CORRECTION TO §44 / TEXT ENTRY 2026.10.7: matching the training term algebraically by copying **`-0.05*rear_speed` onto the Pi is not automatically physically correct**. If the simulation term was meant to represent natural motor/bearing drag, the real mechanism already has that resistance; host subtraction adds virtual braking and can double-count it. If it was an intentional controller, it should instead remain explicitly modeled/implemented as such, with passive losses accounted for separately. Illustratively, real `b=0.05` plus host `Kd=0.05` gives **0.10** total linear resistance in the simplified unsaturated model—**not a measurement of the bike**. All deployment backends still actively apply their existing rear damping; MIT source still calculates `-DRIVE_DAMPING_NM_S_PER_RAD*reported_speed`, with coefficient **0.05**. No removal was requested or performed.
- STEERING / NEXT DECISION: requested MIT **Kd=0.2** is a genuine controller setting and belongs in simulated controller damping. Independently identified passive `b` belongs in `viscous_friction`; do not arbitrarily inflate Kd or duplicate already represented losses. Fit losses with controlled current/speed/load measurements, distinguishing static/Coulomb resistance and electrical braking; then compare matched control/plant models under guarded tests. Moving `.05` into friction or deleting host drag changes saturation behavior and closed-loop dynamics, so existing **41050/91850** compatibility is not guaranteed. No on-bike A/B, friction identification, retraining or demonstrated balancing remedy has occurred.
- FRICTION MAPPING (modern Isaac Sim/PhysX): `friction` is the **static/breakaway effort threshold** `tau_s`; `dynamic_friction` is moving **Coulomb resistance** `tau_c`; `viscous_friction` is **`b`**. Moving mechanics become `J*domega/dt=tau_e-b*omega-tau_c*sign(omega)-tau_load`. At rest, ideal static friction can balance net driving torque up to `tau_s`; it is not a fixed extra `tau_s` always added to moving Coulomb friction. PhysX transitions through an impulse-based solver. Static/dynamic efforts use **N·m** for angular joints in the newer model; old Isaac Sim 4.5 used a dimensionless load-dependent static coefficient, while the installed docs specify effort semantics for **5.0+**. Raw USD angular coefficients may use per-degree units; use the correct API's units, not an unconverted numeric copy. Evidence: installed `actuator_base_cfg.py` and [PhysX joint-friction documentation](https://docs.omniverse.nvidia.com/kit/docs/omni_physics/107.3/dev_guide/rigid_bodies_articulations/articulations.html#articulation-joint-friction).

## 81. Complete installed implicit-actuator configuration and meaning of its warning (2026-10-16)

- LOCAL API, NOT AN ASSUMED ONLINE VERSION: the installed `ActuatorBaseCfg` plus `ImplicitActuatorCfg` define **12 unique configuration fields**, including inherited friction fields. `class_type` defaults to the implicit implementation; explicitly provide joint selection and stiffness/damping (those gains can be `None` to inherit USD values). Numeric fields accept a scalar or joint-regex dictionary; optional `None` means inherit/resolve defaults, **not necessarily zero**.

| Field | Meaning in this installed implicit-actuator path |
|---|---|
| `class_type` | Implementation class/type string; implicit default is supplied. |
| `joint_names_expr` | Joint-name/regex list selecting this actuator group. |
| `stiffness` | Controller P gain, N·m/rad for rotational SI coordinates. |
| `damping` | Controller D gain, N·m·s/rad; not passive motor `b`. |
| `effort_limit_sim` | Configured solver drive effort bound; retain §71's total-torque evidence caveat. |
| `velocity_limit_sim` | Solver velocity constraint; not direct state clipping or a guaranteed overshoot-free motor curve. |
| `effort_limit` | Compatibility alias for implicit effort limit; prefer `_sim`. |
| `velocity_limit` | Legacy field ignored when supplied alone for implicit actuators; use `_sim`. |
| `armature` | Additional joint-space inertia, kg·m² for rotational joints. |
| `friction` | Static friction; version-dependent coefficient/effort semantics (§80). |
| `dynamic_friction` | Moving Coulomb friction effort in the modern model. |
| `viscous_friction` | Passive speed-proportional resistance coefficient. |

- LIMIT/OWNERSHIP DETAILS: inconsistent simultaneous alias/`_sim` values raise an error. Physics-friction fields are inherited configuration, not special PD formula inputs. Joint travel limits, link mass/tensors, action scaling, policy/physics rates, electrical `R/L/Kt/Ke` and runtime targets are **not additional `ImplicitActuatorCfg` fields**. Articulation-provided constructor arguments (joint IDs/names, environment count, device and USD defaults) are not extra user motor-model parameters. Online development branches have evolved names/ownership; do not silently mix their schema with this installed API. Full field list verified against `/home/lmbike/IsaacLab/source/isaaclab/isaaclab/actuators/actuator_base_cfg.py` and `actuator_pd_cfg.py`.
- WARNING INTERPRETATION: the installed docstring says the class “should not be used,” but its broad literal wording conflicts with the supported articulation integration and official examples. The defensible practical interpretation is **do not treat this Python class as a standalone target-to-torque controller**. The articulation installs configured implicit gains in PhysX (`articulation.py`, implicit branch), forwards position/velocity/effort targets, and the solver executes the drive. `ImplicitActuator.compute()` returns the incoming command unchanged while calculating approximate torque diagnostics; it does not replace the command with that estimate. In contrast, `IdealPDActuator.compute()` replaces commands with calculated/clipped effort and clears position/velocity targets; PhysX drive gains are zeroed for that explicit path to avoid double PD. Thus the warning **does not invalidate our `ImplicitActuatorCfg`, disable its gains or require manual USD editing**. The newer [development API description](https://isaac-sim.github.io/IsaacLab/develop/source/api/lab/isaaclab.actuators.html#implicit-actuator) describes backend-driven control/approximate telemetry without that blanket caution; no undocumented historical author intent is inferred.

## 83. Current MuJoCo actuator construction and separately configured passive losses (2026-10-16)

- NO ISAAC-STYLE ACTUATOR CLASS LAYER: for the Trackstand XML path, [augment_trackstand_model()](mujoco_sim2sim/scripts/run_deploy_viewer.py) adds native **`steering_act` / `drive_act`** with `MjSpec.add_actuator()`, fixed gains, affine bias and control/force bounds. `MjSpec`/`MjModel` are model-building/storage objects, not alternatives to Isaac's `ImplicitActuator`/`DCMotor` implementation classes. [BikeBalanceEnv](mujoco_sim2sim/src/newbike_mujoco/env.py) is the whole environment: its `step()` scales/processes actions, writes `data.ctrl`, and calls `mj_step()`; MuJoCo evaluates the configured force law. `ViewerEnvGroup` groups environments, not actuators. The ordinary loader writes an augmented XML; read-only verification instead calls the existing augmentation and compiles **in memory**, avoiding that file-writing path.
- COMPILED SOURCE CHECK (nominal, DR off): recompiled the unchanged [0916 XML](mujoco_sim2sim/models/trackstand_rl_0916_bike/trackstand_rl_0916.xml) with the actual viewer augmentation under **MuJoCo 3.12.0**. Both actuator gears are **1**, both `dyntype=mjDYN_NONE`, model activation-state count **0**, timestep **0.0025 s**. Steering gain/bias begin **`[10,0,0] / [0,-10,-0.2]`**, ctrl **±1.5708 rad**, force **±5.6 N·m**; rear **`[1,0,0] / [0,0,-0.05]`**, ctrl/force **±6.28 N·m**. Thus the **0.2/0.05 are actuator velocity-feedback terms inside force saturation**, not separately authored passive joint damping. Actual action and saturation flows remain in §72; current target clipping/slew and explicit speed-state clamps remain active in MuJoCo, unlike the commented MIT host restrictions.

| Separately configured property | Steering | Rear |
|---|---:|---:|
| Compiled joint linear damping (`dof_damping`) | 0 | 0 |
| Joint dry friction (`dof_frictionloss`) | 0 | 0 |
| Additional joint inertia (`dof_armature`) | 0 | 0 |
| Native actuator passive `damping` coefficients in `MjSpec` | `[0,0,0]` | `[0,0,0]` |

- INTERPRETATION / LIMITS: zero extra armature does **not** mean zero mass/inertia; the verified body inertials still govern coupled motion. Zero configured passive motor/joint losses do not mean zero tire/contact dissipation or prove the real motor's losses negligible. No activation state means no identified electrical/current-loop response in these general actuators, **not instantaneous position tracking**; mechanics/PD still produce finite response. The real MIT tests in §§46–47 establish frequency-dependent attenuation/phase and current-derived torque, but no matched MuJoCo replay has quantified response error. The implementation is an idealized controller/plant baseline, not a validated real-motor match or grounds to replace motors/doubt verified geometry. Physical loss values, equivalent firmware output limits and loaded response remain unverified.

## 84. Ways to separate MuJoCo passive damping from MIT controller Kd, with API checks (2026-10-16)

- RECOMMENDED NATIVE PATH: leave the steering controller's **Kp=10 / Kd=0.2** in actuator gain/bias parameters; assign independently identified passive resistance to the joint. In the existing augmentation, **after joint renaming and before compilation**, the appropriate form is `spec.joint("steering_joint").damping = [b_steer, 0.0, 0.0]`. The same joint property can be authored in MJCF or its linear compiled coefficient set through `model.dof_damping[dof_id]`. **Installed 3.12 `MjSpec` joint/actuator `damping` properties are length-three arrays** (linear/quadratic/cubic resistance); a scalar assignment in the earlier API probe raised `TypeError`, so do not assume the older scalar setter. `b_steer` is a measured/identified parameter placeholder, not an assumed numerical value. Programming the augmentation avoids modifying the original exported XML.
- ALTERNATIVE NATIVE PATH: installed MuJoCo also supports a **separate actuator passive `damping` property**, e.g. `spec.actuator("steering_act").damping = [b_steer, 0.0, 0.0]`. This is **not `biasprm[2]`**: it adds passive resistance outside actuator-force clipping, reflected through transmission **gear²**. Our gear 1 makes the same coefficient comparable at the joint; other gearing requires consistent shaft-reference units. Joint and actuator passive damping are alternative locations for the **same** identified loss; do not duplicate it in both. Native actuator damping need not appear in `model.dof_damping`: the synthetic probe below shows zero joint damping with nonzero passive force from the actuator property. Evidence: [joint/actuator XML reference](https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/3.12.0/XMLreference.html).
- CUSTOM PATH: `mjcb_passive`/`mujoco.set_mjcb_passive()` can add a custom state-dependent loss to `data.qfrc_passive`; add to, rather than overwrite, the standard passive-force vector. Useful for a loss law not covered by native settings, but unnecessary complexity for ordinary linear `-b*omega`. A custom callback was **not implemented or tested here**. See [passive-callback documentation](https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/APIreference/APIglobals.html#mjcb-passive). Naming differs across APIs: **Isaac implicit `damping` = controller Kd; our MuJoCo actuator `biasprm[2]` = negative controller velocity gain; MuJoCo joint/native-actuator `damping` = passive resistance**. The existing rear `-0.05` bias would need an explicit controller-versus-plant decision before relocating anything; simply adding another 0.05 would double-count the modeled term.
- FRESH SYNTHETIC API CHECK, NOT BIKE IDENTIFICATION: in a separate one-hinge model (sphere geom mass **1 kg**, radius **0.1 m**, controller **10/0.2**, target **2 rad**, actual angle **0**, speed **1 rad/s**, actuator cap **±5.6 N·m**), deliberately chose **test `b=0.05`**. Baseline actuator/passive/combined torque **5.6/0/5.6 N·m**. Joint damping, native actuator damping, and runtime `dof_damping` update each give **5.6/−0.05/5.55 N·m**. Joint/runtime paths report `dof_damping=0.05`; actuator-property path retains **0** there while `qfrc_passive=−0.05`. All four asserted cases passed `mj_forward()` checks without time stepping, GUI, policy or hardware. This verifies API availability and force placement, **not any bicycle parameter**. No repository model/configuration was modified.

## 85. Zero-target-velocity MIT permits lumped simulated damping only under stated assumptions (2026-10-16)

- USER'S ALGEBRA ACCEPTED / QUALIFICATION TO §80: if **omega_target=0**, a constant passive viscous term and controller D term combine exactly in the **ideal unsaturated** equation: `tau_net=Kp*e+tau_ff-Kd*omega-b*omega=Kp*e+tau_ff-(Kd+b)*omega`. Thus **`Kd_sim=Kd_MIT+b` is a valid explicitly lumped simulation approximation**, not inherently incorrect algebra. It assumes the same velocity coordinate/signal and timing, no separately duplicated passive `b`, and ideal torque response. For nonzero velocity target, using `(Kd+b)*(omega_target-omega)` additionally introduces **`b*omega_target`**, so the same simple equivalence no longer holds. This does not turn a physical loss into the actual motor's controller gain or establish a numerical `b`.
- SATURATION BOUNDARY: with `X=Kp*e+tau_ff`, separated mechanics use `tau_net=clip(X-Kd*omega, ±tau_max)-b*omega`; lumped implementation uses `tau_net=clip(X-(Kd+b)*omega, ±tau_max)`. They can differ even with **zero target velocity**. MuJoCo's joint/native-actuator passive resistance lies outside actuator `forcerange`, while velocity feedback inside the gain/bias force law is clipped. Distinct feedback filtering/sample rates/current-loop behavior can also invalidate treating the two paths as identical outside the ideal model; no firmware dynamics have been identified here.
- FRESH NUMERICAL CHECK OF THE PREVIOUS ILLUSTRATION: same synthetic hinge, **Kp=10**, position target/current **2/0 rad**, **omega=10 rad/s**, **Kd=0.2**, illustrative **b=0.05**, cap **5.6 N·m**. Separate path has pre-cap controller torque **18 N·m**, actuator **5.6**, passive **−0.5**, net **5.1 N·m**. Lumped **Kd=0.25** has pre-cap torque **17.5**, actuator/net **5.6 N·m**, no separate passive torque. Both `mj_forward()` cases passed the expected-force assertions. These **5.1 versus 5.6** results are a controlled saturation counterexample, not measured bike output or a prediction of balance. Combined with the four API cases above, **six synthetic cases passed**; no full dynamics/policy benchmark was run.
- PRACTICAL CONCLUSION: lumping is acceptable for a deliberately simplified unsaturated **simulation**, but separate controller/physical-loss parameters are preferable for our frequently saturating policy and sim-to-real diagnosis. Increasing **real Pi/MIT Kd** adds active braking atop the motor's already existing natural resistance; it does not merely represent that resistance. An arbitrary “little bit” is a tuning choice, **not identified passive damping**. No simulated Kd/b was changed, no Pi gain/damping was changed or removed, no hardware A/B performed and no calibrated model or balancing fix demonstrated. Retain old configurations for checkpoint reproduction and validate any revised model separately.

## 88. PhysX projected-effort sensor validated independently before bicycle evaluation (2026-10-16)

- MEASUREMENT GAP RESOLVED: the old Isaac `motor_traces.png` / `startup_detail.png` contain true steering motion but **computed/clipped implicit torque estimates**, not measured rear joint effort. Unlike `get_dof_actuation_forces()` (the submitted external effort), this projects the incoming joint wrench onto the active DOF. The new recorder reads it **after every physics step, before automatic reset**; diagnostic estimates are retained separately, not substituted for measurement.
- INDEPENDENT CHECK: a separate fixed-base, free **unit-inertia rotor**, no gravity/contacts/rigid-body damping, timestep **0.0025 s**, implicit Kp/Kd **10/0.2**, drive effort setting **5.6 N·m**, was tested in six cases: positive/negative feed-forward, implicit drive only, both together, oversized feed-forward, and saturated implicit drive. Across **24 physics samples**, projected effort agrees with **`J*(omega_after-omega_before)/dt`** within **7.62939453125e-6 N·m** maximum absolute error. Positive/negative **2 N·m** requests measure approximately ±2; external **20 N·m** measures approximately 20 despite the implicit-drive 5.6 setting; implicit-only saturation measures approximately 5.6. See [sensor_validation.json](deploy/experiments/reports/20261015_deployment_failure_analysis/isaac_actuation/sensor_validation.json). These are synthetic validation results, **not identified bike parameters or bicycle torque measurements**.
- IMPORTANT MEASUREMENT BOUNDARY: the recorded quantity is **solver-measured active joint-axis effort**, not an independent sensor isolating motor-only shaft torque from every constraint/limit contribution. MuJoCo's companion graph uses **actuator force**, a different measurement boundary. The sensor validation supports its stated interpretation; it does not justify equating all green torque curves across platforms or claiming literal delivery of the largest raw torque request.

These are target-range statistics, not simultaneous torque-speed feasibility or successful physical tracking.

| Mode | Steering OOB count /within-range share | Rear OOB count /within-range share |
|---|---:|---:|
| No DR | 128 /99.933333% | 128 /99.933333% |
| Full DR | 1,265 /99.341146% | 171 /99.910938% |

- STARTUP DISTINCTION: both no-DR channels exceed only on each observed episode's first action (**20 ms**). Full-DR rear likewise exceeds only on the **171** first actions; after them its maximum request is **2.765090 N·m** (no-DR **1.831738 N·m**). But full-DR steering has only **171** exceedances on the first action and only **266/1,265** at episode age **<0.5 s**; the latest occurs at **20.18 s**. Thus “most outputs are within bounds” is correct, but “most DR steering exceedances are spawn-only” is **not** supported by all environments. A plotted env-0 transient is not a population statistic.
- RECOMPUTATION METHOD / COUNTEREXAMPLES: age starts at zero, increments per 20-ms action, and resets to zero for the next sample when `done[t]` is true. ## 91. Raw-output ownership, intended versus actual contract, and no Isaac target clamp/slew (2026-10-16)

- EXACT CODE OWNERSHIP: [play_eval.py](isaaclab_train/scripts/rsl_rl/play_eval.py) obtains deterministic `policy` at **line 204**, then produces raw actions with **`actions = policy(obs)` at line 270**, before `env.step(actions)`. Installed RSL-RL `models/mlp_model.py:101` calls **`mlp_output=self.mlp(latent)`**; `modules/mlp.py:64` constructs the final **`nn.Linear`**; `modules/distribution.py:182–184` returns `mlp_output` unchanged in deterministic mode. Project [rsl_rl_ppo_cfg.py](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/agents/rsl_rl_ppo_cfg.py) selects hidden dimensions **[32,32]**, ELU hidden activations, Gaussian exploration and no observation normalization. PPO learns checkpoint weights; RSL-RL implements the actor. The Isaac physics engine does **not** generate/normalize these raw policy values or intrinsically bound them to ±1.
- REMEMBERED TEXT VERSUS ENFORCEMENT: the user's remembered **“each in [-1,1] before scaling”** really exists at [task configuration](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/trackstand_bike/trackstand_rl_env_cfg.py) **line 76**, but is a docstring, not an executable constraint. Saved 91850 `agent.clip_actions=null`, both action `clip=null`; base `ManagerBasedRLEnv` declares **`Box(-inf,+inf)`**. The custom task `step()` forwards actions unchanged. Historical commit **`3c04693`**, `scripts/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl_env_cfg.py`, did explicitly set **`clip={"rear_wheel_joint":(-10.0,10.0)}` with scale 10** for an older rear **velocity-control** variant. That is not a raw ±1 bound for the 91850 position-steering/torque-drive configuration; the later hybrid comment also lacked an enforced bound.
- TWO CONTRACTS, NOT THE SAME CONTROLLER:

```text
CURRENT:  13 observations -> unbounded actor action (sample in training /mean in evaluation)
          -> no raw [-1,1] clip -> [1.57*a_s rad, 6.28*a_d N·m]
          -> platform-specific controller/constraints -> actual response
INTENDED: 13 observations -> normalized action explicitly constrained to [-1,1]^2
          -> [1.57*a_s rad, 6.28*a_d N·m], now bounded to ±1.57 rad /±6.28 N·m
          -> controller/physical limits -> actual response
```

- Scaling remains a **gain/unit mapping and exploration scale**, even with unbounded inputs; multiplication by a nonzero constant does not bound an unbounded domain. For example raw rear **0.5 →3.14 N·m**, **2 →12.56 N·m**. With raw initial Gaussian std **1**, the selected scales set initial sampled physical command deviations, not guaranteed maxima. Keeping a checkpoint fixed while dropping/changing scales changes its controller; “fit commands inside motor limits” requires an additional bound, not scaling alone.
- NO TARGET CLAMP / NO TARGET SLEW IN CURRENT ISAAC: the active steering `JointPositionActionCfg` scales by **1.57**, its conditional clip is disabled, and the joint-position target setter only stores the supplied target. No target-step/rate limit is imposed between policy updates. Approximately **±90° joint travel**, configured **23.9 rad/s joint velocity**, and **5.6 N·m implicit-drive effort** constrain the simulated motion/drive at other layers; they do not clamp or slew the requested position. Raw steering **2** still requests **3.14 rad ≈180°**. A bounded position target can also require excessive torque/speed to track quickly. This confirms §71; do not transplant MuJoCo's explicit target processing into a description of current Isaac behavior.

## 92. Available bounded-action methods, read-only tests and preferred future design (2026-10-16)

- SIMPLE BUILT-IN OPTION, NOT IMPLEMENTED: add **`clip_actions=1.0`** to `TrackstandRLPPORunnerCfg` or pass **`agent.clip_actions=1.0`** as a Hydra override to training/evaluation. Installed `RslRlBaseRunnerCfg` exposes the field; both `train.py` and `play_eval.py` pass it to `RslRlVecEnvWrapper`. Its `step()` executes **`torch.clamp(actions,-self.clip_actions,self.clip_actions)` before the task scales actions**. It bounds the actions entering the task for valid finite outputs, **not** the original network tensor returned by `policy(obs)` or standalone ONNX. A logger reading pre-wrapper raw actions can still see values outside ±1. NaN/nonfinite checks remain necessary; a bound is not a substitute for fault handling.
- READ-ONLY CPU PROOF: extracted/executed the installed wrapper's unchanged `step()` with a mock environment. Inputs **`[[2,-3],[-1,1],[0.25,-0.5],[1e6,-1e6]]`** become task inputs **`[[1,-1],[-1,1],[0.25,-0.5],[1,-1]]`**, while the original input tensor stays unchanged. For the first pair, task targets become **+1.57 rad /−6.28 N·m**. Independently tested the installed Gaussian with bounded mean **0.5**, std **1**, seed **42**, **100,000×2** samples: **75,104/200,000** exceed ±1. This proves that bounding a Gaussian mean alone does not bound stochastic training samples. These are synthetic CPU checks, not a simulator rollout, clipped-91850 performance comparison or hardware experiment.
- INTRINSICALLY BOUNDED POLICY OPTION, NOT IMPLEMENTED: a correctly integrated **tanh-squashed Gaussian** can return **`a=tanh(mu+sigma*epsilon)` in training**, **`a=tanh(mu)` in deterministic evaluation**, bounding valid finite returned actions to [-1,1]. The installed **RSL-RL 5.0.1** supplies ordinary `GaussianDistribution` / `HeteroscedasticGaussianDistribution`, not a ready-made squashed option. A project-local custom distribution can be selected through its qualified class path; probability/log-Jacobian, entropy/KL handling, numerical stability and deterministic/export semantics must match the transformed policy. Merely setting **`activation="tanh"` changes hidden layers only**. Low-level `MLP` has `last_activation`, but the current `MLPModel`/Isaac config does not expose it directly; a tanh mean followed by ordinary Gaussian sampling remains unbounded.
- EXPORT / CROSS-PLATFORM CONTRACT: the project's [ONNX exporter](isaaclab_train/scripts/deployment/export_rsl_rl_onnx.py) reconstructs Linear/ELU layers and returns the network unchanged; it does not export the wrapper's action clamp or infer a new final tanh from weights. A bounded controller must preserve its chosen mapping through ONNX, MuJoCo and deployment exactly once. Adding a tanh/clamp only during deployment is not equivalent to training under it. Neither bounded steering position nor bounded rear feed-forward guarantees all motor torque/speed/rate/latency constraints; retain explicit, validated actuator/plant models and protections.
- DESIGN VERDICT / NEXT EXPERIMENT: prefer the user's bounded normalized-action contract **for a newly trained, deployable controller**, because it makes command interpretation and platform limits explicit and prevents arbitrary demand magnitudes. This is a design recommendation, **not evidence that post-hoc clipping fixes the failed deployment**. First proposed controlled comparison is unchanged 91850 with/without pre-scale clipping under matched seed/DR/physics/terminations, then train/evaluate a bounded version with the same actuator contract across platforms. No such ablation, bounded-policy implementation, training/fine-tuning or hardware A/B has yet been performed; current on-disk task/runner raw-action clipping remains **None**. This disk audit does not establish which processes/configuration are live on the Pi.

## 94. Deployment investigation index and exact observation/action contract (recorded 2026-10-19)

- THREE MAJOR QUESTIONS, NOT FOUR: **Q1** compares motor/control/limit models across Isaac Lab, MuJoCo and the bike; **Q2** asks whether policy demands exceed motor limits; **Q3** asks whether observation differences explain output discrepancies. The subsequent wrapping, zero-reference, speed/angle tracing and channel tables are follow-ups within those questions, not a fourth completed investigation. Q1/Q2 findings are already recorded above; the previously unrecorded Q3 experiments were performed on **2026-10-16** and are consolidated below.
- EXACT ACTIVE INPUT: [balance_imu_state](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/mdp/stationary/observations.py) produces one **13-value** policy term, in this one-based order. The task explicitly selects `steering_joint` / `rear_wheel_joint`; the function's older default joint names are not the active configuration.

| # | Value | Meaning / unit |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `gravity_y` | Body-frame Y component of normalized gravity; dimensionless lean signal |
| 2 | `gravity_z` | Body-frame Z component of normalized gravity; dimensionless, −1 upright |
| 3–5 | `ang_vel_x/y/z` | Root-link angular velocity expressed in body axes; rad/s |
| 6–7 | `lin_vel_x/y` | Actual root-link forward/lateral velocity expressed in body axes; m/s |
| 8 | `steering_pos` | Steering joint position; rad |
| 9 | `drive_pos` | Native PhysX rear-joint position; rad, not `wheel_net_angle` |
| 10 | `steering_vel` | Signed steering joint velocity; rad/s |
| 11 | `drive_vel` | Signed rear joint velocity; rad/s |
| 12–13 | `sin_steer`, `cos_steer` | Sine/cosine of steering position; dimensionless |

- EXACT ACTIVE OUTPUT: raw actions are **two dimensionless values**, ordered **steering position demand, rear effort demand**. `JointPositionActionCfg` maps `a_s` to **`default_steering_pos + 1.57*a_s` rad**, with current default zero; this is not an incremental steering displacement. `JointEffortActionCfg` maps `a_d` to **`6.28*a_d` N·m**, not rear speed/position. These are requested targets, not measured response. The remembered raw `[-1,1]` phrase remains an unenforced docstring; §§91–92 already establish no current raw bound and no normalization hidden in the scaling. No new action restriction was implemented.

## 95. Question 3: observation mathematics agrees, but operating inputs and velocity semantics differ (2026-10-16; recorded 2026-10-19)

- PERMANENT REPORT: [03_observation_contract_comparison.md](deploy/experiments/reports/20261015_deployment_failure_analysis/03_observation_contract_comparison.md), [artifact/reproduction guide](deploy/experiments/reports/20261015_deployment_failure_analysis/question_3_observations/README.md), `summary.json`, `feature_distributions.csv`, `counterfactual_actions.npz` and six PNG/SVG/PDF figure bundles. All use **09221945/model_91850**, checkpoint/ONNX identities already recorded in §70. Fresh Isaac captures store exact actor inputs without extra observation-manager/RNG calls: **64 envs ×3,000 actions**, seed **42**, nominal and full DR, standard terminations, **50 Hz policy /400 Hz physics**, unchanged actions/plant; baseline raw actions/done flags match §89 **bitwise**. MuJoCo baseline captures use **64 independent seeds 42–105 ×3,000 actions**, current viewer augmentation and automatic resets, but only time-out/fallen termination. Different episode populations are not a matched engine-performance benchmark.
- MATCHED-STATE CHECK: executed the actual Isaac observation function, actual MuJoCo `observe()` and deployment `build_observation()` on **1,000 synthetic matched states**, rear angle inside the common ±π branch, noise/bias disabled. Isaac/MuJoCo all-channel maximum error **9.536743e-7**; deployment's other eleven channels, excluding vx/vy, match within the same error. Isaac/deployment **xyzw** versus MuJoCo **wxyz** ordering is handled correctly. MuJoCo free-joint angular qvel matches local `mj_objectVelocity` within **3.552714e-15 rad/s**. Float32 formula roundoff changes raw actions by at most **1.525879e-5 /3.814697e-5** (steering/rear) in these synthetic tests. Across baseline recorded inputs, ONNX replay error is at most **5.722046e-6**, checkpoint/ONNX disagreement at most **1.525879e-5**. No general quaternion/frame/unit/channel/export-gain bug was found in the tested paths; this is not a physical calibration of every IMU axis.
- KEY SEMANTIC MISMATCH: both simulations supply **true root-link body vx/vy**; deployment supplies **`0.07*rear_encoder_velocity` /0**. Wheel circumferential speed is not generally root-link velocity: slip, transients and frame motion matter. Even ideal frame kinematics give `v_root = v_rear_axle + omega_frame × (r_root-r_rear_axle)`; a no-lateral-slip contact constraint does not force every body point's lateral velocity to zero. This corrects any implication in §19 or the deployment observation docstring that thirteen matching channel names guarantee matching observations.
- BASELINE INPUT/OUTPUT EVIDENCE: simulator datasets each contain **192,000** policy frames; the bike has **894** frames from three corrected-sign attempts. OOB below means scaled demand outside **±π/2 rad /±6.28 N·m**, tolerance **1e-5**, not actual motor torque/travel. Bike data are **pre-limit-disable**, both wheels grounded/no manual disturbance, with user-reported kickstand contact **<1 s** after release; substantial data are supported failed motion, not free balancing.

| Dataset | Steering /rear demand OOB | Maximum absolute raw steering /rear |
|---|---:|---:|
| Isaac nominal | 0.066667% /0.066667% | 1.629161 /16.916941 |
| Isaac DR | 0.658854% /0.089063% | 2.642249 /21.019495 |
| MuJoCo nominal | 9.144792% /0.840625% | 12.875419 /38.026218 |
| MuJoCo DR | 8.941146% /1.247917% | 15.218390 /48.921734 |
| Corrected-sign bike | 66.442953% /38.143177% | 25.029812 /106.251175 |

- After excluding each episode's first **0.5 s**, Isaac DR OOB is **0.532142% /0%**, MuJoCo DR **7.084349% /0.386194%**, bike **65.808824% /37.132353%**. Different reset frequency is not the whole explanation. Absolute-input 95th percentiles, Isaac DR /MuJoCo DR /bike: gravity_y **0.0462/0.0893/0.4691**; roll rate **0.0370/0.8689/1.9185 rad/s**; yaw rate **0.0470/1.2258/2.6056 rad/s**; supplied vx **0.0083/0.5169/3.8687 m/s**, vy **0.0026/0.0930/0**; steering speed **1.3247/10.5961/20.2852 rad/s**, rear **0.1233/21.9412/55.2673**. Simulators largely reach different physical states, whereas deployment also changes the estimator. These are observed operating distributions, not proof that states never appeared during PPO training.
- OTHER OBSERVATION DIFFERENCES: both simulations retain gravity/gyro white noise **σ0.001 /0.0012 rad/s** without DR; DR adds per-episode residuals **0.002 /0.0002 rad/s**. Deployment adds no synthetic noise. Isaac sanitizes/clamps to **±100 before** manager white noise; deployment clamps the final vector; MuJoCo has no equivalent final guard. Captured values were finite/below these bounds, so that difference does not explain these samples. 91850 has no observation history/extra scaling, identity normalizer; Isaac's latency helper is dormant. Latest asynchronous bike messages are not a simultaneous physics snapshot. Earlier IMU/motor receipt-age and gyro/quaternion-skew findings remain unresolved sensor-timing issues, not proven axis/scale/firmware-delay measurements.

## 96. Question 3: velocity substitution alone catastrophically destabilizes the frozen policy (2026-10-16; recorded 2026-10-19)

- FIXED-STATE EVIDENCE: successful Isaac trajectories already show `0.07*rear_speed - true_vx` RMS **0.110111 nominal /0.123179 DR m/s**, maximum absolute **4.127392 /4.542484 m/s**. DR absolute-error p95 is only **0.002540 m/s**: the large transient errors are rare, not constant. DR true/estimated vx RMS **0.020637 /0.126617 m/s**, correlation **0.241307**. At **env 24 /action 3 /60 ms**, replacing vx/vy **(+0.180148,−0.219561)** with **(−0.425714,0) m/s**, all other eleven channels unchanged, changes raw steering/rear **(−0.329742,+0.179491) →(+9.122833,+40.185425)**; rear scaled demand **1.13→252.36 N·m**. This is a selected large offline same-input effect, not delivered torque or a physical alternate trajectory. Over all Isaac DR baseline inputs, combined replacement raises OOB **0.659/0.089%→0.996/0.278%**, raw maxima **2.64/21.02→11.56/40.19**; fixed trajectories understate subsequent feedback divergence.
- CONTROLLED ISAAC CLOSED LOOP: four actual evaluations, each **64×3,000**, seed **42**, same checkpoint/actions/physics/noise/DR/terminations. The experimental wrapper changes only **zero-based channels 5/6** to **`0.07*obs[:,10]` /0**, in process memory before manager noise. No runtime source edit. Nominal initial full input/action is bitwise identical; DR initial other eleven channels and physical states/bias match, but original initial vy can already differ from zero. The saved YAML describes the base cfg, not the runtime override: read `experiment.json`'s **`deploy_velocity_ablation`**, invocation and `capture_isaac.py` together.

| Isaac condition | Mean completed episode | Timeouts /completed | Steering /rear demand OOB |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Nominal original | 30.000000 s | 128/128 | 0.066667% /0.066667% |
| Nominal deployment vx/vy | **0.220000 s** | **0/17,408** | **72.733333% /54.533333%** |
| DR original | 27.379225 s | 76/129 | 0.658854% /0.089063% |
| DR deployment vx/vy | **0.262919 s** | **3/14,326** | **66.830208% /48.509375%** |

- Every completed nominal substituted episode lasts **11 actions /0.22 s** and fires `fallen`; its raw maxima are **10.358297 /17.688885**. DR substituted raw maxima **29.947264 /90.413086**; about **85.3% fallen /19.1% yaw-deviation**, overlapping terms. Three timeouts round to **0.000** in the printed rate, not literally zero successes. Evaluator aggregate `fall_rate` includes all non-timeout deaths; do not equate it with the `fallen` term alone.
- INDEPENDENT MUJOCO CLOSED LOOP: **8 matched seeds 42–49 ×3,000 actions/mode**, only vx/vy replaced. Nominal mean completed duration **7.855769→0.439321 s**, timeouts **2/52→0/1,090**, OOB **9.229167/0.812500%→83.562500/48.391667%**. DR mean **4.627059→0.326230 s**, timeouts **8/68→0/1,467**, OOB **9.383333/1.145833%→75.929167/43.733333%**. Final incomplete episodes are excluded, so populations differ; this is not a universal simulator benchmark. Saved graphs: `isaac_closed_loop_observation_ablation`, `closed_loop_observation_ablation`, `velocity_substitution_effect`, `isaac_velocity_mismatch_example` in the Q3 artifact directory.
- CAUSAL CONCLUSION: the observation substitution by itself is sufficient to generate instability and bike-sized excessive outputs in the otherwise unchanged Isaac plant, independently reproduced in MuJoCo. Observation-contract alignment is therefore a **high-priority experimentally supported transfer issue**, not merely distribution correlation. This does not prove it is the only bike cause or that correcting these channels alone fixes deployment; the bag has no independent body-velocity ground truth and the MuJoCo baseline gap already exists with true velocities.

## 97. Rear-angle representation and zero-reference mismatch: confirmed, not an established fix (2026-10-16 →19)

- TASK/SDK VERSUS ENGINE: Isaac's observation reads `asset.data.joint_pos` directly, **without an explicit ±π wrapper**. MuJoCo explicitly maps `(q+pi)%(2*pi)-pi`; deployment's `parse_a3_position` and observation builder explicitly wrap to **[-π,π)**. Comments claiming PhysX always returns ±π in `trackstand_rl_env.py`, stationary rewards/termination and MuJoCo `env.py` are **not supported for the installed tested task**. A prior “write 10, read about −2.5” observation does not identify ±π as the boundary. Historical comments/source are preserved; this entry records the correction rather than silently changing runtime code.
- EXISTING PHYSX PROBES: after baseline evaluation, a separate diagnostic writes six known joint states, clears old targets, advances one physics step and executes the actual observation term. [No-DR results](deploy/experiments/reports/20261015_deployment_failure_analysis/question_3_observations/isaac_no_dr_gpu/rear_angle_probe.json) below; DR behaves similarly. Immediate reads equal written values; after physics, task observation exactly equals the backend result.

| Written rear angle | After one no-DR physics step /actual observation |
|---|---:|
| +3.5 rad | +3.498796 rad |
| −3.5 rad | −3.500000 rad |
| +7.1 rad | −5.466371 rad |
| −7.1 rad | +5.463862 rad |

- The baseline DR actor inputs themselves include **4/192,000** rear angles above π, maximum **3.521434 rad**; nominal range **0–1.619191 rad**. Thus **neither strict ±π wrapping nor unrestricted cumulative turns is justified**. The probe is consistent with a ±2π/4π-period branch in those cases, not a fully established continuous multi-turn rule. Installed native tensor backend is compiled; the exact internal C++ branch formula was not available in this disk trace. Small state changes during the physics step are not an extra observation transformation.
- POLICY SENSITIVITY / 91850 ABLATION: ideal upright, otherwise fixed input at rear **+3.5** yields raw **(−0.462824,−0.054442)**; the physically equivalent **3.5−2π=−2.783185** branch yields **(+1.212733,+14.293247)**. Eight-seed MuJoCo candidate-4π mapping changes nominal mean completed duration **7.855769→6.744746 s**, timeouts **2/52→0/59**; DR **4.627059→4.486500 s**, **8/68→9/80**. Not a consistent improvement, nor a certified PhysX emulator. Fixed-record branch sensitivity is not proof that removing wrapping improves closed-loop balance.
- MULTI-TURN COUNTS ARE ALREADY READ: A3 int32 bytes **[3:7]** supply the cumulative count. Before wrapping, deployment computes **`theta=encoder_sign*(count-startup_zero_count)*(2*pi/16384)/gear_ratio`**, in radians, not raw counts; MIT I/O retains an internal unwrapped value while publishing the wrapped pair. Sending that unwrapped radian value to the actor would be the proposed “remove wrapping” change, not a new encoder acquisition method. Example +3.5 currently maps to −2.783185, versus +3.5 without the wrap. “Blindly removing” meant changing this contract only in deployment and assuming frozen-policy compatibility/improvement without validation—not claiming cumulative encoders are intrinsically wrong.
- ZERO REFERENCE IS SEPARATE: Isaac/MuJoCo episode reset restores joint position zero. Deployment rear A3 zero is latched once per motor-node startup and persists through RC down/up cycles; steering has its own MIT origin setup. Corrected bag attempt starts are **0 /−1.800510 /−2.082379 rad**. No isolated closed-loop RC-rezero experiment has established benefit. First attempt starts at zero and still fails. Prior fixed-record bike unwrapping/re-zero replays in §62 are not a hardware fix; do not combine or confuse branch selection and reference changes. A consistent unwrapped representation could be a new train/sim/deploy design, but is not automatically the frozen checkpoint's input contract.

- REPORT / METHOD: [wrapping_41050_no_dr/README.md](deploy/experiments/reports/20261015_deployment_failure_analysis/wrapping_41050_no_dr/README.md), `run.py`, `inspect_boundaries.py`, `verify.py`, [gallery](deploy/experiments/reports/20261015_deployment_failure_analysis/wrapping_41050_no_dr/index.html). Actual current viewer augmentation, `BikeBalanceEnv` and `DeployedPolicy`, not generic evaluator/model_500 defaults. **09171946/model_41050**, deterministic ONNX; only zero-based channel **8** differs: current **`(q+pi)%(2*pi)-pi`**, native unwrapped **q**, candidate **`(q+2*pi)%(4*pi)-2*pi`**. Actual body velocities, controller/caps/slew, signs, geometry and terminations unchanged. **50 Hz policy /400 Hz physics**, 30-s horizon, no DR but normal white noise; nominal tire friction **0.65**, floor **0.1**, contact solref **0.005/1.5**. No retuning to force success. Historical 41050 training used 100-Hz physics; this is its current 400-Hz MuJoCo evaluation.
- PERFECT NOMINAL BASELINE REPRODUCED: each mapping, **64 seeds 42–105 ×3,000 actions**, zero-phase resets, **192,000 frames**, **128/128 timeouts, zero falls, 30.000 s mean**, roll RMS **0.624590°**. Native rear stays **[−0.744467,0] rad**, zero ±π crossings/branch jumps and zero steering/rear demand OOB; raw maxima **0.309407 /0.044989**. Full observations/actions/qpos/qvel/roll/reset/termination arrays are **bitwise identical** across mappings. Correct interpretation: wrapping is inactive in this nominal operating region, not proven harmless when a boundary is crossed.
- DETERMINISTIC BOUNDARY STRESS, NOT THE ORIGINAL NOMINAL RESET OR DR: fixed initial rear phases **+2.8 /+3.5 /−3.5 rad**, root upright/other reset settings unchanged, **16 matched seeds 42–57**, first complete episode only, at most 30 s. Within each phase physical initial state is identical across encodings; no extra noise draws. Initial phase may affect contact but is held identical within each paired comparison. Cells show **mean seconds; timeouts/16**, remaining episodes fallen.

| Initial phase | Current ±π | Native unwrapped | Candidate 4π |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| +2.8 rad | **21.67375; 10/16** | 5.26500; 0/16 | 5.14875; 0/16 |
| +3.5 rad | **24.75375; 13/16** | 14.79375; 5/16 | 14.81375; 5/16 |
| −3.5 rad | **24.33125; 12/16** | 1.06625; 0/16 | 2.97875; 1/16 |

Entire physical state, other twelve channels and all preceding actions/states are bitwise identical; current mapping supplies **+3.132220**, alternatives **−3.150965 rad**. Raw steering **−0.320292 vs −0.397151**, rear **−0.248475 vs −0.011987**; scaled rear demand **−1.560 vs −0.0753 N·m**, not measured torque. Trajectories then diverge. First-difference invariants hold for **96 seed/phase/alternative comparisons**, saved in `results/first_encoding_differences.csv`.
- CONCLUSION / LIMITS: encoding **can strongly affect** commands/survival when branches differ, but **current wrapping outperforms removal and the candidate branch in these stress cases**. No evidence supports removing it as an automatic fix. Neither native MuJoCo cumulative q nor candidate-4π is a proven general Isaac replica. This different-checkpoint experiment does not settle 91850 deployment, timing/actuation/velocity-estimator errors, or isolated per-RC re-zeroing. Figures `nominal_wrapping_comparison`, `boundary_stress_comparison`, `boundary_crossing_detail` are PNG/SVG/PDF; twelve NPZ captures retain valid-mask-aware states/inputs/actions/outcomes. ## 99. Exact Isaac rear-speed and rear-position reading paths, not inferred from comments (recorded 2026-10-19)

- INSTALLED SOURCE TRACE: `/home/lmbike/IsaacLab/source/isaaclab_physx/isaaclab_physx/assets/articulation/articulation_data.py` **1014/1028** implements `joint_pos` /`joint_vel`, refreshing timestamped buffers with **`_root_view.get_dof_positions()` /`get_dof_velocities()`**. `ProxyArray.__getitem__` forwards to a zero-copy Torch view; no angle conversion there. Installed `omni.physics.tensors` **110.1.11** `api.py` **1639/1668** calls the compiled backend, documenting **radians /radians per second**, unlike raw USD degree attributes. These Python readers have no modulo/atan2 wrapper or position-difference speed estimator. The task's [observation source](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/mdp/stationary/observations.py) **95–101/110–140** resolves the rear index, selects native position/velocity, concatenates them, sanitizes nonfinite values to zero and clamps to ±100.

```text
PhysX get_dof_positions()  -> data.joint_pos[:,rear_idx] -> obs[:,8]  (angle, rad)
PhysX get_dof_velocities() -> data.joint_vel[:,rear_idx] -> obs[:,10] (speed, rad/s)
 -> nan/inf-to-zero, clamp(-100,+100)
 -> observation manager (rear position/speed noise sigma=0, no extra modifier/scale/history)
 -> RslRlVecEnvWrapper TensorDict -> actor (observation normalization disabled)
```

- SPEED CONCLUSION: no wrapping, no absolute value/sign flip, no subtraction of an initial reading, no angle differentiation or speed normalization. `.abs()` in motor-speed logging/evaluation is diagnostic, not the actor input. The previously executed read-only CPU task-function probe with mocked state inputs preserved speeds **±3.5 /±7.1 /±65.47 rad/s** and clipped **±120→±100**; not a physics experiment. Fresh disk recheck of stored policy inputs, each **192,000** samples: nominal speed range **−11.634274 to +60.462433 rad/s**, DR **−30.760651 to +65.473000**, actor-versus-clean speed difference **exactly zero**. Respectively **736 /907** speed values have magnitude above π; angle wrapping is not applied to speed. Configured **61.6 rad/s** solver velocity limit and **±100** observation clamp are separate from wrapping and need not bound transient measured speed identically.
- ANGLE CONCLUSION: no explicit application/SDK-Python wrap or per-read zero subtraction; the actor receives PhysX's coordinate, including the rebranching demonstrated in §97. Do **not** conclude “unlimited cumulative angle” merely because no Python modulo appears. `reset_scene_to_default` writes configured default joint positions/velocities, currently zero, at episode reset; that is a reset, not a per-sample angle wrapper. `TrackstandRLEnv.step` separately integrates **`wheel_net_angle += drive_vel*step_dt`** after its superclass step and zeros it at termination/reset. The wheel-net termination reads that accumulator with a one-step lag; **the policy's rear-position slot does not use it**. Dynamic observation code's wrapped steering and yaw/terrain wrap operations are not this active rear-position path. No production wrapping/reference change was made.

## 100. Observation-investigation next steps and unresolved real-bike evidence (recorded 2026-10-19)

- PRIORITY: for a new deployable policy, train/evaluate with the **same IMU/encoder-based estimator actually available on the bike**, or redesign inputs to omit privileged root vx/vy; true simulated velocities can remain reward/diagnostic ground truth. Dropping channels changes input dimension and requires a new trained/exported policy. Blindly replacing frozen 91850 inputs or appending output clipping/tanh is not a validated remedy. Keep Q1/Q2 actuator/saturation/delay alignment as a separate controlled factor; observation substitution does not explain the entire true-velocity Isaac–MuJoCo gap.
- ESTIMATOR VALIDATION, NOT NEW POLICY SENSOR AUTHORITY: with policy disabled/bike supported, use synchronized video/marked-wheel/known-distance motion as **external validation ground truth**, while runtime policy sources remain IMU/encoders only. Include rear spin with little frame motion, slow rolling, steer/lean/yaw changes and labelled release transients. Measure individual gyro/quaternion frame timestamps/counters and CAN sample age, not just ROS callback/header times; model measured delay/sample-hold effects in subsequent training. Existing bag cannot independently certify body vx/vy, slip, every physical sign/scale or exact sensor delay.
- ANGLE CONTRACT: deliberately choose representation/reference across training, MuJoCo and deployment only after controlled ±π/±2π crossings and continuous multiple-revolution tests. Test RC/startup zero-reference effects **separately** from wrapping. Current evidence shows sensitivity, not a universally preferable branch or re-zero procedure. No unsupported balance retry, safety-bound restoration/removal, new sensor allowance, estimator implementation, gain/loss change or hardware success is claimed by these recommendations.

## 102. Current IMU fusion mode is unverified; quaternion fusion runs onboard, not on the Pi (2026-10-19)

- MODE EVIDENCE, EXTENDING §20: [imu_publisher.py](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/imu_publisher.py) opens the vendor serial driver, starts reception and sets report rate; neither it nor the adapter/vendor constructor calls `set_algo_type`. The serial library supports `set_algo_type(6|9)`, but deployment has no mode parameter/readback. Therefore **the current six-axis versus nine-axis fusion mode cannot be established from disk**. It inherits the device's current setting; neither its default nor persistence across power cycles was established. Raw magnetometer data or a valid quaternion does not identify the selected fusion mode.
- PROTOCOL CROSS-CHECK: [通信协议.xlsx](<tsinghua_bike_project/assign_2_Hardwares/assign_2_Hardwares/1.IMU/Supplementary Materials/通信协议/通信协议.xlsx>), UART sheet rows **41–43**, defines opcode **0x61**, argument **0x06|0x09**, second argument **0x5F**, and **no return**; switching is supported on nine-/ten-axis modules. I2C sheet row **73** defines readable/writable `ALGO_TYPE` register **0x61**. This I2C register is **not evidence of a UART mode-read command**. The spreadsheet was read directly with Python's standard-library ZIP/XML tools; no dependency installation or hardware command.
- CURRENT DATA PATH: device quaternion packet **0x16**, float32 **wxyz** → vendor cache → publisher reorder to **xyzw** → mount correction/normalization → `/trackstand/imu.orientation` → inverse rotation of world `[0,0,-1]` in `build_observation`. Raw packet **0x04** supplies gyro, already decoded to **rad/s**, then host bias/mount correction. The Pi does **not** implement a new attitude-fusion filter. [Current parameters](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/config/params.yaml) are mount RPY **[1.42,0,0] degrees**, host gyro bias **[0,0,0] dps**, publication **100 Hz**; §20's identity-mount description is historical. A zero host correction does not establish absent device calibration.
- FUSION EXPLANATION / EVIDENCE LIMIT: the prior short “yes” and “merge” answers describe **sensor fusion**, not arithmetic averaging: gyro integration follows orientation changes, accelerometer supplies a tilt/gravity reference, and conventional nine-axis fusion additionally uses magnetometer information. The exact YahBoom firmware filter, gains, sensor weighting and currently active magnetic contribution are unavailable/unverified; do not infer a specific Kalman/complementary implementation. [AHRS background](https://www.vectornav.com/resources/inertial-navigation-primer/theory-of-operation/theory-ahrs).
- PUBLISHED ACCELERATION IS UNAVAILABLE: vendor raw acceleration is decoded in **g units**, but `imu_publisher` does not populate ROS `linear_acceleration` and sets **`linear_acceleration_covariance[0] = -1`**. Raw magnetometer is not a direct policy input either; it may indirectly affect the quaternion if used by the device's selected fusion mode. Per-field freshness/coherence caveats in §§20/62/95 remain; a normalized quaternion and fresh ROS header do not certify accurate, simultaneous measurements.

## 103. What the two encoders and current IMU interface can supply for each observation (2026-10-19)

- SCOPE: availability/approximability assessment, **not a physical accuracy certification**. A selectable six-/nine-axis mode does not by itself resolve body-velocity or encoder-reference mismatch. Indices below are one-based, matching §94; exact source/order/units were rechecked in [contracts.py](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/contracts.py), [CAN decoding](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/can_protocol.py) and the active Isaac observation/configuration.

| # | Observation | Available source and limitation relative to simulation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `gravity_y` | Quaternion-derived normalized gravity; can approximate after mount/tilt calibration, but inherits AHRS dynamic error and timing. |
| 2 | `gravity_z` | Same quaternion-derived estimate; not direct gravity ground truth or raw accelerometer Z. |
| 3 | `ang_vel_x` | Direct corrected gyro, rad/s; bias, mounting, scale and field age require validation. |
| 4 | `ang_vel_y` | Direct corrected gyro, rad/s; same calibration/timing requirements. |
| 5 | `ang_vel_z` | Direct corrected gyro yaw-axis **rate**, rad/s, not absolute yaw heading; available in conventional six-/nine-axis operation. |
| 6 | `lin_vel_x` | Current `0.07*rear_velocity` is wheel circumferential speed, not reliably true root-body vx under slip/frame motion. |
| 7 | `lin_vel_y` | Current constant **0** is not a measurement of true root-body lateral velocity. |
| 8 | `steering_pos` | Signed A3 startup-relative joint radians; straight-ahead reference, gearing/play and timing must match the model. |
| 9 | `drive_pos` | A3 cumulative count is available, but wrapping/startup-reference semantics differ from Isaac's native joint coordinate; §§97/99/106. |
| 10 | `steering_vel` | Signed A2 joint speed, rad/s; direct encoder source, subject to calibration/report timing. |
| 11 | `drive_vel` | Signed A2 joint speed, rad/s; wheel slip does not invalidate wheel angular speed, but does invalidate treating it as frame translation. |
| 12 | `sin_steer` | Deterministic sine of observation 8; inherits its reference/sign errors, no extra sensor. |
| 13 | `cos_steer` | Deterministic cosine of observation 8; same limitation, no extra sensor. |

- MAIN CONCLUSION: attitude/rates and joint states have suitable sensor sources **in principle**; their on-bike accuracy/synchronization still needs independent validation. The two true root-body linear velocities are **not directly supplied by the current interface**, while rear-angle representation/reference needs an explicit contract. This is not a theorem that an IMU/encoder estimator can never infer translation under suitable assumptions; no existing bag independently certifies arbitrary-slip velocity or all thirteen physical channels. Six-/nine-axis selection and magnetometer heading do not create a direct translational-velocity measurement.

## 104. Accelerometer specific force is not motion-independent gravity; quaternion accuracy is not guaranteed (2026-10-19)

- PHYSICAL DISTINCTION: in the standard specific-force convention, **`f_b = R^T*(a_w-g_w) + bias + noise`**, with `R` body→world and `a_w` the acceleration **at the IMU**, including lever-arm effects. The policy needs normalized **`g_b = R^T*[0,0,-1]`**, not specific force. At rest an ideal conventional accelerometer gives `g_b ≈ -f_b/g0`; actual device axes/signs and g-versus-m/s² units must be checked before any substitution. [Specific-force/navigation reference](https://rotations.berkeley.edu/strapdown-inertial-navigation/).
- MOTION CASES: a fixed static lean legitimately changes gravity components; leaning itself is not a measurement fault. Rapid lean/rotation, acceleration/braking, vibration and circular motion add acceleration to the accelerometer signal. Normalizing it does not remove that contamination. A gravity-only tilt interpretation can be wrong during sustained turning, including a banked turn where apparent force aligns closer to the bike's vertical than true gravity. The previous **illustration, not bike data**, used upright horizontal circular motion at **2 m/s**, radius **1 m**: acceleration **4 m/s²**, false tilt **atan(4/9.81)=22.183°**, specific-force magnitude about **1.080 g**.
- QUATERNION QUALIFICATION: inverse-rotating gravity is correct for any motion **if orientation is correct**. The real quaternion is an estimate, not independent truth; fusion can have bias, dynamic-acceleration sensitivity and filtering/latency. Pure heading error alone does not change gravity projection, but coupled attitude/filter errors remain possible. Therefore neither direct acceleration nor quaternion projection is guaranteed more accurate for every motion; do **not** replace gravity_y/z with raw accel_y/z merely because the IMU has an accelerometer. [AHRS limitations](https://www.vectornav.com/resources/inertial-navigation-primer/theory-of-operation/theory-ahrs).
- READ-ONLY NUMERICAL CHECK: the actual deployment `build_observation`, fixed **roll 10° /pitch 5°**, yaw **0°/90°/179°**, returns identical float32 gravity Y/Z **[−0.1729873866,−0.9810602665]**, maximum difference **0**. This verifies pure-heading invariance in the implementation, **not physical AHRS accuracy or fusion mode**. Static lean/dynamic motion should be checked against independent orientation ground truth with separate accel/gyro/quaternion timing; no such new hardware experiment or publisher change occurred.

## 105. True linear velocities are useful, not established indispensable actor inputs (2026-10-19)

- TASK VERSUS OBSERVATION DESIGN: stationary balance allows small instantaneous fore/aft motion; it does not imply true vx/vy are identically zero. Actual root velocities provide useful drift/recovery information that wheel speed alone need not capture. Nevertheless, **no experiment here proves that every successful policy must observe true vx/vy**. For a new real-bike actor, requiring simulator-only ground truth is a poor deployment contract unless an adequately validated estimator supplies matching semantics.
- FROZEN POLICY VERSUS RETRAINING: §96's nominal **30.00→0.22 s** and DR **27.379→0.263 s** results establish frozen **91850's sensitivity to replacing** the two channels. They are **not** experiments training a new policy without them. Simply deleting, zeroing or renaming those inputs on the bike is not a remedy for the existing checkpoint.
- PROPOSED COMPARISON, NOT IMPLEMENTED: separately train an actor without true vx/vy (initially retain the other eleven observations to isolate the change), and an actor using exactly deployment-consistent estimates; compare against the true-velocity baseline with matched plant/reset/seed/DR/termination settings. History/recurrence may help infer hidden motion, without guaranteeing ground truth under slip. Keep true simulated state for reward/diagnostic use; an optional **asymmetric critic** can receive privileged training-only state while the actor remains sensor-only. [Asymmetric actor–critic paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.06542). No observation removal, recurrent/asymmetric architecture, new training/export or successful sensor-only balance was implemented or demonstrated by this discussion.

## 106. Both joint angles use model coordinates; episode reset writes zero rather than recording an offset (2026-10-19)

- PRECISE RESET ANSWER, EXTENDING §§97/99: both active angle observations directly slice `asset.data.joint_pos`, in **radians**, with no encoder-count conversion, initial-angle subtraction or task-level modulo. [bicycle_cfg.py](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/bicycle_cfg.py) sets **`joint_pos={".*":0.0}` /`joint_vel={".*":0.0}`**; the reset event invokes installed `reset_scene_to_default`, whose joint writes restore those configured coordinates for resetting environments. Thus both start from the model's zero, but the task does **not** capture `q_start` and calculate `q-q_start` at every observation. Zeroing the physical simulated joint differs from re-labelling an arbitrary measured position as zero.
- STEERING VERSUS REAR: steering is signed deflection relative to the model's straight-ahead configuration; it is not a turn counter. Rear observation is the native backend coordinate, **neither an explicitly single-turn ±π angle nor a certified continuous multi-turn accumulation**; §97's measured rebranching qualifies both labels. The separate integrated `wheel_net_angle` is not an actor input. Deployment A3 already reads cumulative counts before subtracting its startup reference and wrapping; changing only RC references or removing wrapping remains an unvalidated contract change, not an implemented fix.

## 107. Three-question checklist: corrected interpretation and additional mismatch index (2026-10-19)

- QUALIFICATIONS TO THE USER'S SIX-ITEM SUMMARY: unbounded raw Gaussian/actor outputs are a valid design choice, **not inherently a bug**; the confirmed defect is the assumed bounded contract/inconsistent downstream processing. “Deployment wraps but Isaac does not explicitly wrap” and “single-loop versus multi-loop” are not two independent established flaws; the latter label overstates backend evidence (§§97/106). Passive `b` belongs conceptually in the plant, but steering MIT **Kd=0.2** is controller damping; the rear **0.05** has no measured physical identification. Zero-target-speed unsaturated lumping can be valid, while saturation/sample timing breaks general equivalence (§§80/85). Do not mechanically move every damping term or assert a measured `b`.
- ADDITIONAL CONFIRMED/UNRESOLVED AXES, CROSS-REFERENCED RATHER THAN REPEATING EXPERIMENTS: **(1)** per-episode versus process-start zero reference (§§97/106); **(2)** idealized versus unidentified real actuator bandwidth/current/voltage/torque–speed response (§§46–50/77/79/83); **(3)** simulator **400 Hz** actuation versus Pi **50 Hz** policy, **100 Hz** queries and **200 Hz** dispatch for current 91850/MIT, with unknown firmware inner rate and sampled rear damping (§§24/51/77); **(4)** asynchronous/per-field stale IMU and CAN state versus simulator snapshots, with training latency path inactive (§§20/62/95/102); **(5)** opaque real attitude fusion versus ground-truth orientation plus simple simulated noise/bias (§§102–104); **(6)** physical kickstand release/contact and post-failure support versus simulated episode reset (§§61/64); **(7)** different evaluation termination envelopes (§74 and below). These are **not seven independently proven causes** and do not establish replacement hardware or geometry changes are necessary.
- EVALUATION / SAFETY DISTINCTION: default Isaac has timeout/fallen plus yaw/drift/wheel-net/divergence checks; opt-in `--pure_survival` disables the latter four. Current MuJoCo `deploy_sim` uses timeout/fallen only. Deployment's current attitude readiness check rejects inversion, **not Isaac's 30° fall condition**, while other RC/stale-data/motor-fault protections still exist; it does not reset after a simulated 30-s episode or measure stand contact. Supported lean after early stand contact is not survival evidence. See [Isaac terminations](isaaclab_train/source/trackstand_rl/trackstand_rl/tasks/manager_based/trackstand/trackstand_bike/trackstand_rl_env_cfg.py), [viewer](mujoco_sim2sim/scripts/run_deploy_viewer.py), [policy readiness](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/trackstand_deploy/policy_controller.py).
- OPERATING TRAP, NOT A DIAGNOSED CAUSE OF THE MIT BAG: the packaged [trackstand.launch.py](deploy/source/trackstand_deploy/launch/trackstand.launch.py) still starts **`motor_io`**, not `motor_mit_io`; the corrected-sign analyzed bag explicitly used MIT. Record the actual executable, package/source/model identities and effective parameters for each trial; sharing topics does not make C0 host-PD, C2 and MIT backends interchangeable. No launch/backend switch was made.

## 108. Observation follow-up recording scope and verification (2026-10-19)

- THIS UPDATE: appended previously unrecorded sensor-feasibility/gravity/fusion explanations, velocity-observation necessity qualifications and exact reset interpretation as §§102–107 with concise plain-text counterparts. Existing motor/angle/causal results remain in place and are cross-referenced. These are implementation/synthetic checks, **not new GPU balancing evaluations, firmware interrogation or hardware accuracy measurements**.
