# Part II — NewBike balance campaign (pre-trackstand, 2026-08 → 09)

## Phase 0–1: reward sweep on NewBike-Balance-v0 (no DR)
- Convention: drive & steer rewards always exponential exp(−decay·|v|), weights vary; survival 1/0 = binary, exp = exponential; "best" = largest mean episode length reached in fewest iterations, judged by play_eval.py.
- Seed baselines (laptop 5090 + desktop 4090): seed 42/123/1/20/80 each; laptop best = seed 1, desktop best = seed 42.
- Drive-weight sweep (4090, steer 0): 1/2/5/10/20 → drive=5 best, 2 and 20 runner-ups; 10 failed instantly in play_eval. The source calls binary survival better than exponential: its d12/s0 comparison 1826 > 1136 supports that, but its written d5/s1.25 comparison **“1156 > 1204” is arithmetically false**, so that second claimed proof is unresolved.
- Drive sweep with DR (d=2/5/7.5/10/12/20): all performed very bad. NewBike-Balance-v0 drive sweep 1/2/5/7/10/12 at full DR: all bad; arp fallback on d1: 0.1 mild help (156 vs 127 steps), 0.5 fatal (24 steps). Capacity A/B: skrl [512,256,128,64] MUCH worse than [32,32] (9 vs 212 steps).
- Steer tuning: anchors at s0 per drive value; prediction: steer ≥ survival (5) HURTS (steer_reward rewards a still steering motor — steering is the balancing actuator). The historical grid labels d12/s0 = 1826 “best confirmed” and lists exponential-survival 1136 (d12/s0), 1204 (d5/s1.25); however its other listed steer-zero numbers include d11=2149, d13=1854, d14=1901. The ranking is internally inconsistent and must not be used without the original evaluation context.
- PHASE 2 (planned): action-rate penalty for chaos/jitter (arp wired via --arp_w; penalizes action CHANGE, not motion). Method notes: single-seed + cudnn.deterministic=False → nearby cells differ by noise; use train_deterministic.py; rank by ep_len/roll RMS/fall rate, NOT mean reward (not comparable across weight/type changes).

### Additional pre-AI manual sweep details (`cfg_record.txt`, lines 1–132)

- No-DR survival-only baseline IDs, all survival weight 5, drive/steer/action-rate weights zero: RTX 5090 seeds 42/123/1/20/80 were runs 08261408/08261734/08262013/08262024/08262053 (seed 1 called best there); RTX 4090 equivalents were 08261634/08261743/08262017/08262042/08262058 (seed 42 called best there). The 4090 drive-weight 1/2/5/10/20 trials were 08262138/08271011/08271020/08262135/08262141; weight 5 was called best, 2 and 20 runners-up, 10 failed immediately in play_eval. Exponential-survival comparison ID: 08262148 (drive 2, steer 0). Full-DR drive sweep IDs: 08271730 (2), 08271731 (5), an unlabeled 7.5 trial, 08271811 (10), 08271732 (12), 08271733 (20); all were called poor. Position/velocity-controlled full-DR sweep IDs: 08281822/08281948/08282122/08282257/08290030/08290206 for drive 1/2/5/7/10/12, with 08290349/08290514 testing action-rate penalty 0.1/0.5 on the drive-1 leader; 08311840 was the large-network capacity comparison.
- The old no-DR grid listed recorded steer-zero episode lengths d11=2149, d12=1826, d13=1854, d14=1901, d10/s2.5=1122, and d5/s1.25=1156; several other steer-weight cells and action-rate values (1e-5 through 0.5) were marked **plan**, not completed measurements. Its Phase-2 plan was a one-variable action-rate test at 0.1 and 0.5 on two leaders, checking smoothness without shortening episodes; if 0.5 helped, bracket toward 1.0. **Source inconsistencies:** it calls d12/s0 “best” although 2149, 1901 and 1854 exceed 1826 in the listed grid, and writes “1156 > 1204” for one binary-versus-exponential comparison although 1156 is smaller. The recorded numbers and claims cannot both be true; do not use those two ranking claims as established results without consulting the original evaluations. The d12/s0 1826 versus exponential 1136 comparison is arithmetically consistent.

## V1 CAMPAIGN (NewBike-Balance-v1, hybrid: torque drive + position steering)
- Rewards: surv/roll/drift/micro/act/rate (act = −||a||, rate = −||da/scale|| normed). All runs rsl_rl train_deterministic.py, seed 42, 4096 envs, 20k iters, DR ramp on. Satisfactory = mean ep_len > 1400 in play_eval WITH full DR.
- iter1 (09010034, literature weights): ep_len 134.9 (DR), timeout 3.2% — NOT ok. → iter2: micro band Gaussian→plateau+linear tail, roll sigma 8→12°.
- iter2 (09011013): ABORTED 63% — SIGN ERROR: act/rate funcs return NEGATIVE values, so negative weights flipped them into REWARDS (TB: act +0.074, rate +0.005 net-positive — explains iter1's servo slamming). Weights fixed to +0.10 → 09011132: ep_len 854.9, timeout 55.3%, fall 44.7% — 6× over iter1, not ok. → iter3: drift band 2→5, micro target 3→1.5, roll sigma 15°; NEW anti-orbital yaw_band term (w=2, σ=0.4).
- iter3 (09011356): ep_len 769.0, yaw 174.7/180°, turn 282.5°/ep — STILL ORBITING; TB yaw_band=1.7e-12: Gaussian tail dead (gradient vanishes past ~0.8 rad). Iter2 re-eval: turn 256.5° — iter2 was ALSO circling (its 854.9 contaminated). → iter4: yaw_band → plateau+linear tail (band 0.1, slope 0.33, w 4); roll sigma 12°.
- iter4 (09011600): ep_len 846.1, turn 242.7° — STILL ORBITING; TB yaw_band=0.075: the CLAMPED linear tail hits 0 exactly at |yaw|=π → the policy PARKED at the zero-gradient floor (yaw 174.7°). → iter5: yaw_band UNCLAMPED (value −1 at π, constant gradient); NEW wheel_net term (joint_pos WRAPS at ±π — verified write 10.0 → read −2.5 — so a custom env class integrates joint_vel; plateau band 1.0, slope 0.19, floor-capped at −1/step, w=1).
- iter5 (09011824): ep_len 621.7, timeout 35.2%. MAJOR CORRECTION: yaw probe showed the bike SPAWNS at yaw ~±180° (USD root faces −x) — "yaw pinned at 174.7°" = the bike faces its spawn direction, NOT orbiting; iter3's yaw_band taxed upright bikes −4/step for facing spawn and rewarded TURNING AROUND. Real failure mode: the policy balances by ROLLING FORWARD (drive 6.3 rad/s) and dies at the 2 m drift limit or falls. → iter6: yaw_band + metrics RELATIVE to per-env spawn yaw.
- AUDIT (iter6 aborted): FATAL — the hand-rolled yaw formula assumed quat (w,x,y,z) but isaaclab stores (x,y,z,w) → on the identity spawn quat the formula returned π → "spawn facing backward" was an artifact; a true 5° turn read 0.0 → iter6 yaw_band was INERT. FIX: use canonical asset.data.heading_w everywhere (reward, env baseline, play_eval). Also flagged: rate_penalty mixes 1/rad and 1/(N·m) scales (steer biased 4:1); act_penalty name says torque but reads normalized actions; USD steering limits ±89.999985° match action scale 1.57; fork at +x → base+x = forward.
- TERMINATION CHANGE (user spec): drifted 2.0 → 0.5 m (2.0 m allowed orbiting inside); NEW yaw_deviation |heading−spawn|>60°; NEW wheel_net |net wheel angle|>7.2 rad — reset orbiting/rolling outright. (Iter6 loose-limits run aborted at 73%: training loose + eval tight = mismatched comparison.)
- AUDIT2 (iter7 TAINTED, skipped): spawn_yaw was captured from kernel-cached heading_w in the post-reset window where _reset_pose() does NOT invalidate it → baseline = dying bike's heading. Project-wide stale-read audit + fixes: env spawn_yaw, rewards.yaw_band, terminations.yaw_deviation, observations.balance_imu_state all read the raw root_quat_w (fresh in all windows); play_eval roll+yaw from raw quat; eval_logger already raw (xyzw helper); dynamic mdp clean.
- iter8 (09020158, survival-only ablation — 5 shaping terms commented out by the user, all freshness fixes, tight envelope 0.5 m/60°/7.2 rad): ep_len 54.2 (DR), timeout 0.3%, fall 99.7%. SURVIVAL-ONLY VERDICT: does NOT learn oscillation — alive-time gradient too sparse to bootstrap under full DR.
- TERMINATION CHANGE: yaw_deviation 60° → 30° per user (60° still leaves room to learn orbiting; 30° forces oscillation).
- iter9 (09021148): ep_len 29.9, timeout 0%; deaths: fallen 54%, YAW_DEV 41%, wheel_net 9%, drifted 0.5% — the bike can't finish a recovery turn within 30° of heading; squeezed between the yaw wall and the fall wall. (User's 15k duplicate: 29.4, fallen 65%, yaw 27%.) → iter10: keep 30° but make resets survivable: initial roll ±0.15→0.10, rollrate ±0.5→0.25; survival 2→5.
- iter10 (09021627): ep_len 37.9 (user dup 19.4), timeout 0%, fall 100%; deaths: YAW_DEV 50%, fallen 33%, drifted 18% — recovery at this geometry inherently swings >30°; 30° is unlearnable from scratch under full DR. → iter11: yaw cap back to 60° (learn balance first, tighten later).
- CORRECTION (user): the 17-47-42 run was trained with --no_dr (dr_ramp end_step=1e9, alpha~0 forever), NOT the DR ramp. Its fair no-DR eval: ep_len 1306.9, timeout 53.9%, roll RMS 1.45°, yaw max 36° — the FIRST real trackstand of the campaign (no-DR). Under full DR it collapses to 19.4 (67×). The full-DR criterion is the wall, not the reward design. Next lever: shrink DR ranges, not more reward tuning.
- iter11 (09022102, user weights, yaw 60°, DR ramp): DR eval 32.9, timeout 0% — rebalanced weights did NOT break the full-DR wall.
- iter12 (09022132, user run: yaw 30°, NO DR, 15k): no-DR eval ep_len 977.3, roll RMS 0.38° (!), worst lean 1.97°, yaw max 29.98° (cap holds) — BEST balance quality of the campaign; deaths mostly by the 30° heading cap after ~20 s (slow heading wander), not falling. Full-DR eval: 20.0 steps (49× collapse). LOOP STOPPED per user: shrink DR ranges (mass 0.8–1.2, friction 0.8–1.2) and retrain.
- Further manual-run metrics omitted from the short bullets above (`cfg_record.txt`, lines 142–256): iter1 wheel mean/max 15/239 rad/s; iter2 roll RMS 4.9°, drive 5.5/79 and steer 1.5/36 rad/s, drift max 2.0 m. Iter3 timeout 45.2%, yaw mean/max 174.7°/180°, turn 282.5°/episode, and roll RMS rose 4.94°→6.51° after widening roll sigma; iter4 timeout 48.5%, yaw mean/max 174.7°/180°, turn 242.7°/episode. These yaw-based early diagnoses were later **invalidated** by the spawn-frame/quaternion corrections above; the measurements remain historical. Iter5 timeout 35.2%, drive 6.3 rad/s. Iter8 used yaw 60°/drift 0.5 m/wheel-net 7.2 rad and recorded yaw max 60°/drift max 0.5 m, showing the envelope biting. Iter10's train-end wheel-net death fraction was 1%. The corrected no-DR 17-47-42 evaluation also recorded drive mean 1.4 rad/s. Iter12 turn was 113.8°/episode; its roughly 20-second heading-cap deaths were not falls.
- Historical control/config detail: action-rate penalty returns unbounded `−||a_t−a_{t−1}||₂`, so the Phase-2 plan kept its weight small and compared only against existing zero-penalty leaders. Iter3's Gaussian yaw reward had weight 2 and sigma 0.4; the attempted iter4 band used 0.1 rad plateau, slope `1/(π−0.1)≈0.33`, and weight 4. The iter5 wheel-net band used 1.0 rad plateau, 0.19 tail slope, −1/step floor, weight 1. These are **superseded experiment settings**, not the current Trackstand task configuration.

### Exact early V1 settings and probe details (`cfg_record.txt`, lines 140–192)

The columns below are reward weights in the order survival / roll / drift / yaw / micro / action penalty / action-rate penalty. These are **historical NewBike-Balance-v1** settings under full DR, not today's Trackstand configuration.

| Run | Weights | Other recorded settings or change |
|---|---|---|
| 09010034, iter1 | `2 / 8 / 2 / — / 2 / −0.10 / −0.05` | roll Gaussian sigma 8°; drift band 0.2 m; micro target 3 rad/s. The negative act/rate weights mistakenly rewarded the negative penalty functions. |
| 09011013, aborted iter2 | `2 / 8 / 2 / — / 4 / −0.10 / −0.10` | micro Gaussian became plateau `[0,3]` plus linear tail; roll sigma 12°. Aborted at 63% after the sign error was found. |
| 09011132, corrected iter2 | `2 / 8 / 2 / — / 4 / +0.10 / +0.10` | only the penalty signs corrected from the aborted run. |
| 09011356, iter3 | `2 / 8 / 5 / 2 / 1.5 / +0.10 / +0.10` | yaw Gaussian sigma 0.4; roll sigma 15°. Widening roll sigma raised the reward at 30° from 0.0016 to 0.135 but roll RMS worsened from 4.94° to 6.51°. |
| 09011600, iter4; 09011824, iter5 | `2 / 8 / 5 / 4 / 1.5 / +0.10 / +0.10` | roll sigma back to 12°; yaw band/linear tail introduced, later unclamped; micro tail slope increased 0.03→0.05 while drive was still about 6.1 rad/s versus the 1.5 rad/s target. |

These early yaw diagnostics were later invalidated by the spawn-yaw/quaternion-order bugs described above. The iter6 heading correction used `heading_w` (base +x rotated into world, `atan2(y,x)`) and a wrap-aware difference relative to each environment's spawn yaw. Tightening then changed drift termination 2.0→0.5 m, added yaw deviation >60° (`1.0472 rad`) and unwrapped wheel-net angle >7.2 rad; training with old loose limits and evaluation with new tight ones was rejected as a mismatched comparison. This preserves the setup/probe record, **not** the superseded “orbiting” conclusion.

## Reworked termination + reward exploration (0903–0905, user-driven, no-DR 60k)
- Envelope: roll 30° / drift 0.5 m / yaw 30° / wheel_net 7.2 rad; control target reworked from violent steering to slight rear-wheel oscillation with almost fixed steering; 60k iters, then DR one factor at a time.
- 0903 sweep (4 runs): surv5 only → ep 1329.1, timeout 45.4%, rollRMS 0.73; surv5+rate0.5 → 643.7/0.0% (rate 0.5 kills it); **surv5+rate0.1 → 1354.3/68.2%/0.61 WINNER**; surv5+rate0.1+act0.1 → 1142.8/50.4%. Survival-only now PROVEN learnable at 60k no-DR (revises the iter8 verdict — that failed due to DR+20k, not the reward).
- 09031700 A/B (baseline surv5+rate0.1): A = +wheel_net band 1.0 (attacks the 31% eval-side wheel_net deaths); B = +drift_band 1.5. First-round finals: both REGRESSED. REVISED with peak models (finals were late-training degradation): A1 model_44200 → ep 1404.4, timeout 68.7%, wheel deaths 8.4% (was 31%!), drift 22.9%, rollRMS 0.46 — BEATS baseline on every axis; A2 model_44250 → 1381.2/78.1%/0.50. B1/B2 worse. VERDICT v2: wheel_net band 1.0 WORKS at its peak; drift becomes the new dominant death; use early stopping (~45k).
- 090412xx quadratic A/B/C (wheel_quad 1.0 / drift_quad 1.0 / both). FIRST eval INVALID (user had commented dr_ramp → --no_dr was a no-op → evals ran full un-ramped DR → ~19-step collapse). CORRECTED: A RAW 1355.9/62.5%/0.92, A SM 1318.2/47.7%; B SM 1413.8/73.6%/1.21 (best quad); C RAW 1289.8, C SM 1249.9. VERDICT: quadratics did NOT beat the band wheel_net at its peak (A1 1404.4/68.7%/0.46) — the always-negative quadratic overtaxes the necessary oscillation. BEST = survival 5 + rate 0.1 + wheel_net BAND 1.0, early-stop ~44k.
- 0905 FINDING: SOME DR TERMS HELP — with identical rewards, tire-friction DR (0.5–1.2, full from step 0, no ramp) learns DRAMATICALLY faster than no-DR (ep_len 1334 @iter500, 1486 @iter742 vs no-DR's 871 @iter1000 plateau ~900). Mechanism: dynamics diversity prunes degenerate single-dynamics strategies; the only common solution is zero-mean oscillation. Reverses the old DR pessimism: FULL DR was the wall; single-term friction DR is a regularizer/accelerator.
- The same training-curve comparison recorded G1 (wheel Gaussian only, no drift Gaussian) at only ~690 steps by iteration 3000. The source presents the friction-diversity mechanism as an interpretation of those curves, not a separately measured causal mechanism.
- 09050126 G1/G2 Gaussian eval: G1 (wheel_gauss2.0) FINAL 1324.9/64.6%/0.65; G2 (+drift_gauss1.5) FINAL 1338.3/66.7%/0.78. Finals eval BEST here — TB mid-training peaks did NOT predict eval (opposite of the quad batch). vs champions: band A1 1404.4/68.7%/0.46, baseline 1354.3/68.2%/0.61. VERDICT: Gaussian wheel does NOT beat the band wheel (nor the plain baseline). Band plateau+tail remains the best wheel-net reward.
- 0905 DR-TERM ABLATION (6 runs, G2 reward set, 15k iters, one DR term each at full strength, no ramp; **preliminary eval, later found wrong-env for five of six terms**): FRICTION RAW 1500.0/100.0%; MASS RAW 1494.8/97.7%; ROLL-POSE RAW 1500.0/100.0%; ROLL-VEL RAW 1500.0/100.0%; COM RAW 1138.3/65.5%; IMU RAW 927.8/55.7%. These are **not** six in-distribution results: only IMU was evaluated under its intended DR; the other five results measured DR-trained policies without DR. Training itself was correct. The corrected in-distribution results are below; cross-term generalization remains untested.
- 09051101 IMU-60k EXTENSION (**invalid noise-off eval, retained for provenance only**): FINAL 1474.7/94.5%/0.89; RAW 1487.5/95.3%; SMOOTH 1500.0/100.0%. Do **not** cite these as IMU-DR performance: the corrected noisy result was 1129.7/72.6%, not a perfect horizon score.
- CRITICAL CORRECTION (eval-env bug): the dr_ramp wrap in the patch drivers was NOT idempotent — patching an already-wrapped file nested the ''' pairs and re-ACTIVATED dr_ramp → with --no_dr → alpha=0 → the single DR term was lerped to nominal → the eval env had NO DR/noise. IMU decomposition — gyro-only: 528.0/0.6% with WHEEL_NET deaths 95.9% (KILLER: a constant roll-RATE offset → corrective drive → net wheel rotation runaway); tilt-only: 1484.8/97.7% (NEARLY FREE); latency-only: 1117.2/65.3% (~35% timeout cost). Caveat: old full-IMU runs used the G2 reward set (wheel_net_gauss counteracts the runaway mode) — cross-comparison confounded on exactly the term that matters.

## IMU error model → #1+#8 reimplementation (2026-09-08/09)
- Error taxonomy: deterministic/calibratable (#1 turn-on bias, #2 scale-factor ±0.5%, #3 nonlinearity, #4 misalignment, #5 g-sensitivity, #6 vibration rectification, #7 quantization); stochastic/unremovable (#8 white noise ARW/VRW — datasheet 0.07°/s RMS gyro = 0.0012 rad/s, 0.001 g RMS accel; #9 bias instability 1/f wander — missing from datasheet, measurable via 1–3 h stationary log → allantools.adev curve minimum; #10 rate/accel random walk); environmental (#11 thermal).
- OBS-NOISE vs IMU-BIAS MECHANISM (audited): two SEPARATE perturbations — (1) gaussian obs noise per-step, manager-applied AFTER the obs func, gated ONLY by enable_corruption (false in EVERY one of the 71 pre-2026-09-08 run env.yamls → the authored noise was DEAD campaign-wide, regardless of std); (2) reset_imu_turnon_residuals (renamed 2026-09-08) — per-episode bias+latency applied INSIDE balance_imu_state, not gated by enable_corruption. Note: the OTHER task families set enable_corruption = True (their noise was live).
- DECISION (term/include/magnitude): #1 turn-on bias YES at the POST-CALIBRATION residual (measure per-boot spread after firmware calibration; the uncalibrated 0.03-class only if firmware does NOT calibrate — the gyro bias is the one number that can kill the policy, measure it most carefully); #8 white noise YES per-channel 0.0012/0.001; #9 NO at 30 s scale (~1e-5..1e-4 wander); #10 NO (hours-scale).
- REIMPLEMENTATION arc: (1) CHANNEL ABLATION on Z SMOOTH 34450 (10 evals): the 4 joint channels unused (drive_pos harmful: zeroing it gave rollRMS 0.45→0.13) → commented out in balance_imu_state's cat → obs 13→9 dims. (2) RENAME reset_imu_sensor_model → reset_imu_turnon_residuals (post-calibration residual semantics). (3) #8 first attempt: stock GaussianNoiseCfg with a tensor std → functionally correct but dumps as a binary blob in env.yaml; dr_ramp noise helpers assumed SCALAR std. (4) FINAL: custom PerChannelGaussianNoiseCfg in mdp/stationary/noise.py with a PLAIN TUPLE std (clean env.yaml dump; length assert; tensor built at runtime; cfg torch-free). (5) RAMP FIX: dr_ramp noise helpers float/tensor/tuple-aware, element-wise lerp. (6) VERIFIED: func resolves, NoiseCfg isinstance, manager path on dummy tensor (zeros stay zero), length assert fires, clean yaml dump; zero_agent smoke green.
- HOW TO MEASURE the bias residual stds: replicate the firmware's stationary calibration window per boot; residual = 0 − estimate; std across 10–30 cold power cycles; sanity ~1e-4..1e-3 rad/s; accel residual from a leveled stand, gravity-component residual per boot. One bench session (long stationary log) yields gyro residuals + accel residuals + #8 verification — three numbers, one afternoon.
- Measurement protocol detail: keep the bike stationary on its stand through the **same calibration window the firmware actually uses** (example 2–5 s at control rate), or log its already-calibrated output. For each boot, true angular rate is zero, so gyro residual is `0 − bias_estimate`; repeat over 10–30 cold power cycles across hours/days, then use the per-axis residual standard deviation (conservatively, the worst axis) in **rad/s**, not one boot's offset. A result near 0.01–0.1 rad/s suggests calibration is failing; a working calibration should be near max(noise/√N, thermal), roughly 1e-4–1e-3 rad/s. For accel, warm up, level the bike, apply firmware calibration, and compare measured gravity (in g) with the true bike-frame vertical. Use the across-boot standard deviation of each gravity-component residual, conservatively the worst because the sim shares one `accel_res_bias_std`; 0.02 g is about 1.15° of tilt. A fixed mount misalignment is per-unit and should be absorbed by gravity-referenced calibration; include multiple boards only if modeling a fleet. Until measured: if firmware does **not** calibrate, use its uncalibrated bias specification (possibly >0.03 rad/s); if it does, `0.0012/√N rad/s` is a provisional gyro residual estimate. Use a 1–3 h stationary log for Allan-deviation bias instability (`allantools.adev`, curve minimum), check effective **post-filter** white noise, and sweep gyro-bias magnitudes around the measured residual. Historical 9-dim-run suggestion: ten-sample averaging would lower 0.0012 rad/s to about 0.0004 rad/s; its actual deployed filtering must be measured.
- Historical 2026-09-08 pre-training state, **not today's task configuration**: events were reset_scene + pinned ground friction + `reset_imu_turnon_residuals`, gyro and accel residual stds both 0.0, delay 1–3 policy steps (20–60 ms); `dr_ramp` was wrapped, so authored values were full-strength from step 0. Rewards were survival 5.0 + drift Gaussian 2.0 + rate penalty 0.1. The pending run therefore exercised white noise and latency, **not nonzero turn-on bias**. The 9-channel noise tuple was `(0.001, 0.001, 0.0012, 0.0012, 0.0012, 0, 0, 0, 0)`; zeroes on linvel/sin-cos channels were placeholders until measured. The historical `reset_imu_turnon_residuals` rename covered its function/export, curriculum lookups, observation comments, and configuration; the noise helper's tuple path was tested through manager application and a 9-dimensional zero-agent smoke run.
- 09082242/2246/2248 (first runs with the reimplemented IMU model: **9-dim** obs, 11-33 rewards, #8 noise ON always): 2242 noise-only RAW 456.6/0.0% wheel87.4%+yaw10.0%; 2246 +gyro-res 658.6/6.2% wheel81.1%; 2248 +accel-res 764.5/6.5% wheel77.6%. **Historical, subsequently revised verdict:** these 9-dim policies suffered 0–6.5% timeouts with WHEEL_NET runaway (~80–94% of deaths), despite roll RMS 0.74–0.83°. The later 13/9-dim × noise on/off test below showed that dropping the joint channels, **not white noise alone**, caused the collapse; do not generalize the 9-dim finding to 13-dim policies.
- Exact 9-dim checkpoint results (all with white noise on, seed 42, 60k iterations): 09082242 noise-only RAW `model_18950` 456.6/0.0%, roll 0.74, wheel/yaw deaths 87.4%/10.0%; SMOOTH `model_19050` 468.9/0.0%, roll 0.81, wheel/yaw 92.0%/7.0%. 09082246 +gyro residual 2e-4 rad/s RAW=SMOOTH `model_59750` 658.6/6.2%, roll 0.79, wheel/yaw 81.1%/11.5%. 09082248 +accel residual 0.002 g RAW `model_43150` 764.5/6.5%, roll 0.83, wheel/drift 77.6%/5.6%; SMOOTH `model_48200` 452.1/0.0%, roll 0.77, wheel 94.3%. The 9-dim result alone cannot isolate the noise effect; it is superseded by the 2×2 comparison.
- 09090950 EVAL — 2×2 CLOSED (13/9-dim × noise on/off; eval env verified): model_500 (RAW TB 1500.00 @ iter 514): ep 1500.0, TIMEOUT 100.0%, rollRMS 0.38, maxRoll 3.00, turn 58.9 — zero deaths, PERFECT. 2×2: 11-33 (13-dim off) 1401/70.5% | 0944 (9-dim off) TB peak 911 | 2242 (9-dim on) 457/0% | 0950 (13-dim on) 1500/100%. VERDICT: THE NOISE WAS NEVER THE KILLER — THE 9-DIM TRIM WAS. With the full 13-dim obs the policy absorbs the datasheet white noise COMPLETELY (prime suspect: DRIVE_VEL — the wheel feedback the runaway mode needs). Lesson: the frozen-policy channel ablation measured reliance, not trainability — "unused by a frozen policy" ≠ "safe to drop for training". DECISIONS: 13-dim + always-on #8 noise (9-dim dead); 0950 model_500 champion (the 09061147 pattern: early peak > final).
- 09090950 COMPLETION (60k): the curve HELD 1470–1500 to the end. model_500: 1500.0/100.0% rollRMS 0.38 maxRoll 3.00 turn 58.9 (tighter average); model_32400 (SMOOTH 1499.88): 1500.0/100.0% rollRMS 0.55 maxRoll 0.87 turn 21.7 (tighter extremes). Champion pick: model_500. Committed as baseline 292390a.
- 0950 evaluation provenance: 13-dimensional observation, 13-entry noise tuple, **no** #1 turn-on-bias event, no `dr_ramp`, and unchanged configuration verified by MD5. Its raw TensorBoard peak was 1500.00 at iteration 514, hence `model_500`. `model_32400` is an equally zero-death alternative if tighter maximum roll/less turning matters more than the raw champion's smaller average roll. Final `model_59999` exists and the late training curve suggests it may also be strong, but it was **not evaluated** in this record. The 9-dimensional comparison policies require the joint-channel concatenation commented out and a nine-entry noise tuple at evaluation; evaluating them with the current 13-dimensional observation would be a configuration error.

These were suggestions, not implemented features. The manual 2026-09-06 note also states that magnitude sweeps for gyro, tilt and latency had **not** yet been done at that date.

## Engine audit for NewBike-Balance-v1 (2026-09-13)
1. solver_position/velocity_iteration_count = 4/2 in `NEW_BIKE_BASE_CFG` articulation properties — ROOT CAUSE of the ~×24.5 sluggish drive: TGS under-delivers the effort-actuated wheel per step. Probe-verified: 16/8 restores the rigid response (wheel slams the 61.6 rad/s limit).
2. enable_external_forces_every_iteration = False in the logged `sim.physics` — CONFIRMED the PRIMARY cause with an in-air, welded-root probe on 2026-09-13: at 4/2 + False the wheel plateaus at 23.11 rad/s for drive action `a1≥0.6`; flipping True at the same 4/2 restores rigid physics (61.60 rad/s, the configured limit). The measured effort follows `τ = 6.28·a1 − 0.05·ω` exactly, including 0.00 N·m at `a1=0.2`. The 16/8 fix and the flag flip are two independent repairs of the same truncation (once-per-step force injection + two sweeps cannot propagate the impulse); the fall-probe warning “may cause noisy velocities” points to this behavior.
3. Runtime mass ~+20% above the USD payload masses (the USD colliders declare no density → default-density contribution; MuJoCo carries only payload masses 2.3996 kg total). Un-compensated.
4. DR active every reset in that run: mass 0.5–1.5×, COM, tire friction U(0.5,1.2) through 32 material buckets, actuator gains 0.9–1.1× — play_eval numbers are DR-averaged and cannot be compared directly to nominal-only MuJoCo results.
5. Reset state: initial_state resets root z=0, roll U(−0.1,0.1), roll rate U(−0.25,0.25) every episode — NOT the fixed zeros the MuJoCo env comment assumed (that was 0950's protocol).
6. Friction references: ground pinned 1.0 (startup event); tire = global physxMaterial 0.5/0.5 isotropic (the USD has no per-wheel physics material; `materials.usda` provides only the visual “silver”). MuJoCo wheels carry the sim's pair: sliding 0.75 (MuJoCo combines by MAX — verified empirically; MuJoCo's triple is sliding/torsional/rolling). The earlier “anisotropic 1.0/0.005” description misread an orange-bike MuJoCo friction triple.
7. balance_wheel_net_termination reads with a one-step lag (its own docstring); MuJoCo checks immediately. Immaterial.
8. `physx.usda` sets maxJointVelocity 100 rad/s (5729.578°/s) on the steering joint — dominated by the actuator's `velocity_limit_sim` 23.9 rad/s; no effect.
9. GPU patch-based friction (friction_offset_threshold 0.04, friction_correlation_distance 0.025) has no MuJoCo analog — model-level difference.

## Collision meshes in NewBike-Balance-v1 (2026-09-13)
- Runtime wheel colliders = ONE CONVEX HULL per collision mesh (the URDF converter authored `physics:approximation="convexHull"` on each collision prim in `payloads/instances.usda`, with PhysicsCollisionAPI + MeshCollisionAPI; `NEW_BIKE_BASE_CFG` authored no override, so the USD token controlled cooking). The cfg CAN override via `mesh_approximation_name` (boundingCube | boundingSphere | convexDecomposition | convexHull | none | meshSimplification | sdf). PhysX has NO ellipsoid/cylinder primitive collider. This is the provenance of the hull/vertex-walk contact explored during sim-to-sim transfer.
- The MuJoCo deploy's collisions are hand-authored primitives (frame box, fork box, wheel ellipsoids); meshes visual-only.
- Representation gap: the engines' common wheel shapes do not overlap. Fix paths: boundingSphere on both, SDF on both, or keep fitting MuJoCo (status quo).

## Sim-to-sim campaign diary (user entries, 2026-09-11 → 18)
- 09-11: transfer of 09090950 model_500 to MuJoCo revealed a large gap: physics dt differs (policy inference aligned to 50 Hz in both); collision meshes differ (Isaac convex hulls vs MuJoCo primitives); friction/motor dynamics/inertia/initial condition/obs consistency unexplored.
- 09-12: next steps = verify env consistency (simulator metrics), free-fall baseline test across simulators, Tsinghua bike model comparison.
- 09-13: gravity test on Tsinghua's orange bike worked; fixed newbike collision meshes based on the orange bike's ellipsoids → balance 60 → 200+ steps, still far from 1500. Problems: (1) NewBike-Balance-v1 and deploy_sim use different wheel collision meshes (hull vs ellipsoid); pending solution: SDF (supported by both). (2) solver_velocity_iteration_count too small → force doesn't propagate to every link → ×24.5 rear-wheel inertia compensation; ran 09131837 with 16/8 to address. (3) newest model exports: URDF breaks apart on free fall; MuJoCo XML base-link mesh exceeds cap (downgraded export).
- 09131837 (16/8): mean ep_len 1312.
- 09-14: 09131837 eval ok in Isaac; in MuJoCo deploy_sim without ×24.5 only 30–40 steps — WORSE than 0950 with compensation. Focus moved to aligning physical consistency; exported trackstand_rl_0914_description/ (Isaac) + trackstand_rl_bike_0914 (MuJoCo). Consistency checklist: mass, COM coordinate, friction, collision mesh (most important), joint stiffness & damping, actuator, gravity free-fall test.
- 09-15: exported trackstand_rl_0914_sdf (Isaac, SDF via usda edit script) + trackstand_rl_bike_0914 (MuJoCo, collision from visual). Model difference largely eliminated. Built Trackstand-Balance-v0. Problem found: non-zero initial steering angle in both models — fix in Fusion, then re-export/verify.
- 09-16: SDF triangle approximation implemented via PhysxSDFMeshCollisionAPI — high accuracy, almost identical to MuJoCo's visual-STL hulls. Training restarted on the 0916 model: 09161648.
- 09161648: mean ep_len 1500 at model 15650.
- 09-17: still a large sim-to-sim gap — found the reason: the SDF approximation caused FALSE contact points of the wheel with the ground. Switched to cylinder collision wheels (trackstand_rl_0916_description_cylinder/). Train again → 09171946.
- 09171946: mean ep_len 1500 at model 41050.
- 09-18: play_eval of 09171946 showed front/back slight oscillatory trackstand motion — a very promising sign (reliable and robust policy). 09171946 fully overcame the sim-to-sim gap (94% balance success). Next: further audits — DR, torque/time plots for real-life feasibility, remaining Isaac/MuJoCo differences. Discovered difference: actuator physics frequency Isaac 100 Hz vs MuJoCo 400 Hz; MuJoCo at 100 Hz failed (insufficient integrator accuracy). 100/400 Hz effect on Isaac = open question. Train a full-DR model, investigate sim-to-sim with DR: 09182031 (full DR, 100 Hz, air spawn 2 cm); 09190125 (full DR, 100 Hz, ground spawn).

# Part III — Findings & lessons (cross-cutting)

## Isaac Lab environment quirks
- Two machines: RTX 5090 laptop (conda `isaaclab_py312`, Isaac Lab via `isaaclab.sh --install`) and `lmbike-MS-7E06` desktop (RTX 4090, NO conda, system python 3.10; Isaac Sim source build symlinked as `~/IsaacLab/_isaac_sim`, isaaclab 7.2.0, rsl_rl 5.0.1, torch 2.10.0; the `_isaac_sim/setup_conda_env.sh is missing` warning is expected and harmless). ALWAYS run via `isaaclab.sh -p` — plain python3 3.10 fails the >=3.12 requirement.
- Check WHERE the editable CycleRL points before trusting a training run (`isaaclab.sh -p -c "import CycleRL,os;print(os.path.dirname(CycleRL.__file__))"`) — once installed from a stale sibling copy so edits silently had no effect; re-point via pip uninstall/reinstall.
- `from isaaclab.utils import configclass` is BROKEN on 7.2.0 (not re-exported) — always `from isaaclab.utils.configclass import configclass`.
- `scripts/list_envs.py` filters on "CycleRL-" so the balance/trackstand tasks never appear in its table — a display filter, not a registration failure.
- "Failed to find articulation at '/World/envs/env_*/Robot/Geometry/base_link/base_link'" is BENIGN (prints for every bike, physics works).
- `sim.vis_marker_registry` does NOT exist in this build — guard with hasattr (the heading-arrow debug marker crashed locomotion runs).
- The `velocity_limit` deprecation warning is NOT harmless, but `effort_limit`'s IS: effort_limit is forwarded to effort_limit_sim (works); velocity_limit is NULLED (does nothing). Under pure torque control nothing bounds the joint (the rear wheel hit 2420 rad/s vs nominal 61.6). Version-dependent: applied in 0.27.23–0.34.3, ignored (with warning) in 0.34.4+ — the same cfg gives different physics on different Isaac Lab versions; check the version before comparing runs across machines. Even with *_sim set, PhysX velocity limits are SOFT (sustained torque still overshoots to ~130 rad/s). All 10 velocity_limit= fields outside the torque cfg are dead fields (harmless only via PD damping/action clips).
- "Not all actuators are configured! 2 != 3" is expected (front wheel intentionally free).
- Quaternions are (x, y, z, w), NOT (w, x, y, z) — a hand-rolled wxyz yaw formula returned π for the identity quaternion and was first-order blind to real turning; it silently poisoned three yaw_band iterations ("spawns facing backward / orbiting" was a measurement artifact). For heading, use the canonical `asset.data.heading_w` — never hand-roll quaternion math. Verify tensor layout in CODE, not docstrings.
- `joint_pos` WRAPS at ±π for a free revolute (write 10.0 → read −2.5) — net-rotation rewards can't be stateless; per-env running state lives in a custom env subclass (must accept **kwargs; reset_terminated is set BEFORE rewards are computed in step, so post-step integration with one-step lag is safe).
- Action-manager conventions: `action_manager.action`/`.prev_action` are the RAW pre-scale policy actions; targets = offset(default_joint_pos) + scale × raw. "Per-joint-normalised action-rate penalties" dividing Δ(raw) by physical scales mix 1/rad and 1/(N·m) — a hidden per-joint weight (steer:drive 4:1 for scales 1.57 vs 6.28). True unit-free rate = Δraw/2.
- A vanishing bike in play ≠ broken asset — diagnose with zero_agent.py (no checkpoint) first.
- USD history: commit 7cca0b7 gutted every bike's geometries.usd (~4 MB → 132 B) — the classic result of Saving a flattened stage over the payload files; later restored.

## Training framework facts
- skrl does NOT forward env extras["log"] to TensorBoard (only Reward/Loss/Std/LR) — the motor-speed / DR-ramp diagnostics were converted to terminal prints. rsl_rl DOES forward them — a main reason to prefer it.
- LR schedule was broken: CosineAnnealingLR(T_max=500) annealed to ~0 within ~8k timesteps and stalled 99% of a 1M-step run (reward plateau + rising std). Fixed to KLAdaptiveLR(kl_threshold=0.008).
- rsl_rl setup: scripts/rsl_rl/{train,play,cli_args}.py + agents/rsl_rl_ppo_cfg.py using the current 5.0.1 API (obs_groups + RslRlMLPModelCfg); registered on the balance tasks.
- ~/lmbike_rl = the sibling project that balanced the bike WELL — the reference: rsl_rl, Direct workflow, both motors torque-controlled, tiny [32,32] net, 5-dim obs, 4 s episodes, ~no DR. CycleRL's likely struggles: huge [512,256,128,64] net, 13-dim obs, 30 s episodes, heavy DR, dead LR.
- Action-std diagnostics: healthy = std falls high→low; std RISING while reward falls = the entropy bonus overpowering a weak/regressing signal — lower entropy_coef and/or LR; do NOT just add iterations. Very low std (~0.06) + long episodes = converged.
- train.py override flags: --no_dr (dr_ramp alpha=0), --survival_w/--drive_w/--steer_w (weight 0 = term set to None = fully disabled). They mutate the in-memory cfg BEFORE gym.make (hydra constructs the object; the effective weights ARE recorded in the run's params/env.yaml). BUT survival TYPE (binary vs exp) is a code line-swap, NOT a flag — log the type by hand.
- GPU non-determinism: train.py has cudnn.deterministic=False + TF32 → same cfg+seed gives different policies; sweeps are noise-limited. train_deterministic.py (TF32 off, deterministic algorithms, CUBLAS workspace) makes small changes attributable for a seed — but determinism ≠ generalization (re-check winners on a 2nd --seed).
- rsl_rl checkpoints: model_<iter>.pt every 50 iters, NO best_agent.pt; "latest" = highest iter; verify latest==best via TB. exported/policy.{onnx,pt} can be STALE — re-export from the final model before deploy. play_eval.py prints the eval metrics; --checkpoint <path> loads directly. play_with_metrics.py is skrl-only.
- The reward manager computes `value * weight * dt` — a penalty FUNC that already returns negative values needs a POSITIVE weight (a negative weight flips it into a reward; confirmed in TB as the iter2 servo slamming). Decide per-project whether penalties are negative-funcs (weights positive) or positive-funcs (weights negative); verify the per-term TB sign after one run.

## NewBike balance breakthrough (2026-08-24/26)
- First working stationary balance came from a HIGH drive_reward weight (~12): the drive motor is velocity-controlled, and an erratic rear wheel injects destabilizing reaction torques; a strong "calm drive wheel" reward removes the self-inflicted disturbance so steering-based balancing works. Likely a workaround for the noisy velocity-controlled drive (a drive action-rate penalty or lower action scale might fix the disturbance at the source).
- Working baseline: survival = BINARY (+1 while |roll|<30°) weight 5; drive_reward ~12; steer_reward 0. rsl_rl hyperparams: entropy_coef 0.005, LR 1e-3, KL-adaptive 0.01, [32,32] net.
- Judge by EPISODE LENGTH + play.py render, never mean reward. CORRECTION: the "1500-step/30 s cap" was wrong for the laptop — that run was at 30 Hz (900-step cap; mean 700–800 ≈ 78–89% of cap). Reproduced on the desktop at 30 Hz: 97.3% timeout by ~iter1233. Post-fix the env is 50 Hz and the cap is genuinely 1500.

## Reward design lessons
- Don't compare mean reward across reward-weight changes (changing a weight changes the reward SCALE mechanically). Episode length is scale-invariant and honest — but SATURATES near the timeout; beyond that use non-saturating behavior metrics (kick recovery, DR survival, roll/steer RMS).
- Only the RATIOS among reward terms define the objective (PPO normalizes advantages; overall scale is ~irrelevant — only the value loss is scale-sensitive).
- Positive vs negative: keep net per-step reward >= 0 (an unavoidable negative per-step term can make the agent prefer to terminate — "suicidal agent"). Use a reward (not a penalty) when the ideal value isn't achievable while doing the task (steering MUST move to balance).
- steer_reward specifically: steering is the PRIMARY balancing actuator — rewarding still steering directly opposes balancing; its balance-optimal weight ≈ 0 (its only upside is smoother steering for motor wear / sim-to-real).
- Calming an actuator is free ONLY if it isn't the task's control authority: high drive_reward helped (velocity-controlled drive wheel = pure disturbance); high steer_reward hurts (steering IS the control). Corollaries: don't tune drive and steer on a shared ratio (their roles are asymmetric); anchor every steer sub-sweep at steer=0; gate high-steer runs on data.
- A one-time termination penalty is minor (falling already forfeits the entire discounted future survival stream at γ=0.99; the explicit −10 mainly helps early training).
- Gradient placement: exp(−k·roll) concentrates the gradient at the target (precision near upright, weak in the fall zone); arctan at a midpoint concentrates it where the bike is actually falling; BINARY step reward has no per-step gradient (learns only via alive-time + termination). Self-normalize arctan by its vertical value so the weight stays the peak reward. Very small sharpness makes arctan ~linear.
- Weight tuning method: coordinate descent misses interactions; single runs are stochastic — use 2–3 seeds and COARSE sweeps (4/12/24, not 11/12/13/14 below the noise floor); tune on the TARGET task distribution (DR on).
- Shaping family: band (plateau+tail) beat quadratic & Gaussian on eval ep_len and roll RMS (bands give a flat "good enough" basin with a clear escape gradient). DR terms change WHICH optimum is reachable — re-verify winners under their trained DR regime.
- Don't trust "both configs do well" when they differ on multiple axes (fully confounded) — hold the confounding axes constant and vary ONE at a time.

## Entropy collapse & control rate (2026-08-25, commit 16b3d34)
- Two config regressions, NOT the GPU (measured A/B, same seed, 600 iters, 4096 envs, --steer_w 0): entropy 0.003 + 30 Hz → best ep_len 36, timeout 0.003, final std 0.006; entropy 0.005 + 30 Hz → 375 / 0.24 / 0.015; entropy 0.005 + 50 Hz → 1417 / 0.80 / 0.038.
- entropy_coef=0.003 COLLAPSES the policy (std → ~0.006 by iter 400, exploration dies, locks into "hold still and fall over"). 0.005 is required. If std inflates late, take an earlier checkpoint; do NOT cut entropy again. Both 0.005 runs still regressed after their peak — late-run degradation is a real, separate problem (the peak checkpoint is not the last one).
- `self.step_dt = 0.02` in __post_init__ was a SILENT NO-OP — step_dt is a read-only property on the env (= sim.dt × decimation), never a cfg field (git log -S proves zero commits ever made it one). So the intended 50 Hz ran at 30 Hz (1/60 × 2 = 33.3 ms) on EVERY machine — the laptop was ALSO at 30 Hz. Fix: self.sim.dt = 0.01. Knock-on: the IMU delay "1..3 steps = 20..60 ms" was silently 33..100 ms. This bug was in ALL SIX env cfgs (fixed in cd3e180; two locomotion cfgs also READ step_dt and needed a local variable). Any checkpoint trained before cd3e180 was at 30 Hz — not comparable to 50 Hz.
- Metrics rule: episode length in STEPS is not comparable across control rates — compare survival seconds (ep_len × step_dt) or timeout rate.
- Debugging method: read Policy/mean_std, Loss/learning_rate, Episode_Termination/* from the run's tfevents, and read the EFFECTIVE config from params/env.yaml + params/agent.yaml rather than the source on disk; hydra CLI overrides allow single-variable A/Bs with no file edit.

## Laptop vs desktop convergence (2026-08-26/27, fixed 69dc428)
- NewBike-Balance-Torque-v0, survival-only, 50 Hz, entropy 0.005, no DR: laptop took 7245 iterations to exceed ep_len 500; desktop 166–387 (n=7 across 4 seeds — not seed luck).
- The configs were genuinely identical (diffed exhaustively, incl. 25 PhysX solver params). The environments differed physically, measurable at iteration 0 (untrained policy = pure physics): mean_episode_length 12.35 (laptop) vs 4.40 (desktop); diverged 0.116 vs 0.363; Loss/value 0.695 vs 12.26.
- Cause: the laptop runs an OLDER Isaac Lab (schema differences in env.yaml; friction_combine_mode average vs null) — the version window where velocity_limit WAS applied → laptop bike capped at 61.6 rad/s, desktop bike UNCAPPED (2420 rad/s) → two physically different bikes. Confirmed after capping the wheel on the desktop (post-fix iteration-0: ep_len 15.57, diverged 0.000, Loss/value 0.589 — lands on the laptop's side of every row). The desktop's pre-fix 166–387 convergence figures are suspect everywhere.
- Old hypothesis (kept for context, likely downstream of the bug): the laptop's bike is more passively stable → a "coast and fall slowly" local optimum (plateau ~296 steps, fallen 1.000, std 0.06–0.07 for 7000 iters, then escapes at it7245 with std rising to 0.13–0.15 — the signature of entering a new basin). The desktop's harsher dynamics destroy that local optimum so the gradient points at active balancing immediately.

## Torque-task NaN crash (2026-08-27)
- `RuntimeError: normal expects all elements of std >= 0.0` killed 3 of 4 runs at iter ~500–575. The message is misleading: std had NOT drifted negative — the actor weights were already NaN. Chain: unbounded rear wheel (2420 rad/s) → raw value in the obs vector → obs_normalization=False feeds a [32,32] critic → Loss/value 10 → 4001 → nan over ~3 iterations → NaN advantages → NaN actor weights + std_param → next torch.normal raises. Root cause = velocity_limit being a silent no-op.
- The actor and critic are separate networks — a NaN critic poisons the actor one iteration later through the advantages (why the traceback points at the policy).
- How to apply: for any std>=0.0 / NaN-policy crash, read Loss/value in TB BEFORE touching entropy_coef or LR. Exploding value loss = an observation-scale problem; look for an unbounded feature (joint speed under torque control). Diagnostics that settled it: per-checkpoint std_param (std was RISING), Loss/value across runs (one logged literal nan), a 256-env probe printing per-dim max |obs|. The existing diverged termination missed it (checked only ROOT velocities, which stayed finite). Mean reward looked fine (33) right up to the crash.
- Blast radius: the wheel fix moved the desktop's iteration-0 physics onto the laptop's side — this bug is the leading suspect for the whole laptop-vs-desktop gap. Pre-2026-08-27 desktop torque-task results were on a bike whose wheel could spin to 2420 rad/s — re-measure, don't trust.

## DR regime verdict (2026-08-28 → 09-07)
- Early verdict: the AUTHORED full-DR ranges (mass 0.5–1.5×, tire friction 0.5–1.2, IMU bias/latency) are beyond what the plant learns: from-scratch DR sweep (d=2/5/10/12/20, 15k) — all solved the easy regime by ~iter 500 then the ramp collapsed every one (final d5/d12 tie ~365, fallen 0.86–0.92; d20 dead). Warm-start (perfect no_dr 1020 into the ramp) degraded immediately (fallen 0.71 at alpha=0.03! — the no_dr optimum has ZERO margin, std 0.0004), partially recovered, then collapsed permanently at full DR (WORSE than from-scratch). From-scratch-in-DR builds a (weak) robust basin; transplanting a no_dr specialist destroys it.
- Same verdict on position/velocity control (NewBike-Balance-v0): all solved early, all collapsed at full DR (d1=127 steps, others 20–24; arp 0.1 mild help, 0.5 fatal; torque control survives DR better — ~365 vs 127 steps).
- Capacity A/B: big net [512,256,128,64] 23× WORSE (9 steps, std exploded to 5.3) vs small [32,32] (212 steps). Capacity is not the bottleneck — don't upsize to fix DR.
- REFINED (2026-09-06, per-term ablation from a common baseline, true in-distribution evals): friction 0.5–1.2 / mass 0.5–1.5× = ACCELERATORS (perfect 1500/100% by ~15k, ~4× faster than no-DR — dynamics diversity prunes degenerate strategies); roll-pose/roll-vel mildly useful; IMU slows but rescuable with 4× budget (1130/72.6% at 60k vs 1500/100% noise-off); COM lateral (y) is the poison, x/z safe-to-beneficial.
- Per-axis COM isolation (±0.02, 60k): x fine (1485–1496/97–98%, raw peak at iter 1600 = accelerator, roll RMS 0.22 tightest of the campaign); y reproduces the failure exactly (571–621/28–31%, yaw+drift deaths ~55–62% — static lean bias the policy can't cancel → compensating steer → yaw/drift accumulation); z the best result ever (1500/100%, roll 0.45–0.62, zero deaths).
- COM-y tolerance cliff: ±0.005 → 1500/100%; ±0.01 → ~78–81%; ±0.02 → 28–31%; largest tolerable sits between 0.005 and 0.01.
- IMU-term decomposition: gyro bias = the KILLER (constant roll-RATE offset → corrective drive → wheel_net runaway, 95.9% wheel_net deaths, 0.6% timeouts); tilt bias nearly free (1500/100%); latency alone ~62–65% timeouts.
- How to apply: do NOT blanket-shrink DR ranges — ablate per term (keep friction/mass, keep roll terms, give IMU ~4× budget, drop/shrink COM). The eval env must match the trained DR regime — the dr_ramp '''-wrap trick is non-idempotent (wrapping an already-wrapped dr_ramp re-activates it) and produced several wrong-env evals; verify active events by comment-depth parsing, never by grepping for `dr_ramp = CurriculumTermCfg(`.

## IMU error model summary
- Deterministic/calibratable: turn-on bias, scale-factor (±0.5% typ), nonlinearity, misalignment, g-sensitivity, vibration rectification (NOT boot-calibratable), quantization. Stochastic: white noise (ARW/VRW — datasheet 0.07°/s RMS gyro = 0.0012 rad/s, 0.001 g RMS accel; these fix the WHITE-NOISE term, NOT the bias — noise ≠ bias: white AC per sample vs DC per-episode constant), bias instability (1/f wander — measurable via stationary 1–3 h log → allantools.adev → curve minimum), rate/accel random walk. Environmental: thermal drift + power-on transient.
- Sim mapping: gyro_res_bias_std = #1 residual (original 0.03 rad/s = 1.72°/s uncalibrated scale, ~300–1000× above the post-cal residual ≈ noise/√N ≈ 1e-4..1e-5 rad/s); gaussian obs noise = #8 (was DEAD in every pre-2026-09-08 run; the old authored 0.01 uniform was 8–10× too big); #9 negligible at 30 s episodes.
- Implemented 2026-09-08: enable_corruption=True + per-channel tuple-std noise (0.001 gravity / 0.0012 angvel / 0 elsewhere); event renamed to reset_imu_turnon_residuals; obs 13→9 dims (later restored to 13 by the 2×2 verdict). Remaining: measure per-boot residual spread; Allan bench test; gyro-bias magnitude sweep to find the tolerance cliff below 0.03.
- CLOSED 2026-09-09: #8 at datasheet magnitudes is NOT the killer — the 9-dim obs trim was. 2×2: 13-dim off → 1401/70.5%; 9-dim off → TB 911; 9-dim on → 457/0%; 13-dim on → 1500/100% (0950 model_500, roll RMS 0.38°, PERFECT). Architecture settled: 13-dim + always-on #8 noise. Committed as baseline 292390a.

## Friction combine rules (2026-09-13/16)
- PhysX combines two materials' friction with the default AVERAGE mode; MuJoCo combines pair friction with MAX (verified empirically: 1.0×0.5→1.0). To make MuJoCo land on a target effective pair, put that value on BOTH geoms.
- MuJoCo's friction triple is (sliding, torsional, rolling) — NOT longitudinal/lateral; PhysX has only sliding (static/dynamic). A faithful MuJoCo port sets torsional/rolling to 0.
- NewBike-Balance-v1 reference: robot 0.5 × pinned ground 1.0 → effective pair 0.75 (tire DR 0.5–1.2 per reset). Trackstand pair: explicit 0.5/0.5/0 on the collision overs × pinned 1.0 ground → 0.75 both engines.

## Collision pipeline (2026-09-13/15)
- Isaac Lab runtime colliders come from the USD's collision meshes, cooked per the physics:approximation token (URDF converter authors "convexHull"). Tokens: boundingCube | boundingSphere | convexDecomposition | convexHull | none | meshSimplification | sdf. PhysX has NO ellipsoid/cylinder primitive collider.
- MuJoCo: the <geom> in the XML IS the runtime collider; mesh geoms with contype 0 are visual-only.
- URDF-import layout: each link = two instanced Xforms (<link> visual, <link>_1 collision with purpose="guide"); the collision mesh child is inside an instanced prototype — invisible to plain stage traversal; the guide-purposed colliders are what "collision from visuals" deletes.
- THE VERIFIED PHYSX SDF RECIPE: approximation="sdf" ALONE is rejected (falls back to convexHull); the SDF branch is gated on applying PhysxSDFMeshCollisionAPI (token + API together). The API carries sdfResolution 256, sdfSubgridResolution 6, sdfNarrowBandThickness 0.01, sdfMargin 0.01 (spacing ≈ AABB/256 ≈ 0.55 mm on a wheel).
- Patch mechanics: USD forbids authoring through instance proxies → edit the payload-layer spec directly via Sdf (apply_wheel_sdf.py).
- Defaults: an un-marked USD collision mesh is cooked as convexHull; none = raw trimesh.

## Trackstand task structure (persistent)
- Package: source/trackstand_rl/ mirrors source/CycleRL EXACTLY, zero CycleRL imports; registered as Trackstand-Balance-v0 via one aggregation import in cyclerl/__init__.py. 5 deltas vs NewBike-Balance-v1: SDF/cylinder USD, drive joint rear_wheel_joint, solver 4/2 (A/B knob; enable_external_forces_every_iteration ON), explicit friction authoring, names/log folder. The 2 != 3 actuator warning is expected (front wheel free).
- Lesson (partial mdp copy): mdp/events.py's module-level __getattr__ is probed with "__path__" by the import machinery; without mdp/dynamic/ that probe raises ModuleNotFoundError instead of a catchable AttributeError — the mdp copy must be complete.
- Steering spawn-angle knob: init_state.joint_pos regex dict in trackstand/bicycle_cfg.py — add "steering_joint": <angle> AFTER ".*": 0.0; reset_scene_to_default restores it every episode.
- Full record (historical): source/trackstand_rl/.../trackstand/cfg_record.txt and logs/rsl_rl/cfg_record.txt.

# How to append (future convention)
New entries go at the end of Part I (Trackstand) or Part II (NewBike) as dated `## N.` bullets, matching the existing style; cross-cutting lessons go into Part III. Keep every numeric result and verdict; condense prose only.
